Summary

Under the rule of King Alexander Luthor, the kingdom of Krypton falls into chaos when a rampant infection causes the dead to rise, spreading like wildfire to the far corners of the land. In the madness, an unexpected liaison is formed between the royal princess and a lowly cook, and together, they rebel against the new order of this world, uniting the fractured land and bringing order to their people.

Or

The Medieval Zombie AU

    The Siege

    Chapter Notes

    HOOOO BOY HERE WE GO. This story has been so fun to write so far, and I cannot wait to share the rest of it with you all.

    Conspiratorial whispers snake through the din of boisterous exclamations and laughter like spies in a strange land. The smell of pungent, sour mead permeates the air as it sloshes over the edges of gesticulating tankards to soak into the wood of floor and table alike.

    In the darkest corner of the tavern, a group of people hunch over their small table, clutching their pints near as they strain to listen.

    "I heard," says a gruff voice, low against the din, "that the village of Wessel met the axe not three fortnights past."

    At his revelation, those gathered round gasp, gritting their teeth in outrage and disbelief. 

    "The entire village? By whose order?" Comes the incredulous response.

    The man who spoke first takes a long draft of his ale before glancing from face to angry face with a meaningful look in his eye.

    "Every last man woman and child… By order of the King, of course. Who else?"

    Barely contained exclamations of outrage ripple throughout the small group, and several whispered questions overlap each other in a hum of confusion. 

    "Were they…?"

    The man shakes his head mournfully. "An outbreak of fever, nothing more."

    -

    The sweet and savory aromas of freshly cooked fruits, meats, and vegetables permeate the air from their arrayment upon ornate dishes and silver platters. At one end of the long table, a golden spiced fish stares sadly at Lena with an almost accusatory look in its eye as she picks at it. 

    Despite the richness of the spread, meals have always been somewhat of a chore for Lena, for the simple fact of company.

    When Lionel had been the ruling Luthor, meals taken together were silent, stoic and utilitarian affairs. Then Lex had assumed the throne, and meals became a more social event, full of the clever, petty jabs he was so fond of, or the seemingly endless brigade of complaints voiced by Lillian. 

    It had been an odd, gradual shift, and through it Lena found she preferred the silence for it had once offered her time to think in peace. Now, she maintains a guarded presence, offering her own jabs on occasion in a half-hearted attempt to hold her own. 

    Some days she does derive a cruel sort of pleasure in a particularly well-placed rebuttal, for it brings with it a backhanded sense of satisfaction. 

    Regardless, she never could truly enjoy starting her day with subtle conflict.

    Her favorite mornings are marked by Lex's absence, be it for business or pleasure. Those days, Lillian prefers to take her meals in her chambers, citing Lena's company as "dull and unengaging," and while the barb of that comment stings, it brings with it the relief of solitude. 

    "You," Lex flicks his wrist in the general direction of a servant, his eyes fixed on his plate. "Bring me the head cook."

    At his words, a sour taste fills Lena's mouth. Every so often Lex seems to wake up with a thorn in his side; those days, firing, imprisoning, or disposing of those who cross him becomes a distinct danger to staff and royalty alike, and regardless of how small the offense, his retribution is always disproportionate.  

    "As I was saying," Lillian sniffs, "I see no reason as to why Redhaven should act so affronted, when all must abide by simple protocol." With an air of exaggeration, she sighs deeply. "I suppose we cannot fault peasants for being simple-minded, but honestly, after the neighboring village exhibited signs of the illness, you would think they would be more understanding."

    "Quite right, Lady," Lex nods absently, and at his lack of engagement, Lillian glances over to Lena with a frown that says, don't you agree?

    Offering the old queen an appeasing smile, Lena thinks to herself, no, not at all, and satisfied, Lillian turns back to Lex, another comment fresh on the tip of her tongue. Before another word exits her mouth, a door half-way down the great hall opens, and through it, a tall woman strides towards them.

    Her wheat colored hair is pulled back into a tight knot behind her head, and the sleeves of her shirt are rolled up over muscled forearms. A grease-stained apron hangs down just below her knees, obscuring a set of trousers woven from an abrasive looking material.

    Both the energy and clothes this woman wears are equally intriguing, and had Lena not been well versed in the art of concealment, she is sure she would be staring at the woman in a manner most would consider rude.

    How odd, she thinks, that this woman forgoes the standard frock. I suppose Lex has always had an appreciation for oddities, though.

    As if to validate the thought, Lex appraises the woman with a spark of interest in his eye when she bows at the waist in the men's fashion. If Lex is surprised, he does not show it.

    "Your Majesty," the woman says, her voice sure and steady.

    "What is your name?" He asks, though Lena knows he won't remember after today on principle.

    "Kara Danvers, sir."

    Curiously, Lena watches as Lex raises a single eyebrow, and the corners of Lillian's mouth pinch with distaste. 

    "Miss Danvers, do you enjoy your work here?"

    "I do, sir."

    "Then why, pray tell, do you seek to insult my table?"

    It's nearly imperceptible, the way Kara's eyes widen, and for a moment Lena finds herself holding her breath.

    "Forgive my ignorance sir, I seek never to insult, and ever to provide a wide range of experiences befitting one so... Well-seasoned as yourself. Is the meal not to your tastes?"

    With the expression of a petulant child, Lex pokes at the fish on his plate. "Is burnt fish ever to any man's tastes?" 

    Kara's face remains carefully neutral as her eyes flick down to the perfectly golden fish in question.

    "Majesty, forgive me once more, for I beg your pardon. I had not realized you prefer your fish raw- I shall personally prepare another to your preferences."

    The sentiment is delivered with startling sincerity, and Lena finds herself struggling not to smile as Lex narrows his eyes in suspicion.

    "No no, that is quite unnecessary, and I truly have not the time to wait for you to correct this mistake." 

    "I suspect the King is a busy man. Then perhaps a light sauce to pair with the char?"

    At that, Lena does smile, and looking down at her own fish she imagines the way in which Kara must have prepared it. 

    Seeming bored with the surplus of solutions and the distinct lack of groveling, Lex nods absently, turning from the cook in a clear dismissal as though she is not worth another second of his time. 

    "Right away, sir." Kara says, bowing once more before turning away, and Lex leans back in his chair, contenting himself with resuming their earlier train of conversation.

    "You will be pleased to know that the example made of Wessel has in fact touched the citizens of Redhaven. I received word from the ruling Duke affirming both his loyalty, and his realization of fault- he seems now to understand the danger he has put every man woman and child of this country in by not ensuring his sick are dealt with appropriately."

    As he speaks, Lena watches curiously as the shoulders of the cook stiffen slightly, her head turning to regard the king plainly- an affront that would have cost her dearly had he been paying attention. 

    She meets Lena's eyes then, and the bright spark of anger within those blue eyes is enough to take Lena's breath away. It flashes through her, hot and intense, only to disappear so quickly she wonders if she had truly seen it.

    Later that night, she will replay the event in her mind's eye, wondering arbitrarily what it meant.

    -

    Two hours past midnight, Kara is roused from sleep by a loud knock on her door. As the head cook she is allotted certain privileges, one of them being a tiny private room wherein the only articles of furniture are a cot, a small washing basin, and a short table. 

    Another is being woken in the late hours, quite often, Lillian wakes in the middle of the night feeling peckish.

    So it is that Kara finds herself alone in the dark, cavernous kitchen, a gleaming cleaver flashing in her hand as she cuts into a prime hunk of raw meat with practiced precision. She hums softly to herself as she appraises the marbled pattern of flesh, when a strong feeling of unease washes through her.

    The hairs on the back of her neck rise, and with the sensation of cold water running down her back, she turns to look behind her.

    At first, she can see nothing but the darkness. 

    There is a slight shifting in the shadows, and as her eyes adjust, the looming silhouette of a tall man resolves before her eyes. 

    "Woah!" Kara yells, her voice echoing about them as she whirls to face him, squinting hard in a futile attempt to make out his features. The man just sways slightly, his head turning slowly towards her, and the unease within her intensifies when he finally seems to see her.

    "What-" is the only word that leaves her mouth before he charges.

    When he enters into the light cast by the fire of the large brick oven, the sight is enough to shock anyone. The man's entire front is drenched in blood, the source of which seems to originate from a gaping wound in the side of his neck. His eyes are pale and seem to stare both at her and through her in the dim light, and with a wet gurgling sound, he runs straight for her, his gait like that of a drunk.

    Pure adrenaline shoots through Kara and she reacts on instinct, jerking backwards with a skip before she jumps fully onto the counter with a yelp. Her boots slide on the slab of meat, and a distant twinge of regret shoots through her at the spoiling of such a fine cut.

    Before she can do more than register the thought, the man lunges towards her, his arms outstretched, and without thinking she lashes out with the cleaver, severing one of his hands. It falls between her feet with a muted slap, leaving the stump of the man's wrist to spray a stream of blood directly into her face, staining her cheeks, her clothes, and the meat below.

    Unfathomably the man remains completely unphased. 

    Like a terrified deer Kara leaps over his head, grunting in pain as her legs absorb the shock of the landing, and, spinning quickly about, she brings the cleaver down upon his head, splitting his skull with a sickening crunch. 

    Almost immediately he drops to the floor, looking for all the world like a boneless ragdoll, and leaving Kara in the stillness. She stands over him, wide-eyed and panting with fear, and for several long, breathless moments, she waits, limbs shaking as she watches for any sign of movement.

    The body at her feet remains blessedly, terribly still, and, cleaver raised, Kara inches forward, her eyes glued to the wound on his neck. Even before the loss of his hand the man looked as though he had spilled enough blood to leave any man prostrate, yet bafflingly, he had remained on his feet.

    With the toe of her boot Kara turns his head to reveal the wound, and it almost looks like…

    Everything clicks into place, and when she turns her face away, she cannot hold her stomach any longer, folding over to vomit upon the floor. She chokes on it, and when finally it ends, she spits the last of it from her tongue with a feeling of disgust, wiping at her mouth with the back of her sleeve.

    The infection has breached Argo's walls.

    Alex had warned her many weeks prior- had told Kara that the only way to contain the infection was to execute all those infected, lest it spread rapidly and violently across the land. At the time, Kara had thought the measures harsh- had not understood just how terrifying the task of surviving one of the infected might be.

    Eyes wide and searching, she whips her head around the kitchen, scanning for danger. The sounds of distant screams and shouts now echo through the distant corridors, and with a thrill of fear, she begins to understand exactly how much danger she finds herself in.

    The urge to hyperventilate- to scream and scrub the still-warm blood from her face and clothes is nearly unbearable,  and yet she knows too that time is a resource to whom she plays the beggar.

    Her heart feels like a heavy stone in her stomach, and she forces herself to suck in deep, calming breaths, ignoring the metallic tang of iron that accompanies every inhale. 

    Forcing herself to remain observant, she takes stock of the room, running through every piece of usable material she can see, if not remember.

    Quickly she runs to the bucket of water in the corner, splashing her face and scrubbing the blood from her hands before making her way to the storage room. There, she heaves two large sacks of potatoes, upending them with frantic urgency, sending the spuds rolling in all directions. One bag she cuts into strips that she ties to the second bag, fashioning a makeshift pack.

    With trembling hands she throws into the bag several light rations of various jerkys and cheeses; five candles; a set of spark rocks; a box of matches; a small whetstone, and a little pot, before securing the entire thing to her back.

    Hurriedly she tests her mobility, jumping up and down, spinning and flapping her arms and fussing with the ties that span her chest, stomach and shoulders, until she deems the pack satisfactory.

    With one last shrug, Kara turns to the wall whereon the knives hang, and with a grimace she takes three from the line- one large chef's knife and two smaller paring knives. Gingerly, she tucks one of the small knives behind her belt and the other into the side of her boot before taking the chef's knife into her left hand.

    With an almost dizzying sensation of dread Kara takes up the red and shining cleaver in her right and, squaring her shoulders, she faces the darkness.

    -

    A scream pierces the silence and with a start, Lena sits bolt-upright, chest heaving. The quickness of her heart seems preternaturally loud in her ears, and for a moment, she begins to wonder if she had simply dreamed the sound.

    Just as she is about to lie back, a loud crash sounds far down the ornate hall outside her room, speeding the thundering of her heart with anxious fervor. Eyes wide, she slips warily out of bed, hands fumbling for the matches and candlestick that live on her bedside table.

    The light provides a small modicum of comfort, and on bare feet she pads towards the door, hesitating for just a moment before pulling on the handle. It glides open on oiled hinges, and with wide eyes she peers down the dimly lit hall, searching the darkness for any hints of disruption.

    Loud running footsteps sound from farther down the grand corridor, growing even louder as they near, and moments later a large burly guard materializes from the shadows, barreling towards her with a grim look upon his face.

    "Your highness!" He roars at her even as he nears. "Stay in your chambers, it isn't safe out here!"

    It takes Lena longer than she would admit to register his words, and with an impatient huff he pushes her back inside her chambers with a roughness that sends her sprawling on her back. 

    Normally such an action would have caused irrevocable damage. Now, it only heightens the sense of alarm coursing through her body.

    Before he can slam the door shut, she hears the thudding of several feet from down the hallway, accompanied by an animalistic, guttural sound that sends chills down her spine. 

    With a dawning sense of horror, Lena watches as two figures crash into the guard, knocking him to the floor. She scrambles to her feet even as the guard screams like a pig mid-slaughter, and the sounds of tearing flesh, yelling, and muted blows seem suddenly detatched from the bloody scene that plays out before her. 

    The guard thrashes desperately, his massive fists striking blow after blow against his two assailants, one dressed in the colors of the Luthor household, and the other in simple servant's garb. Most of his strikes would have incapacitated any smaller man, but for some horrible, inexplicable reason, they prove woefully ineffective as almost mockingly they tear at his arms and throat with their teeth.

    A virtual fountain of blood that flows across the floor in thick rivulets, and as he kicks and thrashes, Lena lunges for the door, pushing it shut as quickly as she can.

    It stops nearly four inches from the frame, and with growing panic she looks down to see one of the guard's arms wedged into the space between. 

    The adrenaline-fueled side of her brain continues to shove desperately at the door before the rational side yells at her to kick his arm out of the way, but as her foot lifts, a bloodied arm reaches through the gap, swinging towards her face. 

    With a terrified scream Lena lurches back, only to realize her mistake a moment later.

    The door swings inwards to reveal one of the attackers, bent low over the still guard. With great yanks of his head he strips the arm of its flesh, even as the sap of the man's body pools thick and glossy about them.

    Their other assailant- the servant, lurches through the door, her posture slightly hunched as though she has forgotten how to hold her body. She stumbles into Lena's quarters, and for a handful of breathless seconds she seems almost disoriented, but when her pale eyes catch on the light of Lena's candle her face splits into the memory of a smile.

    And then she charges.

    Lena cannot help it. She screams again, the candlestick raised in her hand, and throwing her arm out clumsily, she attempts to slam the burning end of the candle into the woman's face. It misses horribly, but the wick catches on an errant lock of the servant's hair, igniting it like aged tinder.

    The flame climbs quickly, engulfing the woman's head so that she resembles a human torch, and still she stumbles forward, arms outstretched towards Lena. 

    Wild with fear, Lena raises her now- extinguished candlestick, brandishing the heavy bronze before her as though it could offer any true semblance of protection in her hands, and with surprising strength the woman runs into the metal.

    The nauseating smell of blood and burnt hair is thick in Lena's nose as the woman bites at her, jaws snapping shut inches from her face. Her teeth and tongue are stained orange and red with recent gore, and it is all Lena can do to hold her arms out against the strength of the woman as the flames scorch her cheek.

    Her arms tremble with the effort of holding the woman back, threatening to give out, and her breath comes in harsh uneven pants. She turns her face away as the woman pushes ever nearer, the click of her teeth loud in Lena's ears, and she is sure she is lost-

    A spray of blood erupts from the woman's face, dousing her flaming head with a gentle hisss before splattering against Lena's cheek. Her eyes and mouth squeeze shut against the onslaught, and her arms are suddenly pushing freely against the air. At the jarring loss of resistance she lurches forwards, stumbling over the body of the woman as it lays motionless beneath her feet. 

    Gasping with the shock of it, Lena looks up only to see a woman dressed entirely in red, and for a moment, she feels a thrill of fear. 

    It takes her several seconds to realize that the woman is not, in fact, clad in red, but drenched in blood- blood that drips from the stray ends of her hair, her clothes, and the edges of her face. Her fists are wrapped around the handles of two huge knives, and she looks absolutely feral, but when Lena looks into her eyes, they are a startlingly clear blue, alight with fire and a fear that mirrors her own. 

    The woman's mouth is pressed into a hard line, and without a word she holds a finger up to her lips before darting to the door, easing it carefully shut. As soon as the latch clicks into place her head whips around, tiny droplets flinging outwards with the force of it, and she lunges for the chair at Lena's writing desk, yanking it over to brace beneath the door's handle.

    She turns to Lena then, and in a shaky voice, says: "Your highness, we must leave the castle, right now. It has been overrun."

    Lena's mouth falls open. 

    "That's impossible…" is all she can whisper, but the woman shakes her head vigorously, a flash of impatience crossing her expression.

    And Lena recognizes her then- remembers the anger that had sparked in those eyes not twenty-four hours ago. 

    "Kara?" She asks, squinting past the blood. 

    Kara nods, stepping close, and in a low voice she growls, "The dead have breached your walls. If you value your life, we must leave, now. Grab anything of use- money, clothes you can travel in, good shoes you can walk long distances in, and anything you can use as a weapon." 

    Clinging to the direction, Lena nods, turning numbly as Kara tears at her bedding, throwing pillows and thick downy blankets to the floor with disregard.

    Quickly, almost frantically Lena sheds her now red gown, grabbing for her travel clothes. They seem wildly impractical and cumbersome, the skirts swirling obnoxiously about her ankles, but they are a better alternative to the rest of her glittering wardrobe. 

    When she exits into the main room with a bundle of clothes in her arms, she hurries over to her bookshelf selecting three books before continuing on to a set of drawers. From it she pulls a small bag of coins and a slim dagger, which she quickly straps to the braided belt at her waist. 

    When she holds up her gathered items, Kara eyes her choices with a look of exasperation.

    "Books?" She asks in a voice ripe with incredulity.

    Unwilling to be cowed, Lena lifts her chin, allowing a defiant gleam to enter her gaze. "And clothes."

    Rolling her eyes, Kara gestures at the thin sheet laid out along the bed, and when Lena drops her armfull onto the sheet, Kara wraps it into a tight bundle before thrusting it into Lena's arms.

    Kara looks Lena up and down then in a blatant appraisal that she would have found uncomfortable had she not been raised before the eyes of an entire kingdom, and with an odd expression, Kara turns away again.

    She rummages through a few drawers, before returning with a long string, and wordlessly she circles Lena, gathering her bloodsoaked hair in equally bloody hands to tie up into a tight knot. 

    "Best not to give them anything they can grab you by," she offers quickly, before tying her own up and out of the way.

    "Are you ready?"

    "For what?" She asks, as if she did not already know.

    "To face the dark."

    Though all Lena wants to do is return to bed and wake from this nightmare, she nods with a hazy sense of unreality.

    "Whatever you do," Kara says over her shoulder, "stay right behind me."

    "Wait!" Lena hisses, and the woman turns to regard her with that same look of fear and impatience. 

    "There lies a hidden panel through the far wall that opens to the servants corridors."

    When Kara's eyes dart towards the indicated wall, Lena can almost feel the storm of thoughts and evaluations racing through her head, until, with a decisive nod, Kara turns, hastening across the room. 

    Lena hurries to catch up, reaching past Kara's shoulder to hook her fingers into the seam of the door, and when she pulls it swings open to reveal a dark and narrow hall. 

    "This passage leads past the East wing towards my maid's quarters," she whispers, her voice swallowed up by the darkness.

    Together they step into the wall, swinging shut the door behind them before shuffling onwards, the sounds of their footsteps loud in the confined space. A musty smell mingles with the oppressive stench of blood, and Lena has to force down her bile as she hurries on behind Kara.

    They pause at an intersection in the hall, and with panicked desperation Lena racks her brain as she struggles to recall the simple layout of the servant's corridors. 

    "Right," she whispers finally, and with a wordless nod Kara turns to the right. The sounds of screams and agonized yells can be heard through the wall, yielding only to silence, and as Lena listens, her entire body trembles.

    A great struggle begins as she begs her stubborn feet to move, one after the other as she watches the shifting of red fabric across Kara's back. For three endless minutes they travel down the pitch dark hall until they reach an innocuous door, indented in the wall.

    "Here," Lena whispers, her voice harsh in the quiet. 

    A pause, in which they hear nothing.

    Fist clenching and reclenching about her ruby blades, Kara grasps the door's handle, turning it with a too-loud click. They wince, listening for a moment longer before allowing it to swing open, the hinges creaking horribly. 

    Just as they shuffle into the room, a huge figure slams into Kara, sending her flying with a curse into what sounds like a very sturdy piece of wooden furniture. The sounds of metal clattering to the floor ring out in the dark, and without thinking Lena drops her sack of belongings, yanking the knife from her belt.

    With more bravery than she has ever mustered in her life, she springs forward, teeth clenched in a strained snarl as she slams the dagger into the ear of Kara's assailant.

    The blood that trickles from his head drips down the sleeve of her dress, and when she yanks the blade away, his body goes slack against Kara. 

    Kara who falls back, panting as she gathers her wits, and with a grunt of effort she rolls the heavy person off her chest, rasping a shaky "Thank you," to Lena. 

    Bending down, Kara feels about in the dark before she rises once more, knives clutched firmly within her hands. Determined not to wasting another moment, Lena turns back and scoops up her sack in one arm, still clutching the dripping dagger in her other hand.

    "Through this door lies another hall. We must follow it to a split, whereupon we ought to turn left. The third door shall take us to a room that leads to the royal gardens."

    Kara nods once more, holding one of her fists up with three fingers extended. One finger drops, and then the other, and they are sprinting down the hall, whipping left at the split until they reach the third door.

    The room they pass through is small, presenting only one other door which they virtually burst through in their desperation. Lena's first breath of fresh air is an utter relief, and though the scent of blood and fear still masks the aroma of roses, and feels like she can finally breathe again; feels as though they might actually be okay. 

    Above, the moon bleaches the extensive gardens of their color, washing them in silver, and as they stumble onto the gravel path, Lena points towards a break in the crenelated wall, above which a large tower looms.

    "There- we must pass through that tower. The outer door is always guarded, perhaps we might warn them."

    Immediately Kara takes off in the direction of the tower, and the small stones crunching loudly beneath Lena's boots as she follows. Not a minute later they find themselves standing in the shadow of the tower, gazing up into its dark windows. When Kara knocks there is no answer, so tentatively she pulls at the heavy oak door. 

    It swings outwards without resistance. 

    No guards are to be found, and before Lena can worry about why that might be, Kara tosses her knives onto the small table in the center of the room, turning to lift the massive beam of wood that bars the exit.

    She tosses it aside like it weighs nothing, and, scooping up her knives once more she pushes the door open to reveal the long grassy expanse of the hill upon which the castle stands. 

    The sight that greets them fills Lena's stomach with lead.

    Surrounding the castle sprawls the extensive city of Argo. On clear days when the sun shines brightly, the great marble walls and the white stone buildings gleam with an almost ethereal radiance to them. Now, great pillars of smoke rise from its depths as the glow of several fires bathe the city in a terrible bloody light.

    A tear rolls down Kara's cheek, and for several long minutes, all Lena can do is stare at the city with a sense of hopelessness.

    Kara is the first to move, leaving Lena to stand in the doorway feeling nothing but stuck, and when she realizes that Lena is no longer following, she stops short.

    "What are you doing?" She asks, not unkindly, and for a moment, Lena isn't sure. 

    "I…"

    "We must go, highness, or be trapped in the city."

    "I cannot." Lena says simply.

    "What do you mean you cannot?" Kara's voice remains gentle, but Lena can hear a note of hysteria that has crept into it.

    "There are people back there who need help- people I am responsible for. I can't just leave them." Lena whispers furiously, glaring at Kara. Kara, who stares back at her as though she has just lost her mind, and for a moment, she thinks she might have.

    Three long strides brings Kara inches from Lena's face. Her blue eyes flash in the darkness, and Lena has to swallow the urge to shrink beneath the weight of her stare.

    "We. Must. Go. The castle is lost, and the city may very well be too.There exists no true doubt in my mind that if we turn back now, we will die."

    "I would rather die in service to my people than live to be an old coward," Lena says with far more conviction than she feels, her chin raised in defiance. 

    At her words, Kara goes deadly still, and for a moment, Lena thinks she may have made a mistake. 

    "We barely escaped with our lives," Kara says in a voice infinitely softer than Lena expected. "Do not be so quick to throw yours away now. If we return to the castle there is naught we can do but die with them. If we can make it through the city before it is completely overrun, we may yet have a chance to warn the surrounding villages and the towns of this outbreak. They are your people too. We may even find those in need of help, or those who might help us."

    For a moment Lena just stands there, shaking with impotent rage and a creeping sense of shame. And she knows Kara is right- knows she is not thinking clearly.

    When Lena's eyes fall to the ground, Kara steps away, leaving her to follow glumly behind, every footfall adding to the growing pit of guilt in her stomach.

    "Where are we going?" Lena asks.

    "To my sister's house."

    Chapter End Notes

    Always Close the Door Behind You

    Chapter Summary

    Two women, against the world, naked and bloody, and would you like at that? There's only one bed.

    Chapter Notes

    By the time they reach the base of the hill, the blood that cakes their faces and hair has nearly dried, cracking and flaking with every move they make. Distant shrieks echo throughout the city, and when they reach the initial line of houses that surrounds the hill, Kara slows.

    Though the face Kara shows Lena is brave, her eyes betray her. Gazing at the smoke that billows overhead, Kara gestures for the sack of clothes and books, and when Lena hands it to her, she lays it out on the ground, cutting six clean swathes of fabric from it. One she ties over her nose and her mouth, and the other two she wraps around the soles of her boots, offering the last three to Lena.

    With shaking hands Lena copies Kara, tying the cloth to her face. It traps the heat of her breath, hot and moist against the soft material, and she unsheathes the dagger from her belt, trembling with the adrenaline of what they are about to do.

    Though she is more afraid than she has ever been in her life, she draws strength from the steadiness of Kara. 

    Kara, who squares her shoulders, readjusts her grip on her weapons, and faces the shadows, stepping into them after only a moment's hesitation.

    They make their way from shadow to shadow, avoiding any street that veers too near the bright effigies of buildings. Darting from the cover of one shadow to the next with muffled steps, Lena struggles not to choke on the smoke even through the rag, willing herself to remain silent even as her lungs and throat burn with every breath.

    She has never spent much time in the city, but she remembers the bustling markets and the laughter of children as they ran along the cobbled paths. Now, the streets have taken on an unfamiliar hostility, rife uncertainty. She is just about to follow Kara through a dark alley when she realizes that Kara has stopped dead at the corner, the rigid line of her body trembling slightly. 

    She puts a hand out behind her, her broad palm landing on Lena's stomach, and she pushes until Lena's back is pressed against the wall. 

    And then she looks at Lena with a nearly inscrutable sadness in her eyes, mouthing 'Stay here.' 

    She readjusts her grip on her knives, and with a deep breath she darts around the corner. 

    Moments later there is a wet crunch followed by a muffled thud, and when Kara returns, the cleaver in her hand glints with fresh wetness. Eyes downcast, Kara motions for Lena to follow her, tucking Lena into her arm in an attempt to obscure her vision. 

    She sees anyways. 

    The small body of a young boy lies on the cobble, a pool of red growing steadily beneath his head, and appalled, Lena stops short. Gently Kara pulls her onwards, and she thinks she leaves a piece of her soul behind when they step past him. 

    They navigate the streets like silent specters, diverting their path on occasion when a peek around the corner reveals dangers they would be foolish to try and face. It is in this manner that they navigate Argo's inner ring without misfortune, and passing through the wall that marks the outer ring, they enter a near labyrinth, wherein the tall houses cram together to create a claustrophobic canyon. 

    Their dark windows stare down menacingly, and all Lena wants to do is to take Kara's hand like a child reaching for her oldest blanket. Around the knive's handles, Kara's knuckles are rigid, and the sight of it almost makes Lena feel safe until she remembers the twisted face of the boy lying in the street. 

    Once more Kara freezes, a tremor running through her frame, and when Lena turns the corner following Kara's line of sight, she sees three large figures hunched over the body of a young woman their age, tearing her apart.

    They remain stubbornly in the path, and when Kara glances at Lena, the worry in her eyes mirrors her own feelings of trepidation. 

    Lena watches helplessly as, without warning, Kara charges the group, throwing the chef's knife with deadly aim. It buries itself in the right eye of one of the men, leaving him to slump over his meal, and without missing a step, Kara brings her cleaver down onto the head of the second man.

    It sticks in his skull, and with a feeling of abject horror, Lena watches as Kara struggles to free it, only for the third and final man to grab at her, pushing her to the ground. Kara's hand disappears for a moment, only to reappear, and with a shout she slams her fist against the side of his head. 

    When he falls, Lena sees the thin handle of a knife protruding from his temple, and she runs to Kara, glancing her over with a worried expression. Kara waves her off, and wearily yanks the knife from the man's head, bracing her foot against the second man's neck as she pulls the cleaver free with a wet squelching sound. 

    Beside Lena, the woman below twitches, sitting slowly up. Lena's first instinct is to crouch down and to help her, but when she does, the woman turns towards her with unfocused eyes, leaning towards Lena with a low gasping sound.

    Lena scrambles back in alarm, and before she realizes what is happening, Kara strides up, swinging the cleaver down into the top of the woman's head with a grimace. When she pulls the knife free, the woman falls on her back with an almost peaceful expression.

    Unable to process what she has just seen, Lena remains sitting, frozen in place as she stares at the woman's face. A sense of unreality blankets the world, and she feels as though the weight of it might crush her.

    And then a red hand enters her line of sight, and as she follows it to its owner, meeting Kara's eyes. They are fierce and filled with sadness, and distantly Lena knows she must go on. She takes the slippery hand, allowing herself to be pulled to her feet, and it's almost gentle the way Kara hands her her sack of belongings and takes her hand.

    They forge ahead, the outer wall seeming just out of reach with every blocked street, and it takes them nearly twenty more minutes of dodging between the houses to find a path to the main gates. They stand tall and heavy, wedged slightly open with just enough room for the two women to squeeze past and out of the city.

    Lena can almost sense the relief radiating from Kara as they stumble out into the countryside and down the main road, and though she feels she could collapse right then and there, Lena matches the quick pace Kara sets.

    They walk for nearly an hour before the silhouette of a small house rises up against the dark horizon, and when she sees it, Kara quickens her pace, almost running towards the house. 

    When she slows, Lena knows instantly that something is wrong, and when she nears, she sees the front door hanging open, beyond which lies an all-obscuring darkness.

    Tensely, almost mechanically Kara steps forward, the muscles of her jaw standing out as she continues on towards the house. Worried, Lena reaches out, catching at Kara's elbow, but when Kara turns to her, there is a nearly frantic look in her eye. 

    It strikes a particular type of fear into Lena's heart, and when she allows her hand to fall from Kara's elbow, she follows behind as Kara melts into the darkness of the house.

    Both floors are clear, and as soon as that fact is made clear, Lena hurriedly shuts the door, bolting it into place. From the dark room behind her there come the sounds of muted cloth and metal scraping against wood, and a soft, quick scratching sound that gives way to a tiny pinprick of light. 

    It illuminates Kara's face from below. making a ghoulish picture of her red-streaked face. She glances around briefly, before reaching for an oil lamp, quickly touching her match to the wick of it.

    A soft light diffuses in the darkness, offering a small, comforting ring of warmth.

    Gingerly Kara picks up the lamp, and for the first time since Lena's seen her, she looks weary in the way that children do. She beckons Lena to follow her, and without another word, she turns towards a narrow, steep set of stairs tucked away in the wall.

    The sparsely furnished room at the top contains a small bed, a simple rectangular wardrobe made of wood, and a plain wooden desk upon which lies a neat stack of parchment and a quill.

    Exhaustion floods Lena, and without another word she sets down the sheet wherein her worldly possessions lie, wipes her dagger clean on the hem of her dress, sheathes it, and turns to Kara.

    Kara, who is stripping down with total abandon, flinging her blood crusted tunic and shirt away from her. It lands in a shadowy corner of the room, and she kicks off her boots and pants in a similar frantic manner. When she is free of her clothes, she closes her eyes, taking a deep and calming breath before sitting on the edge of the bed with her head in her hands.

    It creaks under her weight, and for the first time, Lena feels almost awkward. She is a stranger here, in a strange house, and she can't help but feel as though this is a private moment. 

    And she is covered in blood herself, empathizing with Kara's need to rid herself of the prison of her clothes, and suddenly the impropriety of it could not matter less. Hands shaking, she unties the belt from around her waist, and lifts the dress over her head. She cringes as her hair crunches stiffly with blood, and when she looks up from removing her boots, she finds Kara looking straight at her.

    Lena's breath catches in her throat, and Kara quickly turns her gaze away. Devoid of the cognizance to question what that was, Lena walks over to the bed, sitting on the edge of it next to Kara. 

    "Are you tired?" Lena whispers.

    "More than you could ever know." Is the somber reply, and though she disagrees with that particular sentiment, she nods.

    "Lie back then, brave heart," she says, low and soft, and to that, Kara offers no resistance, instead leaning down to snuff out the light. In the darkness the two of them shuffle into the bed whose builder, Lena is sure, intended it for only one occupant. There is barely room to lie perfectly still side by side, but they manage, staring up at the ceiling together in stunned silence.

    And the stillness and the silence of it is too much, and Lena can scarcely breathe, let alone think, for if she thinks, she will remember, and all she wants to do in that moment is to fall asleep in hopes that this entire saga becomes a distant nightmare. 

    The heat of Kara's body is soothing though, and slowly, slowly the rhythms of her body calm.

    -

    The dawn comes swiftly and mercilessly, carrying with it every hopelessness and grief. Kara wakes to the stillness, tears leaking from the corners of her eyes when she opens them. A terrible numbness seems to blanket the world, and though she half-heartedly wills herself to rise, she lies there for a long time. 

    Time who marches ever on, bidding her to eventually roll out of the bed. She does so as gently as she can to avoid jostling the frame, and as she moves, her gaze catches on Lena's face, still stained with red- on the peaceful set of her features, relaxed for the first time Kara has seen.

    She feels an odd twisting in her heart, and before she can devote too much thought to it, she turns away to crack open the shutters slightly. 

    Alex's wardrobe is almost empty of clothes besides the spare set Kara keeps for the rare nights she comes to visit. As she pulls on the clean trousers, she glances over, and with a jolt, meets Lena's eyes. 

    "Mornin," Kara says in a voice rough and unrefined, but Lena just looks at her with a curious expression. Kara pulls the white linen shirt over her head and shrugs on the blue tunic, relieved to be wearing something clean even if she is still coated in foul blood, and without another word, she turns and descends the stairs, stopping to peek out of the shuttered windows. There doesn't appear to be a soul in sight, and with a wary glance about, she picks up a bucket, opens the door, and makes her way to the well outside.

    It is only a matter of seconds before she is pulling up the bucket, now filled with water, and she walks several paces from the source before stripping off her clothes and dumping the bucket fully over her head. She gasps with the cold and throws the bucket back down into the well again, hurriedly pulling it up, taking down her tangled, crusty hair and pouring the water slowly over her head, yanking the fingers of one hand through her hair.

    The water flows over her skin with a reddish tint, and she scrubs at herself, running her hands through her hair over and over until the water begins to run clear.

    When she finally pulls her clothes back on, she feels infinitely better, turning back to the house, only to stop short in surprise when she sees Lena leaning in the door, wrapped in the blanket with her dagger clutched in her fist. She pointedly watches the horizon, even as Kara approaches.

    Flashing a small grin in her direction, Kara raises a hand, saying, "Thanks for watching out for me. Now come'ere and let's get you rinsed off too. You look frightful enough to scare a dog."

    Lena rolls her eyes, but the relief on her face is plain to see, and she crosses the distance between them, pressing the dagger into Kara's palm with a smirk. 

    "You really ought to tell me where you're going. You might find yourself in trouble." 

    And at the way Lena's voice coils in her ear, Kara feels in trouble all right. 

    More trouble than she could have imagined. Lena lets the blanket drop, and Kara's eyes instantly snap skyward, only for her attention to jerk back to Lena's eyes at her polite cough.

    "Raise the bucket for me?" She asks, a playful note in her voice, and Kara turns so quickly to throw the bucket into the well, it leaves her hands at an awkward angle, smacking loudly against the sides of the well. An echoing crash after glorious crash sounds back at them, and Lena raises her eyebrow at Kara.

    "On second thought, maybe you ought to stand guard."

    Her ears are hot as she listens to the splash of water and the groan of gratitude Lena releases as she washes herself free of the old gore.

    Once finished, they return to the safety of the house once more, bolting the door with a solid thunk.

    Lena excuses herself upstairs, and while she is gone, Kara begins searching through the cabinets of Alex's house. They are frustratingly barren, anything of use already having been removed, leaving only items of limited use.

    Alex was- is a doctor, and Kara had desperately hoped to have her sister, the knowledge she contained, and her supplies. And so it is that she finds she has none of them, and an excess of mounting frustration. She stands before the empty cabinets with her shoulders slumped inwards, her hands in two tight fists as a hot tear rolls down her cheek. And then another, and another, until she finds herself on her knees, clutching herself as she cries silently. 

    She thinks of the maid who fell beneath her cleaver, of the boy, of Alex, and then of every great loss she has endured in her life, and for a moment, she cannot breathe. She cannot bear the tragedies of life, and yet she must. She must, for the only alternative is to perish. 

    She doesn't know how long she's been kneeling there when a soft hand touches her shoulder, and when she glances up, she finds eyes the color of a forest pond. Her mind calms when Lena takes her face between her hands, brushing the tears away. 

    "Hey now, hey," Lena says in a voice so low and soothing it feels like a song. "I am so sorry, brave heart…"

    Kara sniffs, holds her breath, really tries to compose herself, and after a moment of stillness, breaks down again with a quiet, gasping sob. Lena just cradles her head to her chest, rocking her back and forth, smoothing her hand along Kara's hair until she has run out of tears. 

    And when Kara finally stills, she whispers hoarsely, "Alex… She's all I have in the world, and she's gone. She's gone, and she's probably… like all the rest of them by now."

    Lena stills for a moment, then continues rocking, waiting for Kara to go on. When she does not, Lena offers gently, "You don't know that, Kara."

    Kara pushes herself upright, wiping at her nose and eyes. 

    "But the door-"

    "Maybe she escaped," Lena suggests. "I see no signs of struggle or bloodshed. Only an open door, and a nearly depleted wardrobe."

    Kara frowns. Lena has a point. When they had fought their way free of the castle, the floors and walls were slick with blood. This house was, aside from the missing items, unscathed.  

    "But the door…"

    "Maybe she left it open. Maybe someone else came in, searching for supplies."

    The words filter sluggishly through Kara's brain, and she sits for a long quiet moment, considering the possibility. 

    "If you're right, then I need to find her."

    Lena nods as though she had expected Kara to say just that. 

    "Then we will search for her."

    Kara's eyes snap to Lena's, almost expecting her to be smiling, or laughing at some jest unknown to her, but when she looks at Lena, the woman is completely serious.

    "Your highness, I cannot take you on such a dangerous venture. It would be-"

    "Kara, please. Not only for you, but for me as well. I have nobody I can go back to, nor can I navigate this land on my own. My only choice is to go with you, or to lose myself in this land and hope that I find my way.

    I cannot promise to never be a burden to you, but I will try my best to hold my own. Please, let me try."

    And Kara looks at Lena, a princess asking for her help to survive in a foreign land, and a familiar chord strikes within her.

    I have nobody. I cannot go back.

    "Very well."

    A look of pure relief crosses Lena's face and they sit together for a moment, lost to their own thoughts. Kara thinks of Alex- thinks of where she might have gone. Surely not towards the city where the population thickens. 

    Surely she would have traveled away to the farms and forests- away from the masses of death. But regardless of where she might be, what if Alex were to return home?

    "Do you know how to write?"

    Lena looks up at Kara from under raised eyebrows. "Of course I can write."

    Gritting her teeth, Kara humbles herself before she says, "Well, I cannot, your highness, and I need to leave my sister a message in case she returns here. Will you help me?"

    Lena's expression softens, and she nods. "Of course I will, Kara."

    And she has never heard her name spoken so sweetly before. "Thank you," she manages at last.

    It's uncomfortable, relying on someone else for something as sensitive as a letter to a loved one. Especially when that somebody is a Luthor. 

    But, she thinks with mild surprise, Lena doesn't feel like a cruel person. She feels anything but. 

    Whether Lena is cruel or kind, it changes nothing about the fact that if she wants to leave her sister a message, Kara has no other choice than to trust her words to this woman.

    "Alex,"

    She dictates. 

    "The castle has been overrun by the infection. The city… The city of Argo has fallen. It is not safe there, and you must stay away."

    Kara pauses, and for a brief moment considers the wisdom of revealing the identity of her travel companion before deciding against it.

    "I must send word to the village of Orvai and the surrounding farms, warning them of what has occured. 

    I swear to you I will try my hardest to find you, wherever you are in the world.

    Should you receive this letter, know that I love you, and that I hope for nothing more than your safety. 

    May we meet again."

    At the end of her dictation, Kara takes the quill from Lena, scrawling her name in messy letters beneath the neat rows of script, and with a deep sigh, she runs a hand over her face.

    "Thank you, your highness," she sighs out.

    "It's the least I could do after you saved my life."

    And when Kara glances searchingly at Lena, she finds only an unquestionable sincerity in her eyes. 

    -

    As Kara digs around in the small garden outside Alex's house uprooting several roots and tubers, she thinks of the preserved meats and hard cheese wrapped in her pack. She would prefer to save them for a time when they are desperate and short on supplies, and so she fills her little pot with water from the well and lights the cook fire inside Alex's house, throwing her spoils into the boiling water. 

    She almost laughs at the face Lena pulls when confronted by the bland meal, but to her credit, she eats all of hers without complaint.

    As the morning passes, Kara searches around Alex's house, and with a promise to replace it, she breaks apart one of the wooden chairs, and with a sincere apology to her cleaver, begins to split and smooth the wood down.

    Every so often she has to run the whetstone along its edge only to dull it once more upon the wood until several slim but sturdy poles take form. She lashes them together, binding them into a large frame, trisected by crossing poles. 

    Quickly, two suitable pack frames stand before her, and Kara sets to work washing clean the sheet that held Lena's items, and the sack that held her own. Once they are as free of blood and debris as they can be, she stretches them over the frames, tying them down and hanging them from the rafters to dry.

    Lena watches the entire process with interest, seemingly absorbed in every step.

    "How did you learn to do that?" Lena asks as Kara wipes her wet hands dry against her pants.

    "When I was younger, my family went through some hard times. I spent several years living deep in the same forest that borders the kingdom. My father taught me many different handy things to help keep me alive, and this is one of them. That is, with a few of my own modifications." Kara says with a wistful smile.

    And then Lena says something that surprises her. 

    "Will you teach me?"

    Kara grins. "What, how to make packs?" 

    "All of it," Lena says solemnly. "I want to know how to hold my own."

    And Kara regards her seriously then, nodding thoughtfully.

    "All right, your highness. If you really want to learn, then I will teach you." 

    And Lena looks at her with a mixture of excitement and something else- something Kara never can figure out.

     

    Chapter End Notes

    If anyone is curious, this was the prompt from the generator that spurred this whole story:

    Primary character: A prince, who has a strong moral compass.

    Secondary character: A chef, who loves puns.
    (Yes I do feel bad for writing Kara as less punny, but it feels at odds with her character/what she's going through right now.)

    Plot:
    It's a romantic suspense story about empowerment. It kicks off at the pub with a rant about politics.

    And there's a twist! Midway through the plot, all other problems will be foregone in the face of a zombie apocalypse.

    Anyways, please enjoy the fruits of my labor & shoot me a lil message about what you liked if you'd like to add some momentum to this roll.

    Next up: A sword finds its way to our girls.

    Your Highness

    Chapter Summary

    In which Lena exudes the essence of the "it's one banana michael, what could it cost, $10?" meme

    Chapter Notes

    The door to Alex's house groans loudly as it shuts, sounding for all the world like it's mourning the loss of yet another tennant. 

    As they start down the road, Lena can't help the way her glance keeps flicking over to Kara through the corner of her eye, nor the way she can almost feel the pain behind the pointed way Kara sets her jaw and refuses to look back.

    She trails behind Kara, feeling as lost and new as an infant in this vast open landscape, and as the miles pass them by, she finds herself wondering about Kara's life. Already what she understands of Kara has proved fascinating, and she finds herself attempting to weave together the disjointed strands of knowledge into some semblance of a pattern.

    The countryside passes by so gradually that several times when Lena looks around she is surprised to find the scenery has shifted completely in many small and subtle ways. 

    "Where are we going again?" She asks absently, gazing down the seemingly endless plain of fields.

    "To the farms that lie along the path to the village of Orvai," Kara says. "They are the largest group of people nearby, and they exist far enough from Argo that they might yet have escaped this curse. They could be our best hope at surviving this mess, and for searching for any others who might have escaped the city. We must warn them of what is coming, if they are not already aware of it."

    "And then what?" Lena asks, her brow furrowed as she attempts to gaze into the murky future.

    "I don't know," Kara replies, frowning at the ground with equal consternation.

    The question repeats itself over and over in Lena's head.

    And then what, and then what?

    They carry on in a companionable silence for the better part of an hour, and as they continue, Lena falls deeper and deeper into the swirling eddies of her thoughts until she feels sullen with the weight of them. The urge to cry and to scream at the sky eats at her soul, but she holds it back behind gritted teeth and continues on.

    It is in this manner that Lena walks along, staring sightlessly at the ground as she runs straight into Kara's back. The crash of the louder materials slamming together fills the air, and Kara whips around with wide eyes.

    "Your highness, please," Kara whisper screams at her with wide eyes, and all Lena can do is look at her helplessly, her jaw working soundlessly as she attempts to convey every ounce of her regret.

    "Well," Kara huffs, "at least there's nobody around."

    It is at this moment that a quiet rumble sounds from the field to the left of them, and when they whip their heads towards its source, what looks to be a forgotten tool sticking out of a mound of dirt suddenly moves. 

    The largest man Lena has ever seen in her life stumbles to his feet, and turning towards them, showcases the long sword lodged in his gut. He stumbles from side to side with a drunken gait, before ominously turning his head in their direction.

    "Oh," is all Kara says when the mountain of a man begins charging towards them. 

    When Lena meets Kara's fearful gaze one last time, eyebrows scrunched in an expression that says Forgive me, Kara simply pushes Lena backwards by her shoulders with considerable strength.

    Lena lies in the dry irrigation ditch beside the road for a moment, slightly dazed as she tries to make sense of the hoarse scream Kara lets out as she runs directly at the man, removing her cleaver from where it's tucked into her pack. 

    In a single, burning moment of clarity, Lena understands exactly what Kara has just done for her, and subsequently, she rejects it completely, scrambling upright with a wince of pain.

    Seconds before the man reaches Kara, she ducks, dodging to the left of him, and as he stumbles by she kicks at his back. Unbalanced, he runs three tripping steps and falls flat on his face, the sword sliding farther into his gut and out his back with a grizzly crunch. 

    Wasting no time, Kara jumps onto his massive shoulders, forcing him down with her weight as she raises her cleaver, but below her, the man twists, unbalancing Kara's footing.

    And Lena is sprinting towards the two of them, willing herself to run faster, her dagger raised and glinting in the sun. Time seems to slow, and she watches helplessly as Kara falls onto her side, scrambling backwards as quickly as she possibly can while the man crawls after her. The hilt pressed into his stomach catches on the soil beneath, and he tears himself on the blade without care as he grabs at Kara's legs. 

    Finally, desperately Lena bounds up to his side, stabbing down with all her strength. He stills in the way that she has come to know means he is gone, and she leaves her knife buried in his skull, stumbling past him to Kara, falling to her knees beside the panting woman to cup her face between her own shaking hands. 

    "You fool, you absolute fool!" Lena growls vehemently, refusing to allow a single tear to fall. Kara just looks up at Lena with a dazed sort of expression, still breathing hard as almost hesitantly, she raises a hand, pushing back the hair that has fallen before Lena's face. 

    "If the king had really gotten to know me, he would have made me his royal jester instead." And Lena can't believe that that is what's coming out of Kara's mouth. With a barely-contained sense of exasperation Lena shakes her head, squeezing Kara's cheeks together until her lips purse out. 

    "Don't you ever do that again, you hear me?"

    She can already see the rejection forming on Kara's tongue, and something within her breaks on it. In a voice softer than she has ever learned to use, she stares straight into Kara's eyes, attempting to stamp every word onto her stubbourn brain. 

    "We must endure this nightmare together, Kara. That means we both must allow each other to try. This is not the first time I have protected you, just as you will likely save my life again, should we travel together for long enough. I do not doubt your courage, brave heart, but we can only fulfill this role for each other if we go in together. Do you understand what it is I am asking of you?"

    And as Lena speaks, something flashes behind Kara's eyes, and she whispers so softly that Lena can't pick it up. 

    "What?"

    "El mayarah," Kara says, and Lena tilts her head inquisitively. The phonetics sound familiar, but the words remain foreign to her, or perhaps she just misheard Kara.

    "Once more?" Lena asks.

    Kara sits fully upright then, gazing at her through eyes as clear and blue as the sky, and she repeats once more, "El mayarah. It means…" and she pauses thoughtfully for a moment, before saying, "stronger together." Kara gets to her feet then, stands tall and proud before Lena, and holds her hand out before her.

    And she is beautiful against the sky. 

    With a sense of gravity, as if some pact is being made, Lena takes the offered hand, and when Kara pulls her to her feet, they look together upon the corpse beside them with twin looks of distaste. 

    "It is a nice sword…" Kara says thoughtfully.

    "Do you know how to wield a sword?" Lena asks, following Kara's train of thought with a raised eyebrow. 

    "Not really, but how hard could it be?"

    Very hard, as it turns out later that day. Lena winces as, for the third time, Kara lashes out clumsily, crookedly embedding the edge of the sword into the shoulder of a very tall, very angry woman who remains completely unphased.

    The blade does, Lena concedes, look quite awkward to weild. 

    It seems to require a precise and level swing, and no matter how confident Kara looks, most of her strikes land upon the flat of the blade. She finds only a slight bit more success when she employs a quick series of jabbing motions, though none reach their intended mark.

    Lena tries to help, she really does, but Kara has been swinging the blade wildly about with one of the most stoic faces Lena has ever seen, and she fears she will only get in Kara's way.

    At least it doesn't look like the woman is gaining ground, she thinks, only to immediately regret it when Kara yanks the blade free of the woman's ribcage, her arm snapping out straight with the force of it. The sword tears itself from her hands, burying itself in the grass at an angle.

    "You've got to be joking," Kara says, backing up with her hands held up before her in a placating gesture. The woman continues to lurch forward, stumbling slightly from a wound on her knee from one of Kara's more successful blows.

    As she grabs at Kara, Lena dives for the sword, her fist curling solidly around the hilt, and when she brings the polished blade up before her, something about it feels inexplicably right. 

    It feels safer than a dagger, and more powerful than anything she has ever held before. When she moves, it is with an understated grace, striding up confidently behind the woman as she brings her arm up and around in one smooth and deadly motion.

    The woman's head tumbles from her shoulders, affording Lena a view of Kara's awed expression. Continuing the motion of the cut, Lena turns the sword with a slight twist, bringing the point of it straight down and into the woman's still growling head.

    She pulls the blade free, and for a moment, she just stares at the sword in her hand. And then her eyes flick to Kara, who is still standing there, her mouth hanging open. 

    It is with a sort of reluctance that Lena holds the blade out, offering to return it, but Kara just shakes her head. 

    "I don't think I should be the one to have that," she says with a smile, and at her words, a thrill runs through Lena. 

    Kara kneels then, cuts a swathe of fabric from the hem of the woman's skirt with an apology to her, and hands it up to Lena. 

    "To clean your sword," she says, and Lena takes the cloth, running it along the blade until every memory of gore is erased. 

    My sword, she echoes in her heart, and a deep, abiding gratitude floods her, that out of anyone the fates could have given her to go through this mess with, they gave her Kara. 

    -

    It is late evening by the time they reach the first farmhouse. The entire property stands still and eerily quiet, every window either dark, or completely shuttered.

    Their eyes meet once before they press on, Lena drawing her sword, and Kara her cleaver. 

    Lena considers the merits of Kara's current weapon with slight amusement, wondering how Kara can be so efficient with a knife and yet so clumsy with a sword.

    Together they enter into a long L-shaped stable, and peeking warily around the corner, they spy two people, eating the belly of a large sow as she lies in her pen. 

    The sight is gruesome, and at a signal from Kara, they stride up to the pair together. Lena brings the sword down at a slight diagonal, effectively splitting the head of one man, while Kara swings her cleaver into the side of the other man's head. 

    They spring back at the resulting spray of blood, only to still, listening intently for any sounds of movement. A soft snort breaks the silence, and both tense, whirling about with their weapons raised high.

    It is then that Kara relaxes beside Lena, a brilliant smile spreading across her face. With an excited air she walks towards a gate set into the large wooden wall, carefully swinging it open.

    "Hello," she says, low and soothing as she stands just outside the pen, and beyond the wall, Lena hears a creature snort with fear. 

    Curious, she watches as Kara takes a slow step towards the gate, effectively blocking the path. She stands there calmly, wincing slightly when the clatter of iron-shod hooves crash loudly against the walls of the pen. 

    "Woah there, my apologies," Kara says in that same calm voice. The cacophony in the pen quiets, and Kara crouches down, pulling her pack before her with exaggerated slowness.

    She rummages through it, and with a smile of triumph, pulls a slightly wilted carrot from the bottom, holding it out in an open palm before tossing it gently forward. 

    "I can't let you leave this pen right now, you could get hurt. But I won't push you either, okay?" She says, her words, or maybe the tone she speaks in, sending shivers cascading down Lena's spine. 

    Kara sits down fully then, crossing her legs and leaning against the side of the gate, and Lena takes up a position a few feet away where she can see down both ends of the hall. She allows the heavy weight of her sword to rest against her leg as she listens to Kara.

    "I'm glad you've survived this long, my friend," Kara says, "Though I am sorry you have already been through so much."

    She pulls another small carrot from her pack, this one slightly more crisp, and holds it out towards the horse in an open palm. This time she remains completely still, her hand outstretched for a good twenty minutes, and like the thawing of ice, the horse warily approaches, ducking its head to look at Kara every so often. 

    Lena can practically feel the joy radiating off of Kara as the creature nabs the carrot, retreating quickly as it munches hungirly. Kara then picks out a yam from her pack, this time holding it out before her and taking a step towards the horse. 

    Lena hears the creature's alarm as it shuffles back a step, but Kara pauses for a moment, saying, "I promise I will not hurt you." And it's as though the horse understands, for it swings its large head closer to Kara, takes a small half-step forward, and picks the yam up delicately between its teeth, its chin hovering over Kara's palm for a handful of seconds before shuffling back once more. 

    "All right," Kara says. "I am going to close this gate so we can make sure the rest of the farm is safe.  I will return for you, my friend."

    And with that, Kara stands slowly, placing one last vegetable on the ground before backing away slowly. She swings the door shut, wedging a shovel up against it to prevent it from being pushed open.

    "Where did you learn to do that?" Lena asks, unable to keep the awe from coloring her voice. 

    "I just know."

    -

    Once the rest of the farm is confirmed to be empty, riddled with signs of a quick departure as it is, Kara and Lena follow the deep ruts scored into the road that tell the tale of a wagon, piled high with family and belongings, riding towards an unknown future. 

    Twisting a scavenged length of rope between her hands, Kara frowns, thinking of the horse trapped in its stall, listening to the drying screams of the pig. 

    With a sigh, she knots the length into a large, loose circle with a long tail.

    "I have come back for you, my friend," she greets, casting an appreciative eye over the horse's glossy white pelt. When the beast brushes its velvet muzzle against her palm, the warm air that puffs out makes her smile. 

    She places her hand carefully upon the creature's nose, touching the rope to it even as she moves her palm in gentle, soothing circles.

    Gradually, unassumingly, she moves the rope up, and in one smooth and easy motion, she lifts it past the horse's eyes and over its ears.

    The rope falls loosely around the horse's neck, and the creature immediately startles, jumping away fearfully. Kara allows the rope to fly from her hands, and when the horse stills, she picks up the dusty end of it, holding it loosely in front of the horse.

    They stand there for a long while, and when the horse finally looks away, she begins to walk in circles around the pen, only ever applying a gentle tugging to the rope to indicate the direction she is about to walk in. 

    At first, the horse walks simply to avoid her path, but soon it settles into an easy pace beside her. When Kara opens the door the horse offers a slight, wary resistance, but when Kara stops to wait for it, the horse walks out on its own. 

    "Your highness," Kara says with a large grin, stopping short beside the horse's shoulder. "Meet our new traveling companion!"

    The horse eyes Lena warily, but Kara just smiles, placing a hand on its shoulder.

    "Don't worry sweet one, Lena is a friend," she says, and when Lena just looks at her with wide green eyes, deeper than Kara has ever known, a sudden joy wells up within her heart.

    After a long hour of consideration, Kara gives the horse a name. "Ehl Ahvrig," she says. "It means 'the star that is found'."

    Their path detours to three farms on the road to Orvai, two of which are occupied by families, and the other by rats, mice, and a selection of other tiny hidden creatures, and it is there that they spend the weary night.

    The occupied farms they tell of the violence the world has fallen prey to, and though every member old enough to wield a pitch fork looks fearfully after them, they know the families are grateful. 

    Orvai comes into the view at dusk, sprawling out along the edge of the forest, small against the horizon, and instead of traveling the rest of the way in the darkness, they decide to make camp at the edge of the forest. 

    A stream can be heard, tripping and gurgling through the trees, and when they lead the tired horse to it, she quaffs the water with huge heaving gulps.

    -

    The air is cool and clean, and carries with it the scent of sweet rot. Lena wakes, sore and grumpy, having never slept on the ground before that night. When she looks over irritably, Kara is gazing straight at her, and Lena frowns in her direction.

    "It's rude to stare, you know," Lena snips.

    "Good morning to you too. I was just waiting for you to wake, your highness," Kara says with a teasing smile. 

    Lena can't help feeling less than royal. She sits up rolling her eyes in Kara's general direction, and tilts her head from side to side in an attempt to alleviate the cramped muscles of her neck.

    As she rises, Kara begins to walk off into the forest. 

    "Where are you going?" Lena asks after her. 

    "I set some snares while I was on watch last night. We may yet have some breakfast this morning. I'm sure Ehl Ahvrig would like to go down to the water as well." And with that, Kara disappears through the trees. 

    Absently, Lena wonders how Kara ever navigates the forest. Every tree and shrub looks the same to her, but without fail, Kara returns shortly holding two coneys in her left hand, and the rope that encircles Ehl Ahvrig's neck in her right.

    The horse snorts softly as Kara ties the lead to a tree, bending to nibble at the grass, and as the sun sets her silvery pelt aglow, Lena thinks she has rarely seen a more beautiful creature.

    She watches as Kara places the two coneys on the ground, kneeling silently before them with closed eyes. 

    "What are you doing?" Lena asks softly.

    "Thanking them for their lives," Kara murmurs back. They have died so that we may survive, and that sacrifice is important to acknowledge, even if it changes nothing of their lot."

    Lena nods, contemplating the sentiment, and after a moment, she sends her own offering of thanks. 

    When Kara begins preparing the coneys, it is oddly less gruesome than anything she has seen over the past two days, though Lena still has to force herself to watch.

    Kara begins by removing their heads before hanging the little creatures upside down from a tree. She places her little pot beneath them to collect the blood, and begins gathering up several sticks and twigs, organizing them by size into two separate piles. 

    At her request, Lena brings her a small nest of dry grass, into which Kara strikes her spark rocks, sending hot little embers into the bundle. 

    A strong crackling fire rises up before them in no time, and satisfied, Kara disappears for just a moment, only to return holding a large flat stone and two forked branches. Upon the stone, she cleans the carcasses, removing the fur and viscera, until two pale, hollow bodies lay beside each other.

    She then digs small holes on either side of the fire, and secures the two forked branches upright. From her pocket, Kara pulls a handful of green herby plants, stuffing them into the hollow carcasses before pushing a long wooden stake through both of them. And then, she does something Lena does not expect.

    Kara reaches into her pack and pulls from its depths several little wooden containers that Lena has only ever seen the outsides of. With a gleeful expression, Kara opens one after another to reveal red, green, gray, white, and yellow powders.

    An incredulous look crosses Lena's face, and she looks up at Kara with a faint smile. 

    "Spices? Kara Danvers, do you mean to tell me that in the middle of the most horrifying, cannibalistic event we may ever experience, your priorities were to loot spices?"

    Kara grins, the corners of her eyes crinkling. "Your highness, what world is worth living in if you can't eat good food at least once?"

    And then more seriously she adds, "You know, I have only ever tasted these spices in meager amounts. Prior to working for the royal family, I had never known much more than salt. The most I have ever really been allowed to sample though, is usually confined to taste tests."

    "Only taste tests?" Lena asks with a note of surprise, and for the first time, Kara turns to regard her seriously. "Highness, are you aware of how much some of these spices are worth?"

    Frowning slightly, Lena shakes her head.

    "Let me put it this way. When I started working as a cook at the castle, the pockets of my trousers were sewn shut, and I was given a pocketless apron. That is standard amongst the kitchen staff, because a single peppercorn," Kara emphasizes her point by holding up a tiny, black round object as small as a blackberry drupelet, "is worth more than three lifetimes of my wages. People have suffered their hands cut off for thefts as small as this."

    Shocked, all Lena can do is stare at Kara, her mouth slightly open. Every meal she has ever left unfinished flashes before her eyes, and a sudden wave of shame courses through her at the wastefulness of it all.

    "So, yes, spices were one of my priorities, for when shall I ever have the chance to experience something like this again? I figured, 'even if I don't live long, I might as well allow myself the true experience of what I could have only ever tasted.'"

    And Lena takes the sentiment as yet another thread in the tapestry, falling into contemplation as Kara grinds the peppercorn into dust with the handle of her knife. She rubs salt and a carefully selected set of spices onto the carcasses, sprinkling the ground pepper on last. 

    "Why did you become a cook, Kara?"

    The lines at the corners of Kara's eyes appear once more, but she does not smile, placing the stake over the fire and rotating  it slowly.

    "When you're offered employment by the royal family, you take it." She says simply. "As for why I became the head cook, a Duke who once sat at your brother's table recognized and praised what he named my 'gift for flavor,'. It paid well, but it was without question the most perilous job I have ever worked."

    Lena raises an eyebrow. "For the fire and knives?" 

    A distant look enters Kara's eye, and carefully she says, "You know, the leading cause of staff shortages in that place is execution… It's not exactly a place you can easily make friends in."

    And Lena thinks of her own loneliness in the castle- how it pales before the fear that Lex's servants must have felt, and she cannot meet Kara's eyes.

    "I did know," Lena admits in a quiet voice.

    At her words, Kara's head lifts to level her with a piercing stare, the smoldering embers of righteous rage burning straight through Lena. "You knew?" She says, in a voice deadly with calm. 

    Sudden anxiety leaps within Lena's chest, and she feels the frantic urge to take back her words, or to feign ignorance. The only thing that gives her pause is the feeling that if she lies, or speaks carelessly, Kara will know somehow, and will never forgive or trust her again.

    Eyes downcast, she nods hesitantly.

    "And did you ever once speak out against their barbaric methods of toying with people's lives?" Kara says, retaining the evenness of her tone even as her hands tremble slightly, and Lena understands for the first time the terror of those faced with a righteous rage.

    "Please believe me when I say I tried, Kara. It's was not my place-"

    "Your place?" Kara interrupts. "When we left Argo, you spoke of courage- Of dying a meaningless death in pursuit of those still held within the castle walls, and yet when it comes to the cruelties laid out before you by your family-"

    Lena holds up a hand, cutting Kara off. 

    "I will forgive your tone, only because you don't understand what it's like to hold the position that I held within my family. Do not presume to know the depths of me, or the complexities of my life."

    And Kara's face twists into a look of such deep displeasure, Lena knows that she has made a grave error.
