    "I may not understand the complexities of the privileged life you have led, your highness, but know you this: I, and everyone I hold dear, has suffered greatly for it."

    The anger that had reared its defensive head within Lena quiets somewhat with confusion.

    "Then tell me, so that I may understand." She implores, in a voice shaky with emotion. They fall into an awkward sort of silence, and in the stillness of it, Kara's face gentles slightly. 

    "I owe you my life, highness, as you owe me yours. But I do not owe you this." She says, refusing to meet Lena's eyes. 

    And so, they sit together, tense with all that remains unsaid as Kara continues to turn the stake.

    Her words fling themselves from one end of Lena's mind to the other, and she wonders in futile circles exactly what Kara has suffered at the hands of her family. 

    Something within her aches with regret to be the source of that pain. 

    The rabbits release a wonderful, mouthwatering aroma as they cook, and in time, Kara's expression softens farther.

    To speak now, Lena thinks, would still be a mistake, so instead she takes one of the spare strips of cloth from her pack to soak in the stream.

    She holds it out towards Kara- a small peace offering, and Kara looks up at her for a moment, her expression tinged with a wariness Lena wonders if she has missed this whole time. 

    But Kara takes the cloth from her, wiping her hands of blood and dirt with a nod of thanks. Not quite trusting herself to speak, Lena takes her place on the opposite side of the fire.

    The rabbits, when they have finished cooking, are crisp and golden, the meat within steaming with an herbal scent. Taking each end of the stake in her hands, Kara snaps the stake in half, waving both in the air in an attempt to cool the meat impaled upon them.

    Lena takes the offered spit with nothing but gratitude, and though it nowhere near approaches the culinary riches she has grown up with, she finds herself absolutely famished. 

    It's almost painful, waiting for the meat to cool, and when it does she digs in hungirly. Across from her, Kara gazes almost lovingly at her treat before taking her first bite, her eyes rolling back with an exaggerated moan. 

    "Gods above, you ate like this? Every day?" 

    "Not anymore," Lena says ruefully, and she can't help herself. She smiles at Kara's infectious delight as she watches her companion devour the meat quickly and efficiently, and when Lena hands Kara a quarter of her own rabbit, an absurdly sincere look of gratitude blooms across Kara's face.

    "How could you ever go back to plain salted food after this?" Kara asks mournfully, as she savors the last scraps. "How can I?"

    She laughs then, grinning cheekily at Lena. "Maybe one of the damned will take me before I have to find out."

    And though the sentiment is more than morbid, but it still makes Lena smile.

    -

    As Kara sits beside Ehl Ahvrig with a feeling of contentment, Lena surprises her by sitting next to her, their sides nearly touching. The stray hairs of her bun tickle the side of Kara's face, and when she looks over, an odd feeling stirs in her chest. It's a confusing sort of twisting, and when she tears her eyes away from Lena's face, it remains lodged in her chest.

    "Kara?" Lena says, with a slight question to her tone. 

    "Hmm?"

    "Would you like me to teach you your letters?"

    And Kara turns to face Lena, searching her expression for any signs of patronization. All she finds is a sincerity to rival her own.

    "I would like that very much, your highness." Kara says, and at her words,  Lena looks down awkwardly, pulling at the fingers of her left hand. 

    "I wish you wouldn't," she murmurs softly. 

    "Wouldn't what?" Kara says. "Want to learn?"

    "Refer to me as- Well, out here, it just feels- I don't know, wrong I suppose."

    Kara just smiles, nudging playfully at Lena's shoulder. "You're still a princess, even with a little dirt on your hands."

    But Lena just shakes her head, gazing sadly at her hands, still soft with disuse. 

    "Not out here. Here, I am nothing."

    With a frown, Kara catches Lena's eye, and pouring every ounce of conviction she can into the words, she says, "Never that."

    And Lena gazes at her with a shining, unreadable expression. 

    "Never, Lena." 

    And Kara utters her name so gently that the lightest breeze of the sky carries it away.

    Chapter End Notes

    This chapter was one of my favorite chapters to write initially. I was so excited to delve into the dynamics (and the yearning, God the yearning) of these two, for good and for ill.

    Anyways, my lack of ability to wait on posting things once they are finished benefits you directly, so please, enjoy the story!

    Thank you to those who have commented on and shared this! I've been working really hard on it, and the support means the world. <3

    My Father's Daughter

    Chapter Summary

    The sins of my father are mine to bear, for the fruits of his labor have elevated my standing.

    Chapter Notes

    When they arrive at the village of Orvai, the sight of smoke rising from the chimney stacks and the sounds of children playing between the houses instantly bolsters Lena's spirits.

    They arrive travel-worn and dusty, trudging down the main road to Orvai with Ehl Ahvrig close behind. She prances to the side snorting skittishly, knocking Kara several feet back even as she tries to press herself against the woman for protection. Lena watches with some amusement as Kara struggles to regain her balance, patting the silver horse's flank with a soothing coo.

    With a regal grace forged into her from girlhood, Lena draws herself up, raises her chin, and walks into Orvai as though she is wearing her finest gown and not the drab, blood-stained travel dress that currently hangs from her shoulders, and together they make their way to the center of the village.

    The people of Orvai follow them long down the road with curious, almost mistrustful stares, almost as if they can recognize the two travelers for the harbingers of death that they are. As the three of them near the village center, marked both by a sign and a tall bell tower, a merchant selling meat pies points them in the direction of the town hall. 

    Offering her thanks, a smile, and a coin pressed into his hand, Lena marches directly towards it, leaving the man to hand a steaming pie off to a delighted Kara.

    They enter the shadow of the tall building, and when Lena begins ascending the stairs, she realizes that Kara has stopped. Turning with a raised eyebrow, she fights back a smile as Kara gestures helplessly, first to the pie and then to Ehl Ahvrig, and with a fond roll of her eyes, Lena waves her back, continuing on alone.

    Two guards stand outside the door to the entrance of a large courtroom, looking for all the world as though they are bored out of their minds. Before they can think to block her passage, Lena strides between them and into the large room, her dusty skirts swirling about her ankles with every step.

    Sitting near the back of the room in a large chair is a man- young by most measurements of responsibility, who regards her with a certain brightness in his eye. 

    "I wish to speak with the person responsible for this village," Lena says, her voice carrying with authority through the nearly empty room. The man looks at her curiously, sitting slightly straighter as he beckons her forward.

    "I am the right hand of the Lady Catherine Grant," says the man. "Sir Winn Schott, by name. What is it you would have me hear, Madam?" 

    And so, Lena steps forward to the center of the room, tall and proud and bloody with the memory. 

    "Sir Schott," she says, almost surprised that one so small has managed to secure a knighthood,  "The land is awash in devilry- overturned, overrun by a sickness that damns our dead, allowing them to rise up beyond the passage of their souls. 

    Their every quest and reason seems to be to devour the flesh of the living."

    Her eyes drop to the floor as she forces out the words, "Argo has fallen." 

    A wounded look of surprise crosses Winn's face, and as he bows his head, a shadow falling across his eyes.

    "How can this be?" He asks, a slight tremor in his voice. 

    "I know not sir. I know nothing of the fate of any who survived the city's walls beyond my own and my companion's.  

    We have come to beg sanctuary with you, and to implore you to open your gates to the refugees of this madness.

    I believe we must gather as many people still alive to ourselves with the purpose of uniting against the fallen, for they may very well take us all."

    When she quiets, Governor Schott remains looking down, his brow creased with concern.

    "Perhaps over dinner we might discuss what it is exactly that has befallen you. I would take your counsel. If the world is indeed in the state you claim it to be in, then I must know what my people are to face."

    And Lena feels an immediate surge of relief, because she is starving and she has been listening to Kara's stomach growl for the last seven hours.

    We made it. He is going to listen to us. And then what?

    -

    Dinner is fresh fowl and cooked vegetables, and Kara has never been more excited for a meal since the coneys. As she tries her best to savor the meal, she listens to Lena and Winn while they speak of the damning of the world. 

    "Let this be clear to me: you fear not the infection, but its victims?" He asks.

    "I fear both, but its victims more."

    The knight remains silent for several seconds before he says with a solemn frown, "Three days past, we came across one such as the damned souls you speak of. He harassed a youth on his farm, but his family was able to detain the man, and he is currently being held in a wooden cell far outside the village, for fear of contamination. He has neither slept, nor spoken a word in his imprisonment."

    Crossing his arms with a frown, Winn continues, "I would ask a favor of you- In the morrow, would you would ride out with me, to identify him as one damned or alive?"

    Lena nods once, and with a look of gratitude and gravity, he breathes a deep sigh. "So, tell me. How did Argo fall?" 

    And Kara's fist clenches around her fork as she remembers how small the boy had looked- how different his skull had felt beneath her knife. She sits there, frozen in the memory of blood pooling in the smoky streets, seeing nothing as she stares down at the table.

    "-And Kara saved me," Lena's voice cuts in. "Without her, I would have been many times lost."

    Kara glances up to find Lena looking directly at her, eyes searching and seeming to ask Are you all right? And Kara tries to offer a true smile, she really does, but she just can't feel it.

    "Once we escaped the castle, we saw the outer ring burning, swarmed by the damned. Between the castle and the wall, I would place their sighted numbers at nearly five hundred when we fled."

    "Five hundred?" Sputters Winn. 

    "Most definitely more within the entirely of the city, for we passed through but a portion of, and the fires raged at all ends of Argo." 

    With every word the crack in Kara's heart widens. She pokes at her food, still trying to savor the hot meat, but it just makes her feel like crying.

    "Any survivors that escaped might still be hiding in or around the countryside, and I believe we must search them out, both for their sake and for ours, for if you can house them, they will contribute to your house."

    The Governor takes a shuddering breath, and he asks the question Kara feels as though he has been dreading and fixating on. "How many do you think might have escaped?"

    "I have no answers," Lena replies, soft and sorrowful, and at her words, a terribly weary and expression crosses Winn's face, and he looks more fragile than any leader aught to. 

    "The Lady Catherine departed for Argo not six moons ago. She took with her a man- James Olsen by name, to seek an audience with the king." As Winn speaks, his voice shakes slightly, softening imperceptibly as the name James falls from his tongue.

    As he speaks, he looks at Lena with a candid, searching clarity. "Your highness, did you see or hear of this man?"

    When the title falls from his lips, Kara looks at him with surprise, wondering if Lena had divulged her prowess in the hall. No, she thinks, for she sees the surprise that lies beneath the carefully neutral face Lena regards him with.

    "At the time of the reckoning, the King's days were filled with errands of a personal nature. I heard of no audiences granted in those days, nor many of the days prior. I am sorry."

    With a sour look on his face, Winn nods, and in a voice unable to contain its bitterness, he simply replies, "Ahh."

    They sit in the silence for a long span of moments, before Winn lifts his chin from its place tucked against his chest.

    "The two of you," he says slowly, "are welcome in my house. I have the room, and I would hear your counsel, for if the world is truly in the perilous state you claim it to be, I must know what it is our people are to face.

    And as Lena and Winn continue to talk the night away, Kara falls into her own thoughts, and in their depths, she wonders how much time will pass before Orvai meets its doom along with the rest of the world.

    -

    One Month Later

    The great forest provides many nooks and clever crannies in which several persons might hide or tuck themselves away in. But only if they are clever. 

    As it happens, Kara is riding happily alongside Lena when a distinct yelling rings out distantly to the left of the path. With a shared glance the two quickly dismount, and Kara pats Ehl Ahvrig's back with a gentle stroke, deftly pulling the blanket from the horse's back to place it on the ground. 

    She shrugs her pack to the ground beside it, pulling only her bow from its frame and slinging it over her head to rest over her quiver.

    Orvai's weapons master had offered her a proper weapon in preparation for both a harsh winter and a harsh war, but every weapon she had laid her eye on besides the bow, had felt unyieldingly awkward in her hands. 

    She had taken the bow then, and the following morning Winn had taken her to the tanner and asked that a sheath be crafted for her blade.

    Maybe I just wasn't made for fighting, Kara thinks as her arrows rattle softly beside her ear. But then, what man is?

    Looking first at Lena, and then at the horses, Kara unsheathes her cleaver and begins running through the trees. The horses trot after them, free and unencumbered, their nostrils flaring with fear even as they push on. 

    As the four of them crest a shallow hill, a stone knuckle comes into view rising high out of the earth. Upon it, two people stand, staring down at the base where roughly sixteen of the damned mill about in various stages of decay and agitation. 

    Raising her cleaver with a motion towards the four of them and the boulder, Kara catches Lena's eye, but Lena shakes her head in response, pointing towards a distant tree. 

    She then touches the bow on Kara's shoulder, looking up into her eyes, and while Kara's heart thuds in response, the way Lena points to herself and then towards the boulder fills her with immediate denial. 

    Slashing her empty hand through the air, Kara shakes her head, but Lena just nods, pointing stubbornly at the horses and brandishing her sword. Her eyes say It's the best way, and you know it. And Kara does, though she despises the knowledge.

    So it is with a sigh of resignation that Kara holds up her hand and then taps her chest with a fist three times. 

    Wait three beats after the first shot.

    Her face turning fearsome and still, Lena nods, readying herself, the leather of her armor creaking slightly as she raises her sword. 

    Quickly running down the hill, Kara virtually launches herself into the tree, searching rapidly for a clear vantage point. She finds one in moments, and once her footing is secure, she draws five arrows between her fingers.

    Deep breath in,

    She knocks one arrow, draws the feather to her cheek, stilling her mind and her body.

    Deep breath out.

    At the bottom of her breath, she relaxes her grip and looses the arrow. It whizzes down towards the most daunting of the damned, striking him in the head with a quick thwip.

    She cannot allow herself even grim satisfaction, fighting instead to retain the stillness of her mind. 

    First beat. 
    Second arrow, one more head- this time, a quick youth. She almost misses him.

    Second beat.
    A third arrow, a shot to the shoulder that Kara will curse as the difference should Lena fall. A vow to do better.

    Third beat. 
    A fourth arrow, whose feathers tickle her cheek, only to adorn the ear of a woman that reminds Kara of her mother. 

    And Lena is running, the thunder of hoofbeats beside her, and Kara wishes she had told her to wait five beats, or ten, or as many as it would take for Kara's arrows to run dry, and for her to go with them. 

    Lena always calls Kara's heart brave, yet here she is, running headlong towards a group of the damned, and she looks so small before the crowd. 

    A fifth arrow, and a sixth, and a prayer that her arm does not somehow betray her and hit those she is meant to protect. 

    A seventh arrow to the head, and a sword stuck in a thick neck, and Kara throws her bow to the ground, drawing her cleaver as she drops from the tree, sprinting madly. 

    She knows that the tree is objectively a better position to provide cover from, but Lena and the horses stand before an advancing wall of death, and she cannot. Will not. Cower in a tree.

    Ehl Ahvrig rears up, her hooves kicking out, sending a man to his back, and as he falls, Lena tears the blade from where it is lodged in his neck, stabbing his head even as he meets the ground.

    Lena's mount, a black stallion named Zil Eshs, or Night Sky, kicks out with his iron-shod hooves, smashing in the head of a woman as though it were an old, hollow pumpkin. 

    What a magnificent creature, Kara thinks. She remembers the stirring of her own spirit when Lena had come to her one day, asking her advice both in naming and in gaining the trust of the horse. 

    Sir Schott had gifted the stallion to her to aid in the perils of their missions, and when he had revealed Zil Eshs to them, Kara had thought Lena might cry at the sight of him. 

    A month spent teaching him to trust them alongside Ehl Ahvrig revealed his character to be noble, fierce yet infinitely soft, and as he throws himself against the group, Kara knows he does it to protect them. 

    Without hesitation, Kara throws her cleaver at a man who has latched onto Zil Eshs' leg, fingers grabbing at the horse's hind even as he bucks and kicks frantically. When she dives to retrieve her cleaver, Lena thrusts her sword up into the jaw of a woman, suspending her right above Kara's head.

    Old blood drips onto the back of her neck, and with a shudder Kara yanks the cleaver free, jumping into position beside Lena, whose eyes dart to the half-empty quiver in her back. 

    Kara almost dares the woman to order her back before burying her knife into the side of a short man's head. With a look of intense focus Lena spins her sword in a deadly fan, severing an arm Kara did not see reaching for her, and though she sputters as the blood sprays into her face, she still manages to grin.

    Fighting with Lena is like dancing, and she thinks she understands a little of how men might find joy in a war. She darts forward, nearly hacking the face off of the rotting head of a woman whose teeth graze at Lena's hair with an audible click, and when Lena's eyes meet hers for a moment, glittering with a fierce brilliance, Kara knows she feels it too. 

    They cut and dodge and defend the horses as each other, and as they do, the people crouching atop the boulder jump down.

    The man stoops briefly, plucking from the grass a long staff with a blade fixed to the end of it. Like a heron he jabs it forward, effective, quick, and at a safe distance, as his companion- a woman with a long brown braid, draws a thin saber that looks to be of foreign make. 

    Any sword that thin, Kara thinks, must surely break on impact.

    Wrong, as it turns out. The woman whips the sword forward, and like a willow switch it bends and whistles through the air, slicing through the head of a gaunt man with the subtlety of a sharp wire. 

    Intrigued by the weapon, Kara watches the motion of it only to yelp with surprise when Lena's blade stabs past her face to embed itself in the roof of an open rotting mouth. She flinches, and Lena turns her burning eyes upon her with a reproachful look that clearly says, Focus.

    Kara swallows, nodding, and takes in the scene before her only to lunge back as Ehl Ahvrig barrels into one of the damned. The horse whinnies as she slams the woman into the base of the boulder, and Kara sprints to where she lays on the ground, quickly splitting her head. 

    Beside her, Zil Eshs lets out a fearsome cry, and Kara turns to look, her jaw dropping as the horse kicks one of the damned straight onto the man's spear. He grunts as the impact shakes him, only to stare at the bleeding head where the tip of his spear used to be.

    "What are the chances?" He whistles, jerking his weapon back. Years later, he still swears that the horse winked at him as it turned away.

    And like a wave, their panting is the only thing that fills the air. Everyone stills, weapons raised, listening and searching, only to gather quietly beneath the trees where Kara and Lena had dropped their packs and riding blankets.

    Farther from the mass of death, they relax slightly, offering their forearms to each other.

    "Thank you for saving us," says the woman, and her companion nods with a sharp bird-like motion adding, "Your arrival could not have been more timely."

    And he turns to Kara, holding out two of her arrows. "It would be a shame to leave such fine arrows behind. " 

    With a delighted smile, Kara takes them, exclaiming, "Indeed it would be! Thank you, good friend. Now, business bids me ask, what business have the two of you so deep in these woods?"

    And the couple share a look so weary and sad, she almost knows.

    "We have traveled far, from the city of Kandor, whose walls have fallen to the dead." The woman says in a tone of grief Kara is well versed in. "We have come in hopes that we might seek refuge in Argo." the man says, and Kara can see the raw wound of it within his eyes.

    She glances over at Lena who is chewing her lip, looking worried and unsure. But Kara knows anything less than the truth could doom a man in this world.

    "Friends," she begins, forcing back her own sorrow, "you have truly known a great loss, and for that I am sorry. Cruel fate would have it that we share both this lot, and this grief. 

    At the height of summer, Argo fell. 

    My companion and I fled the very heart of the city to the village wherein we endure- the village of Orvai.

    For two fortnights our quest has been to gather the survivors of this illness behind our walls, in hopes that we may all survive this madness to see a brighter day.

    Will you join us?" Kara asks, her voice soft and open, and the two strangers look at each other for a long time.

    "You don't even know our names," the woman says eventually, and Kara just grins at her.

    "Well, my name is Kara, and my friend's name is Lena. We hail from the village Orvai." She says, raising her eyebrows as if to say, Your turn.

    "My name is Querl Dox, and this is my friend Nia Nal. We hail from… Kandor." He whispers the city's name softly, like one might whisper the name of a loved one, and Kara holds him firmly with her eyes, willing her own strength to pass to him.

    "Querl Dox and Nia Nal of Kandor, you are welcome to return with us to our village, to find a home, and a people, should you choose to stay."

    And the look of longing the two give her is palpable.

    -

    The day passes in a blur of travel and talk, and when their company stops for the night, the air is still warm. The stars array themselves brilliantly, and the trees speak in the tongues of the ocean, whispering of things man has long forgot.

    With four people on watch they feel confident enough to allow a fire, and so, as Querl and Nia stare out into the forest from the border of their camp speaking in hushed tones, Kara and Lena sit by the warm crackling heat, content to draw in the dirt.

    "And this," Lena says, dragging her stick through the dirt, "is the letter T."

    The ridges of the dirt excised from small trenches cast little dancing shadows across the ground, and as Kara traces the lines, she puts to memory the square angles of the letter.

    How simple it is, she thinks, that all we speak and write can be contained by so few symbols. 

    And she looks up and her breath catches in her throat, because the firelight is warm upon Lena's cheek, casting her fine features in alluring shadow. 

    Is there a word for this? She wonders.

    Swallowing dryly, Kara forces her eyes back down to what Lena is scratching into the earth, and a surprised bark of laughter leaves her when she sees a very round, very sad looking creature. 

    Lena's sharp frown has her hurriedly straightening her face, looking between the drawing, and the 'T'. 

    Kara follows that circuit three more times only to glance up helplessly at Lena, whose eyes seem to say, Do you get it?

    She does not, and after several long seconds of scouring her brain for any semblance of recognition, Lena huffs in exasperation, saying in a flat tone of disappointment, "It's a toad, Kara."

    "Ohhhh, I see, I… 'T' begins toad, I must have missed that." And Kara tries to be sincere, she really does.

    But then Lena cracks, a wide grin dimpling her cheek, and Kara can't help but let her mirth spill over. 

    "It looks more like a toad with every passing second!" She laughs, but Lena just shakes her head, leaning closer to pat her hand on the dirt, erasing the drawing.

    And Kara's eyes are drawn to the line of Lena's throat as she speaks, and she feels the throb of her heart in her temples. 

    I would be nowhere else, even if the other side of the world were free for the knowing, she thinks, and the thought is almost too much for her to ignore.

    It is too much to ignore, and her gaze drops to Lena's mouth, and the colors of her lips are so enticing. And Lena stops talking when she sees, and Kara knows Lena sees but she cannot stop herself from looking. And wanting.

    Something within her knows that whatever this is, it is a hidden truth of her- the kind that would stand in plain sight but for the lies she tells herself. Something inalienable and essential. 

    And though she isn't ready, she opens that box; stares the truth of it directly in the face. With clear intent, slow enough that Lena can lean back if she wants to, Kara leans forward, raising her hand almost timidly to graze the backs of her knuckles against Lena's cheek, softer than she could have ever imagined.

    Before her, Lena remains frozen, watching Kara approach but never flinching, never pulling back. And Kara's heart thunders, lodged firmly in her throat because she can feel the heat of Lena's breath against her lips, and-

    There is a rustling in the brush, and as they leap away from each other with guilty expressions, Querl and Nia enter the circle of light. 

    Shakily, Kara smiles, raising her hand in friendship as Lena breathes out beside her, her ears tinged with red. 

    -

    One Fortnight Later

    The air takes on a slight chill as time wears on, but the expeditions never slow. Lena knows that anyone to be found must be found before winter, lest winter damn those that find themselves lost in the cold. It is with an almost incessant drive that she launches herself and Zil Eshs out into the wilderness, endlessly grateful for the fact that Kara always offers to go along.

    By now, Lena is almost certain she could survive in the wilderness, having absorbed lesson after lesson that Kara has offered her on following game trails, setting traps, starting fires, and purifying water.

    One thing she lacks, however, are Kara's tracking skills, invaluable in their own right as these searches continue, for she seems to find clues of people and their headings as though they are glowing beacons, not marks held private by every leaf and twig in the forest.

    When Kara signals Ehl Ahvrig to stop, Lena follows suit, gently clicking twice into Zil Eshs' ear. Kara dismounts then, signaling the horses to wait, and without question Lena follows her as she pushes through a thick brush. 

    Keeping a hand near her sword, Lena listens intently, eyes searching the trees as Kara crouches down before a print in the earth, only to curse and stand quickly.

    "The damned are near," she whispers over her shoulder, and Lena nods, pulling her blade from its leather loop. Kara reads the tracks as quickly as she can, darting forward until the sounds of a distant struggle can be heard. 

    They run towards it, turning with the bank of a dry riverbed to see a man, teeth gritted into a feral snarl and arms locked out as he holds back two of the damned back by their chests. He pants and strains with the effort, and bounding up without hesitation, Lena brings her arm up, turning her body with a sweeping slash that drops his two attackers to the ground.

    Kara runs up, a hand held out to the man, and when he gets to his feet she turns back exclaiming, "Lena, that was amazing!"

    But the man does not share Kara's excitement. He examines Lena for a long, hard moment, his expression twisting into a mask of pain and anger. 

    "I know your name… and I know your face," he says, his voice choking slightly.

    A prickle of fear runs down Lena's spine, but instead of charging her like her instincts tell her he will, the man just stands there, his shoulders turning in and his hands curling into trembling fists.

    From a deep frown he glowers up at her, whispering, "Though I doubt you have ever even heard my name."

    And Lena looks over at Kara helplessly, but Kara's gaze is focused on the man, unblinking and ready. 

    "Or the name of my son… My daughter-" his voice fails him, and in the absence of words he seems to crumple farther into himself despite his large frame. 

    He fixes his burning eyes on Lena, seeming to damn her in every way. 

    "But perhaps you have heard of my village. My village to which I was returning, from my cousin's farm." He says, and the rawness of emotion within his voice sends a shiver through Lena. "Only a day's journey away. And when I returned…" He takes a gasping breath, shaking as he looks into the heavens. "Wessel was my village. My home. My family."

    And with a sudden look of understanding, Kara looks at Lena, that same accusing glint in her eyes, and it feels like a physical blow to her chest. She takes a step back, unsure, even as the man begins to yell.

    "And why?" The man asks, almost manic. "WHY?"

    Lena has no answer, racking her brain desperately. "My brother spoke of Wessel, said the infection had breached your walls-"

    "It was a simple fever, any doctor could attest!" The man screams in response. "I returned home with medicine for my children, and my home was burning." He cries, tears leaking from the wrinkled corners of his eyes.

    And Lena doesn't know what to do. She feels her lip tremble involuntarily like it did when she was a little girl just learning to control the motion. 

    "Oh yes," says the man, "Cry over my children whose names you have not even learned. What use are tears when the bodies of my children lie on my parent's graves?"

    With a troubled look, Kara steps before Lena then, and in the same voice she used with Ehl Ahvrig, she holds her weapon out to the side in a loose grip. 

    "By the same hand, I have known the sting of your loss, good sir. My regrets cannot ever hope to soothe such pain, but still, I would offer them."

    The man gazes skywards, nodding at Kara's words, though Lena isn't sure he truly hears them.

    "Many now have lost their homes and their families, and many we have gathered to us in the village of Orvai. My friend, I bid you join us, for our purpose is to search out those thrown to the wind by cruel fate, to offer sanctuary, and to make of ourselves a home in which we might survive this world."

    With a feeling of unreality, Lena watches as the man spits at her feet, turning his venomous glare upon her once more.

    "I would rather the wind take me, and the wolves blunt their teeth on my bones than betray my blood by sharing camp with a Luthor." He grits out, shaking with ire. Gathering his pack in his hands, he growls back at her, "May you never have the misfortune of crossing me again."

    And Lena stands, motionless, the point of her sword grazing the ground as they watch him go. She takes in a shuddering breath, and sighs, looking up at the sky, tears of frustration and anger building at the corners of her eyes.

    I have failed for a shortcoming that is not truly mine to bear, she thinks, and with a frustrated laugh she sits down with her back to the river's tall bank.

    "I am my father's daughter," she says quietly, and immediately Kara sits down beside her, watching her carefully. 

    "I am my father's daughter, but I was not born noble." she says, and it feels like a weight has been lifted from her chest as speaks the words she has only ever been allowed to think. "I was born to a peasant in the countryside of Kandor. I ought to have grown up there, in a little house, with nothing to my name but the clothes on my back and the love of my mother." And the bitterness of that statement seeps into her words.

    "Then Lionel…" she says quietly. "I grew up with a woman who was not my mother and a man who was not my brother, and have through these thin strands of blood, inherited every one of their sins."

    And Kara looks at her ever kindly, though there is an odd look to her eye. 

    "Like so many in this world, you have lost much," she says in that voice that reminds Lena that kindness still exists in this world.  "You are not your family, Lena. Nor have you committed the sins they have, but in the eyes of the people you are tied to them, for you have received the benefits of the Luthor name, and they are plentiful. 

    By accepting one, whether by choice or by default, you tether yourself to the other, for the benefits of your standing exist only for the crimes of your blood. For this, the last son of Wessel was indeed just in his wrath."

    And Lena is horrified because it is a truth so plainly mapped out- a truth that stains her hands in the ways she has always tried to keep them clean, and it is terrifying.

    "But," Kara says gently, "You are not your family. So long as you live, you have the chance to carve out your own legacy instead of laboring away at theirs- to decide to become that which they chose never to be, though it was their sworn duty. 

    I have seen you choose with every passing day to conduct yourself with kindness and with grace. The selflessness of your many acts do not escape me, and I do not take them for granted."

    And Lena forces every roaring voice of pride down, trying to accept what Kara has just said, though it fills her with loathing for herself.

    "Twice now," she says, "you have mentioned the hurt my family has dealt you."

    Kara stiffens beside her slightly, looking off into the forest, and Lena puts a hand on her arm, saying gently, "If ever comes the time in which you would tell me, know that I will accept you, and any pain you have to offer."

    And Lena has never meant anything more. Kara relaxes, a look of gratitude spreading across her face, and they sit there for a moment more, allowing the weight of everything unsaid to settle between them.

     

    Chapter End Notes

    Oh MAN. Things are about to get wild!

    Estimating the amount of chapters that will arise from a list of bulletpoints is never an exact science, so, uh, this story is going to be longer than anticipated.

    I'm not complaining though :D

    Some notes on this universe:

    -I have decided that infection is not passed from the infected, but that their bites cause a necrosis that cannot be recovered from.
    -Kara still has her spices tucked away for a special occasion.
    -The city of Argo houses 5,000 within their walls, while Orvai houses a mere 200 (a sizeable village).
    -Kandor is another city under the rule of the Luthors, housing roughly 2,700 citizens.

    Thank you all for reading, and to those who have offered your kind commentary, you are the sole reason I was able to get past the editing stage of this chapter. Thank you.

    Next: Family ties and a harsh winter.

    Olde Friends

    Chapter Summary

    In which the only survivors of this land just happen to be queer.

    Chapter Notes

    Wasps buzz thickly through the air, their simple purposes lending a hum to the passing leagues that blur in a gradient of green and yellow fields. The breeze carries with it a slight chill- an ancient harbinger of change that whispers menacingly of the future. 

    Soul after lost soul seeks sanctuary in Orvai, leaving our resources to dwindle with increasing rapidity. And still, winter approaches with a speed we can neither match nor delay. We cannot afford to sit idly by- to allow this fate to run its course, nor can we turn away those who would join us.

    Then what? Then what?

    Frowning, Lena rubs at her forehead, a headache beginning to bloom on either side of her temples as another thought springs to mind- this time a memory of her father frowning stoically down at Lex in one of his attempts to impress upon his young son the important lessons of ruling.

    Your power lies in numbers, he had rumbled in his deep, rich timbre. With steady hands, you must maintain the fragile balance of control, for within the people lies the strength to conquer entire nations. But be warned, a serpent starved will swallow its own tail.

    At the time, Lena had listened idly, content to read before the fire, paying little heed to the lessons Lex often fell subject to. Now, she begins to understand what her father had meant. 

    Their return to Orvai is sharp with silence, and as if she can sense the contentious nature of Lena's thoughts, Kara walks along behind her, content to lead the horses as she broods. When they enter the village, she tells herself she isn't panicking, even as she charts a course straight to the house with a murmured request that Kara see to the care of Zil Eshs alongside Ehl Ahvrig, just this once.

    "Anything," Kara had said. "Anything for you."

    Hurriedly, almost frantically Lena scrubs the sweat and old blood from her skin, and once clean, she dons a fresh tunic, her hair still damn against her cheek as she marches to the town hall.

    As she knows she will, she finds Winn sitting at his large wooden desk, hunched over a parchment marked with nearly as many lines as those that crease his forehead. 

    "Winn," she greets almost breathlessly, and though the markers of stress still score his face, he greets her with a warm smile.

    "Lena, my heart is glad indeed to witness your safe return. Did your venture meet with success?"

    Bitterly Lena shakes her head, her mood plummeting at the memory. "Nay. But let us lay that venture to rest for the time, for there is another matter we must discuss." 

    Leaning back, Winn folds his hands atop each other, offering his silence as an invitation.

    "What must be done," she asks, turning to pace with nervous energy, "to prepare for the scarcity of winter? Already our numbers have increased by forty souls, yet our stores of grain remain stunted by our reticence to tend to the fields, and to cart our harvest to Striped river."

    As she speaks, the lines deepen once more upon Winn's forehead. 

    "Every night since we first began this campaign of soulsaving, I have asked this of myself," he says in a voice more somber than she has ever heard him use, "and every night, no solution presents itself to me. To turn these people away is to damn them, and by damning them, ourselves. 

    To bring in too many damns us anyways." 

    "Aye. It is certain that we cannot abandon those who would join us, therefore we must devise a way to extend our food," Lena says with more conviction than she feels.

    Winn frowns skeptically, his mouth set in a line of resignation.

    "And how ought we do that? I have read and reread the harvest predictions for the wheat, the barley, the rye; I have counted and recounted the mouths we are to feed, man and beast. This list," he says, holding up the parchment, "says in no uncertain terms that we shall be short on both by mid-winter, and far depleted come the end of it.

    As Lena walks back and forth along her self-appointed track, she pulls anxiously at her fingers, as her mind races.

    "I believe you, Winn, however I must ask you to reread and to recount them once more- for my sake, and to my own ears. Beyond food and livestock, I too would know the numbers of our other various resources, no matter how insignificant, so that I might pit my mind against this conundrum alongside yours. 

    There must exist a way for us to continue on."

    So it is that late into the night a rooster crows, and with surprise, Lena sits up to find pale blue light filtering in through the small windows of the hall. 

    Littering the floor about her is a maze of parchments scrawled with notes, reports, lists of names, occupations, resources- and amidst these papers, she feels like a spider in its web, tugging at the strings of information and watching the effects of subtle choices spiral out into devastating consequences within her mind's eye.

    The ecosystem of Orvai is especially delicate now due to the lack of trade, decreased access to farmland and the mills, and their ever increasing citizenship.

    "If we were to limit the portion of grains alloted to the chickens, perhaps-" 

    And for nearly the thousandth time Winn shakes his head, yawning widely. "Less grain is the path to less eggs, and should they stop laying, we may as well slaughter most of them alongside the cattle for all the good they will do us."

    Refusing to allow herself to fall into the ever-nearing abyss of hopelessness, Lena nods, eyes jumping through the puzzle of papers.

    There must be a way.

    -

    The first day of Autumn

    The cleaver lies flat against the table, slightly dented, though the integrity of the blade remains stubbornly intact. It gleams up at her with an almost accusing glare, and to the flashing of it, she has no response. 

    You have warped my purpose, it seems to say, and Kara can't help but feel twisted herself. In her short life, she has never desired anything but peace, yet the simple demands of this world seem to allow for anything but. 

    Beside the blade lies the open book Inferno- one of the three Lena had taken from the castle all those weeks ago. 

    Over the past month Kara has been crawling through the pages of myth and legend, sounding out each stanza nearly thirty times over, only to stitch their convoluted meanings together.

    More than half of the narrative escapes her, and it is of itself slow and morbid work that often leaves her exhausted by the time she sets it down. Still, reading offers with it a sense of pride, however small it may be, and though she often burns with the inadequacy of a child, Lena's delight at her progress never ceases to lighten her spirits.

    Absently she finds herself wondering which layer of hell she might call her own. Perhaps the ninth, for what could stain her hands with more permanence than any else, but the blood of those she once named her friends and neighbors?

    A harsh thudding sound jolts her out of her reverie, and blindly she grabs at the cleaver, raising it over her head as she whirls to face the empty room.

    Her heart races in the stillness, even as the tension of her flesh fades into a vague sense of panic. Casting one last look at the blade, Kara lowers it, only to jump as the sound repeats itself. This time, she recognizes it as a pounding on the door, and with a troubled heart she answers it, her head bowed in thought.

    Three dusty, blood-marked strangers stand before her, and for a moment she is reminded of her own arrival to Orvai. Two of them stare at her with equal measures of confusion, and with an awkward raise of her hand, she returns their stares.

    "What… Who are you?" One of the men- the taller of the two questions with a frown, an edge of surprise to his voice.

    "I- who are you?" Kara returns defensively, one eyebrow raised.

    "My name is James Olsen. I seek the man who lives here, Sir Winn Schott." He demands with an air of rightful entitlement. 

    Frowning, Kara glances quickly from face to face, only for her eyes to catch on the shorter man. Though he is turned slightly away, he seems familiar to her in some indecipherable way. 

    The wide brim of his hat shades his face, and beneath it, a large bandage wraps about the right side of his head. 

    Absently he turns to meet her eyes, and a shock runs through her.

    "Kara?" He breathes, and his voice sounds higher than Kara had expected.

    And she would know that voice anywhere. 

    "Alex!" Kara practically shouts, bounding forward. With a sense of unreality, she pulls her sister into her arms, knocking the hat clean off her head.

    "I can't believe-" Kara begins, as Alex says, "I thought you were-" and the sight of her now shortly cropped auburn hair and her kind face is enough to heal the world for a moment. 

    "Your face," Kara breathes softly, the fingers of her right hand reaching out to hover just barely over Alex's cheek.

    With a grimace, Alex takes her hand, holding it tight. "It's nothing," she says looking away, and Kara sees the sadness and the untruth of her words. 

    Nearly a thousand questions bumble through her mind, yet none of them seem right to ask, so they just stand there for a long, silent moment. James clears his throat, only to turn at the sound of feet pounding up the road behind them.

    Winn, arms pumping like mad, sprints up to his door, breathing heavily as he stumbles to a stop before James, an expression of pure wonder and heartache accentuating both the youth and age of his face. And he looks almost afraid to touch him, but James shows no inhibitions, crushing Winn within a hug.

    "Arven spoke truth- he told me he saw you, and here you be, love." 

    "I will always come back to you."

    "Never leave."

    As they stand there in each other's arms, a sudden realization floods her. Love. They are lovers, she thinks. Lovers.

    And she remembers then that soft night- the way Lena had allowed her to near; the rightness of the pull she had felt, though it remained one of the most incomprehensible things she had ever experienced. 

    "Kara," Alex cuts through her thoughts, drawing the third person forward by her hand. "This is Sam." 

    Though her words are simple, there is a certain importance to the way Alex introduces her that feels almost formal. And Kara realizes she recognizes Sam, who smiles pleasantly, her eyes dancing, and when she holds a hand out, Kara takes it, laying a soft kiss to her knuckles.

    "My Lady, I do not mean to exclude you," she says with a bow, and when she rises, the whole group is staring at her with confounded and slightly amused expressions. 

    Suddenly terribly unsure, Kara looks quickly from person to person, stammering, "I… Are you not a lady of the courts?"

    Sam just laughs, shaking her head with amusement, even as James' face falls.

    "Though I could fill the dresses of any lady of the courts, the title has ever evaded me." She says with a prurient wink that causes Kara's ears to heat.

    "Ahh, well, regardless of what station you maintained in the past, welcome to Orvai." She replies, mentally slapping her forehead as the words tumble out.

    In the end, it is Winn who saves her from her own awkward fumbling, clapping his hands as he lets out a deep whooshing breath.

    "Friends, let us not tarry by the door any longer, for I am certain your travels have left you weary and bereft. Come, bathe and dress yourselves in the linens of my house, and we shall have a meal. I would hear your accounts."

    And as the last words depart his mouth, his gaze flicks to James, searching his face even as he refuses to meet Winn's eyes. 

    So it is, freshly bathed and dressed, that they crowd around Winn's table, awaiting a steaming pottage of peas, onions and beans, and though Kara seasons it sparingly, the gratitude they express towards the meal is greater than any she had ever received when working with the finest of ingredients.

    The sound of the door opening and shutting announces the arrival of Lena, who strides in through the doorway, eyebrows high with surprise at the number of guests around the table. 

    To Kara's eyes she seems incredibly weary, though she never assumes any posture short of perfectly straight, and fondly Kara watches the movement of her hand as she fiddles with the hem of her red tunic.

    But Lena freezes, her eyes locked onto Sam, and she walks forwards with steady purpose, gathering the woman up tenderly within her arms and cupping her face with a gentle hand.

    "Your hand is rougher," Sam says with that same coy smile, though her eyes glisten with joy and tears. Lena simply shakes her head, laying a kiss to Sam's brow as she whispers, "What mercy has spared you, I could never repay."

    And it tugs at something within Kara's heart- something dark and restless that she shuts away within the deepest recesses of her.

    Lena regards the rest of the room then, wiping at her eyes. "You are most welcome- all of you," she says. "My name is Lena."

    The brief look of shock and revulsion that crosses Alex's face wounds Kara in a way she does not understand.

    "Your highness," James bows, "My name is James Olsen, and my other traveling companion is Alex Danvers."

    At that, Lena whips her head around to face Alex, eyes jumping between Kara and her sister like twin grasshoppers.

    "I have long heard fond tell of you," she says with a note of surprise, sitting on the bench beside Kara. "So fond, I admit I have more than wanted to meet you." 

    At first, Alex regards Lena with a flat, shrewd expression, but when Kara's foot collides with her shin, she seems to remember herself.

    "Ahh, your highness, I am at a loss for words. It is not every day that a member of the royal family desires to meet with someone of my station."

    On Kara's other side Sam winces, but Lena simply regards Alex with an expression Kara has learned to mean that she is choosing her words with the utmost care.

    She says finally, softly, "You have endeared me to you by every story of kindness and bravery Kara has told me. How could I not?"

    Alex's shoulders soften slightly, and in a softer voice, she says, "Well met then, your highness."

    A sad smile crosses Lena's face then, and in a soft voice, she implores, "I pray thee, use not that title for me."

    And Kara feels like slapping a hand to her forehead when Alex without any guise of politeness or decorum asks, 

    "Why?"

    The table goes awkward and still around them, and once more Lena goes silent and thoughtful.

    "In this new world, there exists no kingdom whose rules we must abide, nor any hierarchy that existed prior that has done justice to the needs of the people. 

    Though I can never escape the crimes of my family, I desire never again to pay homage to that legacy, nor to take it for myself."

    Beside her, Sam's jaw drops, as before her, Alex continues to meet Lena's open gaze, looking as though she is measuring every word against the person. James simply nods his acceptance, and though Kara can sense his surprise, he maintains an unaffected exterior. 

    "I would ask," says Winn, "if our guests are sated and comfortable, and if so, that they might regale us with an account of their travels. I would allow more time for you to rest before plying you with this favor, but news of the land and its happenings is more valuable than gold.

    The mood darkens slightly, but Alex sits up straighter, saying, "Your kindness has earned the labor of my heart, sir." 

    And Kara takes a deep breath, as Alex begins her tale.

    "The night that Argo fell, a man rode to my door. He begged me to go with him on behalf of his wife- told me that come the morn, she would wither away, where a day prior she had been bursting with life. 

    His pleas stirred my heart, and so, I rode back with him to Argo. He told me he had bribed the night guard to open and close the gate upon his arrival, and so we entered the city.

    Her ailment was nothing I had ever seen. "In the morning, my wife suffered a bite from a young boy," her husband had said. And surely there was a sizable bite upon her right calf. 

    The strangeness of it was found in the skin around the bite, for it was discolored and dead. Every hour that passed, more tissue stiffened, and the terrible blight grew.

    The poultice I applied did nothing for her, and the infection of her flesh stirred a fever within her. She passed that same night."

    The dark flesh beneath Alex's eye tightens as she lives the memories. "But strangeness of strangeness… 

    Not three minutes passed, and the man called for me, told me her eyes were open once more. At first I thought it was some trick of the body- one I had never seen before; some mechanism that lends the eyelid life, even as the body stiffens mysteriously. 

    But there she was, sitting upright, and for a moment I thought death himself cheated."

    Alex looks down at the table then, her face twisted with regret. 

    "I was wrong, of course. He hugged his wife to him, and as he did, she took her pound of flesh, dooming him. It was in that moment I knew the infection rumored to plague the East had come to Argo."

    When I ran from the house, I trapped the two of them inside and barred the door, only for another of the afflicted to charge me. I ran, and I ran, and I found myself at the stables. Many noble beasts were already being devoured." Alex says, anger clear in the clench of her jaw and the shining of her eyes.

    "I climbed onto the roof of the stables, and waited the night there, listening to the horrible tearing of flesh and the screams that echoed in the night. In the morn, I heard the sounds of a struggle below me, and as I leaned my eye over the roof's edge, I spied Sam, holding her shoulder against the stable door while a woman battered violently against it.

    Before my courage could desert me, I jumped to the ground and grabbed the first thing I could swing."

    Sam laughs then, the corners of her eyes crinkling with fondness. "Never before has my life been saved by a pitchfork," she says, and Kara turns to Alex, eyebrows raised.

    "A pitchfork? Truly?"

    "Aye," Alex nods, ducking her head almost sheepishly. "It bore us safely through the city." 

    "It, and its wielder," Sam says fondly, and Kara smiles at the openness of her affection.

    What good fortune, that we might find ourselves with such pleasant company in these dark times.

    "How did you escape the castle?" Lena blurts, her eyes flashing with guilt- a guilt Kara recognizes better under blood and moonlight. 

    Sorrow clouds Sam's face, leaving her somber and solemn. "I hid," she says simply. "At the first sounds of screams, I thought we were under siege, and sought refuge in the space beneath my bed. Many spiders crawled into my hair, and for a time, all was still. But then Jorran opened my door, only to fall beneath the weight of one of the cooks, Mishe."

    Stunned, Kara stares at her hands beneath the table, and she understands with sudden, brutal clarity, the guilt she can feel radiating from Lena. 

    If I had not made us leave the city, perhaps… and Kara searches Lena's tense expression for any trace of the same thought, wondering once more if she held Kara to blame for leaving everyone behind.

    She would be right to.

    "I laid still and quiet even as her blood soaked into my clothes, and there I stayed 'till the morn, long past the time she arose. At first light, everything seemed so quiet, and clutching my life to me, I ran through the servant's corridors. 

    There I found poor Haden, pressed into a recessed door. He was relieved to see me, but perhaps he should have run the opposite way. 

    We fled the castle, and it was there in the inner ring of the city that fortune failed us."

    Sam's gaze, now fixed on the table, darkens as she presses her lips together tightly. "The streets were strangely still, and I thought it wise to search out a horse. Haden agreed, but as we passed by a row of houses, a man sprang from the darkness of an open door, and knocked him to the ground. 

    He… he hit his head, and fell to slumber and tooth."

    Eyes soft with understanding, Alex reaches a hand out across the table, offering her palm, and when Sam slips her hand into Alex's, she takes a deep, steadying breath even as a tear falls from the corner of her eye.

    "I ran for the stables, and there I met Alex. We traversed a good portion of the city by rooftop, and when we ran through Argo's western gates, we came upon James and… And the Lady Catherine.

    All eyes turn to James, and he clears his throat several times, his eyes downcast.

    "Ah… Well. My duty- that is…

    When Lady Catherine and I arrived at Argo, we came upon Alex and Sam. They warned us against entering the city, and at first we were inclined to pay them no heed. The blood on their clothes stayed my decision however, and we listened to their words of caution.

    "Then we must travel to Kandor," My Lady said, "for it is of the utmost importance that the news of this reaches their city.

    So we thanked them, and went on our way. But the night hides treachery better than any Judas. We awoke to the screaming of the horse, only to bear witness to its bloody end.

    We fled, continuing down the road on foot until the dawn. My Lady was most unhappy, and our progress slow, but we found refuge with the farming families on the way. In two days time, we were overtaken by Alex and Sam."

    "We too were on the road to Kandor, for there I was born, and had many a friend." Sam adds, a heavy sadness within her tone.

    "Alas, Kandor was a mirror to Argo," Alex laments. "As soon as we set foot within the city we knew it to be lost, but when we turned to leave, the dead flooded the streets in our wake, barring the entrance from us. 

    At every turn we found ourselves pushed deeper and deeper in the maze of Kandor, and eventually, we arrived at the other end of the city. There, the souls of the damned roamed thickly, and we turned once again, unsure of the path."

    "We decided to traverse the rooftops," says James, "for though they lie on even ground as opposed to those of Argo, they are closely packed together in many places. 

    We traveled quite far in this manner, but eventually, between two rooftops there spanned a great alleyway that required a sure foot and a great running leap. 

    I jumped, and turned to catch the Lady Catherine." 

    Eyes cloudy, as though looking across a great distance, James raises a hand, almost as if he still stands upon that rooftop. 

    "I had her hand in mine for a single moment, but she missed, slipped, and for my failure to hold her, she fell."

    And finally, terribly, James meets Winn's eyes, his shoulders shaking almost violently, though his face remains stoic and expressionless.

    "We escaped Kandor in much a similar way to Argo," Alex continues. "And as we reached the gates, one of the dead charged us."

    Sam tenses beside Kara, and a horrible twisting begins somewhere within her stomach. 

    "He was nearly upon Sam, when I pushed her to the side. I almost got away too, but his hand caught in my hair, and he sent me to the ground, whereupon I landed in a pile of broken glass. 

    Blinded by blood and pain, I thought it to be the end, but before the man could bite me, James slew him.

    I lost my eye, but I kept my life."

    And Alex says this so flatly, Kara winces. 

    "A farming family allowed us to stay with them for a day, and from their farm, we traveled on foot to Orvai. From there on you know our tale."

    -

    At the end of the meal, Winn, citing urgent business he must attend to, leaves for the city hall, pressing a lingering kiss to James' temple. 

    James himself retires, leaving Alex, Sam and Kara at the table to speak in low, serious tones. By Kara's expression, Lena knows that she is offering their tale in return, and though she longs to join them, she casts a worried glance to the door.

    Slipping out quietly, Lena makes her way through the near dark, and in the large room of the town hall, she finds Winn, sitting at his desk with his head in his hands.

    He sniffs thickly as she approaches, angrily wiping at his nose. 

    "I cannot fill her position," he says in a small, shaking voice.

    "But you can- you have, since the moment you became the steward of Orvai," Lena protests.

    "That is different!" He says, waving his hand through the air. "Before, I was simply holding the position for Lady Catherine. Now, the accursed balance of souls rests firmly, unquestionably upon my shoulders, and I cannot bear it, Lena."

    And though she feels his fear like a spike being driven into her chest, she approaches him calmly, crouching before him and taking his hands in hers.

    "You can bear this," Lena says, gazing firmly at him, "for you are not alone. I will help you, and so will James, and Kara. The entire village would help you if you asked, for their survival depends not only on your choices, but upon theirs too. 

    This burden is one I shall gladly shoulder beside you, for as much as they are your people, they are mine as well. You need not make these decisions alone, nor need you be the sole bearer of their consequences."

    And he looks up, his shoulders slumped, looking weary beyond his years. 

    "How can we withstand this plague, Lena? How can we possibly endure the winter?"

    "Because we must," Lena says. "And we will do it with the souls of every person firmly in our hearts and minds. Should we fail, we fail with honor, knowing we fought this to the end.

    And for a moment, the light of life flickers in the depths of Winn's eyes. "Aye. Even if the cost be my life, I would not die any differently."

    Lena nods. "I know, Winn."

    He sits up straighter then, the set of his jaw resolute as he says, "Very well then. Together." 

    And when he meets her gaze, there is a strength behind his eyes that fills her with hope.

    "Together."

    Chapter End Notes

    Finishing this chapter was like pulling teeth and I may never reread it again :)

    Next: Ye olde winter adventure: A Danvers special.

    The Heist

    Chapter Summary

    Two Danvers, one braincell.

    Chapter Notes

    The days grow ever colder and the winds pick up a tireless nagging, refusing to allow any to forget what is to come. Orvai bustles with activity as every tradesman, farmer, and able-bodied person organizes into a delicate puzzle of interlocking efforts.

    At Winn's right hand, Lena ensures the tradesmen are plied with multiple apprentices- to their petulant grumbling; that houses and buildings with room to spare are filled- a feat in itself, for the needs of each family are varied, and none are ever truly happy about it.

    Still, regardless of the difficulties that are bound to arise with the overcrowding, all remain grateful for a roof over their heads.

    "Those who tend to the fields," she had said to Winn, "ought to be accompanied by guards, and by any extra hands if we can spare them, for the labor must go quickly. To stand out in the open for too long is to invite peril. 

    I think we must also send scouts and hunters out into the countryside, two per party who are suited to each other and their task- that being, to search for fields that have been sown and abandoned, or to capture any roaming cattle and livestock."

    So it is that Lena stands in a large field, squinting up at the gray sky as she measures her hand against the height of the sun in the way Kara had taught her to. 

    Shifts guarding the farmhands are her favorite, for the stillness of the fields and the vastness of the sky overhead affords her a peaceful clarity of thought. These days, moments of contemplation are a rarity, and she often finds her attention pulled in several different directions from dawn until dusk. 

    The field is bright and open, and she can see the meandering, purposeless figures of three damned souls in the far distance, warily watching their progress. 

    "Hand me that," grunts an old farmer by the name of Khune as he gestures towards a shovel. Despite the chill in the air, sweat pours from his wrinkled face, falling to water the ground in an ironic cycle of give and take.

    Her sword scrapes against the ground as she bends to pass the shovel to the man, and with a barely present nod, he takes it from her hand and drives it into the ground between his feet. 

    The field they stand in is a property owned by none present, but an abandoned farm found by Eve Teschmacher- a young woman from the farms in the outskirts of Orvai. 

    Eve is light upon her feet, and possesses a shrewdness of perception and wit that grants her an affinity to the work, and for her efforts, five farms have been discovered, whose occupants have long fled, their absence marked by weeds and cobwebs, or evidenced by old remnants of gore. 

    To harvest the work of another's hands feels ineffably wrong, Lena thinks, but to allow so much barley to rot in the ground would be a sin far greater.

    In the distance, the three figures walk towards the sounds of striking tools and lowing beasts, their uniquely swaying gaits causing them to fall on occasion. 

    Lena watches them approach for thirty long minutes, and when the first man stumbles onto the road, she puts him down with a decisive jab of her sword.

    The second and the third, she fells with ruthless efficiency, their heads tumbling with soft thuds into the grass, before swallowing the blade's shining point. 

    Wiping the mess from her steel with a weary sigh, she squints up and allows the blue of the sky to clear her vision, returning to her musings.

    The newer residents of Orvai have proved to be invaluable, desiring more than anything to prove themselves as more than burdens upon the shoulders of their new neighbors.

    Even now, the fields are worked by the hands of Orvai, Argo and Kandor, and by midday, their labors meet completion, as tools are loaded into carts, and the great stacks of barley tower high above the wagons, to be returned to Orvai. From there, small group guarded by Nia and Arven will take the wagons to the mills along Striped River, returning at the end of the night with the freshly processed grain.

    The demands of survival requires the value of the coin to disappear, for every man, woman, and youth begins to understand that the work of one man's hand is also the work of their neighbor's. 

    And it seems nearly backwards, for stripping the work of its assigned value elevates it to something priceless. 

    For Lena, her life is filled with more purpose- more rightness than it ever has been. The pains and hardships of simple life have faded a bit into normalcy, though some nights she desperately misses the comforts of her past. Some nights, she pulls out her small pouch of coins and stares at the glittering silver, wondering how something so small could have held so much power.

    Just before Autumn fades, the granaries are full, their stocks of grain at their driest and most ideal temperatures, and with a feeling akin to relief, Lena predicts that Orvai will have enough food to survive to the last week of winter. If the winter crops should grow on schedule, she believes Orvai might just succeed

    If, she thinks, we do not gain any more mouths to feed.

    And for the first time in months, she allows herself to breathe.

    -

    Winter comes upon them with the violence of an angry god, swift and without mercy. The ground freezes, rendering it impossible to plow, let alone sew the wheat for the spring harvest.

    They gain four more citizens when a family, gaunt and wrapped in rags, arrives with the first snow. Along with the largest of the cattle and livestock, several smaller members of the herds are slaughtered too, in an effort to conserve grain, and Lena begins to keep a ledger of who to offer spring's first calves, kids, lambs, and piglets to. 

    Regardless, she cannot help wondering with each beast felled, whether it would be that one that defines the difference between life and death.

    I never imagined wool would be a resource we would fall terribly short on, she thinks, though the results of the coat shortage meets its own conclusion when the people of Orvai begin wearing their blankets about their shoulders, pinning them at the throat for warmth.

    The second month, they are able to break ground, and the weeks are a blur of action- of plowing and planting fields upon fields of wheat and barley, spreading thin their limited manure over the fields.

    Lena has combed through the numbers- through the lists of every edible resource; through the timeline of plant, cultivation and harvest dates for the year prior, and though they are behind schedule, she remains hopeful that Orvai will survive without undue casualty, even if the rations must thin even more. 

    But on the twenty-third day of January, winter's frost leaks into one of the granaries, poisoning the grain within, and the mess is discovered far too late. 

    With twin looks of dismay, Winn and Lena stand over the nearly chest-high pile of blackened grain.

    "We are lost," Lena whispers, a rebellious tear falling from the corner of her eye as her every hope turns to ash.

    Winn just stares at the moldy pile, his face as despairing as her own, and for the first time since arriving at Orvai, Lena feels truly afraid. Perhaps they won't get through this; perhaps maybe more people would have survived had she not arrived and decided to bring another seventy-five mouths to feed.

    The snake has swallowed its tail.

    As if he can hear the darkness of her thoughts, Winn puts his hand upon her shoulder, and though his eyes shine with his own unshed tears, he gazes at her with nothing but kindness.

    "To whatever end, we will fight this doom," he says, low and steady. "We have done what we could do, and should we fail for this, then we shall fail with honor, knowing that we never surrendered our people. 

    I am proud of you. For all that you have done, Lena."

    And she cannot help the way her lip trembles as she looks up at Winn, or the way her mind echoes with Kara's voice. 

    El Mayarah.

    "Thank you," she says, and their doom seems somehow less fearsome.

    -

    Odd, Kara thinks, squinting through the trees as she crosses through the shallow snow. The tracks tentatively meandering the road had veered off suddenly into the forest- the first deer they have found trace of in a week, and she had immediately taken off after it.

    Just within the trees a man stands eerily still, seeming simply to observe the forest. She sees no tracks behind him, and when she nears, the snow around him remains unbroken.

    Slowly she pulls the cleaver from her belt, and edges nearer, her tread loud upon the snow, but he remains standing, still as death.

    "Hail, friend!" She risks calling, but he does not turn to face her, and when she circles him, she lowers her arm with a surprised curse. His face is partially rotten, and where his nose ought to be, a fleshy hole disfigures his face. 

    And then it dawns on Kara.

    The damned have frozen. 

    She buries her cleaver in his skull, leaving summer to lay him down in her sweet grasses.

    The damned have frozen. 

    Eyes wide with the implications, Kara sprints to the road as quickly as she can.

    Not three days prior Lena had come to Kara's door, looking smaller and more fragile than ever she could recall. And as she had whispered the terrible news to Kara, a horrible twisting sickness took ahold of her gut.

    "How am I to tell them?" Lena had asked, her voice tiny and trembling, and Kara simply held her close, unsure herself.

    But now…

    "Alex!" She cries as she kicks the snow from her pants and boots, bursting into Winn's house.

    At the table, Alex sits hunched over the bleeding arm of a youth, stitching closed a small gash. He winces and grits his teeth, whimpering slightly each time the needle pierces his skin.

    "They're frozen!" Kara exclaims bounding into the room, almost manic with excitement. "Do you know what this means?" 

    Alex just looks at her, waving her to sit with a look of slight annoyance, and like a chastised child, Kara sits and waits, nearly vibrating with contained excitement.

    Once a poultice has been slathered on the boy's arm and a bandage has been laid securely atop it, Alex sends the boy on his way with a weary sigh, washing her hands in a basin before wiping the sweat from beneath the leather patch that sits over her eye.

    "Who is frozen, Kara?" She asks, her expression brightening slightly as Kara's energy suffuses the room.

    "The dead! They are frozen, though whether all remain still or simply slowed I know not. But know you what this means?"

    Alex shakes her head, and Kara forges ahead without a hint of hesitation or doubt, "This means that those within Argo's walls may also be still with last night's frost- that there may exist the opportunity to ride in without fear, and to secure a wagonload of grain from the castle's granary."

    A brief moment of satisfaction washes through her as Alex's jaw drops.

    "You're mad," she says softly. 

    "I saw one." Kara replies firmly. "I came from the forest where I saw a man standing in the trees- he was frozen in the snow, and nothing had struck him down.

    There exists a chance- a real chance to restore hope to Orvai."

    "Argo was home to thousands, Kara. We have both seen what lies within her walls. If you are wrong…"

    "If I am wrong, then they may still be slow enough to escape, or to fell with ease."

    For one long, quiet moment Alex chews her lip, and Kara can almost feel the storm of thoughts as they rage within her head. 

    "When must we depart?"

    "We?"

    With a severe frown, Alex glares at her. 

    "Who else accompanies you on this venture?"

    Hanging her head, Kara remains silent, offering weakly, "What if you are needed?"

    "J'onn is a skilled doctor. He will make due without me for a week. 

    I will not allow you to do this alone, Kara,  so tell me, when must we depart?"

    "Today. As soon as we can," Kara says, after a moments hesitation. "Every day carries us farther from the last storm, and boldly the sun shows her face. We must strike while the iron is hot- or cold, rather, should a day make all the difference."

    "Then we are going to need thicker clothes."

    Kara smiles grimly. "There aren't many of those going around these days."

    With a weary sigh, Alex nods in agreement. "It changes not our need for them."

    She is right, Kara thinks as they run to their room, pulling layer upon layer of thin clothings over their head, and wrapping thickly their feet with long strips of cloth before pulling their heavy boots on.

    With a guilty knock, for she knows he is out, Kara enters Winn's room, raiding his wardrobe for the thickest wool coat and hat he owns, offering the empty pegs a silent promise to return them. 

    She runs to the center of the village to gather rations for the venture, returning with a small bundle of dried meats and a large sack of oats, which she fastens to her pack alongside her bow and quiver. 

    Knocking quietly on the room that Sam and Lena share, Kara's ears perk up at the sounds of two voices, eager to share her discovery and plan with them. Sam bids her enter, but when she opens the door she sees Alex, red as a tomato, sitting beside Sam on the edge of her bed. 

    "Alex spoke to me of your plan." Sam says, gazing seriously up at Kara. "Thank you. I wish you both good fortune, and a safe return."

    And she smiles slyly at Kara, as though she is laughing at a joke heard only she is privy to. 

    "Lena guards the efforts to sew the northern fields, and she shan't return until nightfall. But she will know of your plans from me, and of any other words you might wish to leave for her."

    With a bow of gratitude Kara smiles awkwardly, unsure why her ears have become so hot. 

    "Thank you. I would not leave without saying goodbye, but time races against us. Please tell her I will return for her, and that I know what she is doing for us all. That for her, I could hold no more pride in my heart lest it burst."

    As soon as the words leave her mouth her blush deepens, and though Sam looks at her in a way Kara can only describe as pointed, she smiles kindly, reiterating her promise to carry the message.

    So it is that Kara and Alex shoulder their packs and run to the fenced fields of roaming livestock, their boots crunching through the thick crust of snow, and soon James and a youth with a bow come into view, standing guard over the herd.

    "James!" She yells, waving an arm wildly through the air. "Where is Querl to be found?" 

    Several sad looking creatures swivel to face the ruckus, and after a moment, Querl pops his head up from behind one of the small cows, raising a stiff hand as he jogs in their direction. 

    "Querl, I am in dire need of a pair of strong shoulders to pull two of our largest wagons. Some shovels, four days worth of feed for the two of them, and any spare sacks that can hold grain- any sealed barrels and crates, I need them all. As many as you can find! 

    Oh- and rope!"

    With a wordless nod, Querl whistles to a man, Erish by name, who is currently kneeling by one of their few remaining sows, and points out two oxen who stare dolefully after him as he sprints towards the barn.

    As Erish ropes the great beasts, a soft snort rustles the hair by Kara's ear, and with a thrill of delight, she turns as Ehl Ahvrig leans her face over her shoulder.

    "Hello my friend," she greets, savoring the soft velvety feel of the horse's nose against her palm. "Has Querl been keeping you good company?"

    Ehl Ahvrig simply snorts, her hot breath warming Kara's cheek. 

    "You are right as always, but do not hold it against him." She banters, whispering secretively, "Truthfully, the rations are decided by Lena."
