    She swears she catches a gleam of betrayal in the horse's eye, and she smiles, rubbing at Ehl Ahvrig's smooth shoulder in a consoling manner. 

    "Hey now, do not fret. Spring shall come yet, and with it, many carrots. Besides, I have a plan."

    The sounds of running footsteps approaches, followed shortly by Querl who gestures for Kara and Alex to follow him. 

    Bidding a quick and regretful farewell to the mare, Kara follows Querl to a tall barn wherein stands a massive wagon. Several sacks stuffed full of other sacks fill half the wagon, along with several lengthy coils of rope, five crates, and eight barrels.

    He disappears once more, leaving Kara and Alex alone, and when the oxen trod slowly up, Erish locks the heavy yoke around their necks, embarking on the lengthy task of buckling the great creatures to the wagon's front. 

    Querl returns, this time leading a large draft horse who draws behind it a second, smaller wagon, and as the oxen pull their load forward, he brings the second wagon up behind it, securing them together with a length of chain.  

    Gratitude floods Kara as she looks at the labor of his hands, and as he steps back to look over his work, she offers him her forearm.

    "Thank you, my friend. We must depart with haste, else I would tell you of our venture. Know that you have helped me greatly."

    Clasping her arm, Querl dips his head, his eyes bright and serious. "Be as the wind, then, and return safely."

    "We shall."

    -

    The winter stars wheel overhead, brilliant and glittering like a sea of dew. A low creaking fills the silence as the wagon scores deep tracks into the snow, and standing at the forefront of it, watching the oxen sway, Kara allows the vastness of the sky to swallow her.

    The air stings her cheeks, and her breath forms small clouds of frost that curl into the sky, and for a moment, she feels like a dragon- impossibly large, yet still so tiny in the eyes of the heavens.

    With a groan, Alex rises from where she lays on the floor of the lurching wagon, moving to stand beside Kara, and together, they watch the land slide by.

    "Are you prepared?" Alex asks.

    "For what?"

    "To return to Argo."

    Unsure, Kara looks down at her hands where they grip the edge of the wagon, offering a noncommittal shrug.

    "I must be." Is her simple reply, though she can hear the rawness in her own voice.

    Alex nods, gazing out into the darkness for a long minute. "You always were the stronger of the two of us," she says softly, and the sentiment is confusing, for Kara knows the weakness of her own heart- the anger and the hurt that may never heal, nor sweeten with age.

    "I know not why you say that," she whispers, her words freezing in the air before her, and with a sigh Alex leans over, bracing her palms against the edge of the wagon in a pose that mirrors Kara's own.

    "I say it for the bravery I see in your heart. There may be a foolishness to your methods, yes, but there exists too a nobility that no king could think to match."

    And at her words, Kara feels like crying.

    "Lena told me what you did for her," Alex says. 

    "What?"

    "She told me of the way you saved her, when you could have easily left her to die."

    "That was no bravery- it was a choice I would make for any. Besides, she has saved my life many times over."

    "It is a choice not many would have made in your position," Alex says, pointedly. "I of all people understand what that means. What you have done for her. What you do for her now."

    Too weary to pretend ignorance, Kara turns to regard Alex.

    "I do this, not just for her, but for you, and for Orvai as well."

    "Both are true."

    Kara accepts the point silently, wondering what exactly Alex is driving at. 

    "Do you ever think that Orvai is not meant to last- that it has simply evaded its doom for longer than the rest?" Kara asks, and Alex goes quiet and still beside her. 

    "Aye, sometimes I do think it, but I give none of myself to that fear. Doom comes for us all in many differently shaped tragedies, and on no man's time. What matters then, is that we live as ourselves so that we may die as ourselves. 

    To me, it matters not if I die tomorrow or in five years, for I will have known these at least once in my life: the inalienable truth of my soul, and the experience of genuine happiness."

    And Kara thinks on that notion- thinks of the truths she hides from those around her, and then brazenly, of the truths she hides from herself. 

    "It makes the loss of it all no easier to bear," she whispers, and Alex shakes her head in agreement. "Nay, but it makes life easier to live."

    And she cannot dispute the truth of that sentiment. 

    "So then, what of your life?" Kara turns to ask, a small smile tugging at her lips. "What joys spur you to continue, long past the twisting of the world?"

    "You, of course." Alex says, her tone serious and contemplative. "Being alive with you, somehow; the work of healing, though it is endlessly bitter; the home we have found." And then, so soft Kara barely catches it, "And Sam."

    "Sam?" Kara prods, a cheeky grin spreading across her face. 

    "Aye, Sam."

    "Pray tell!"

    Alex sighs, running a hand through her short hair. "Before we departed she… She kissed me." 

    And she looks down at her hands, repeating softly, "She kissed me." As if she cannot believe it.

    "Any pain tomorrow may bring, I will gladly weather for love such as that which touches my life."

    "So you do this for Sam then?" Kara teases, and Alex shoots her a sharp look. "And for you, and for Orvai." 

    And Kara feels as though she's swallowing her words, even as she asks, "Do you love her?"

    "I do."

    "In what way?" She asks, keeping her face carefully neutral.

    "As a lover does, I suppose."

    "What is that… Like?" Kara asks with a vague gesture. She feels, more than sees the way her sister regards her, but she remains facing resolutely forward.

    "It is like nothing I have ever known before." 

    And she understands exactly what Alex has just said. She thinks of Lena, and feels that which she has never been able to name, and the last lie she holds before herself falls like autumn's last leaf.

    -

    Dawn- ever the alchemist, transmutes the silver fields to gold and the air to fire- a blaze that ignites the heavens in a gradient of color that inspires Kara's soul to cry out with the joy of the sight.

    Though so much has fallen to ugliness, there is still beauty to be found in this world.

    The rising sun peeks over the horizon, and as Kara stands in its brilliance, she feels a  peace in that which she bears lone witness to. 

    "Alex," she murmurs, "It's time."

    On the floor of the wagon Alex blinks awake, the anticipation of the day brushing aside any trace of drowsiness. For as long as Kara has known her, Alex has despised the mornings, finding more common ground with the creatures of the night, and it is with some surprise that she watches her sister rise without complaint. 

    Kara jumps to the ground scooping a little pile of the snow into a mound, building a tiny square atop it. Around the base of the hill she packs the snow down, leaving two raised rings encircling it, and when Alex joins her on the ground, she points at the tiny diorama of Argo.

    "Two granaries exist in the city- one here," she says, poking a spot on the west side of the inner ring, "that stores grain predominantly used to feed livestock."

    "And of course, the grains more suited to people are within the castle walls." Kara finishes, tapping the tiny square of snow.

    "Why could they not keep both in the outer ring?" Alex grumbles sourly. 

    "They kept it nearer to the center to protect it from siege."

    And Alex sighs, her fist pressed to her mouth as she thinks. 

    "First," she says, "we ought to go in ourselves. Better not to trap the cattle in the streets if there is reason to flee."

    So it is that they leave the wagons and cattle outside the city walls, and venture tentatively between the large gates of Argo. The city is as silent as the grave, elevating the crunch of their boots upon the snow to a maddening volume.

    The main road is dotted with frozen figures, and when Kara turns to look at Alex, she jumps back with a startled yelp.

    From the open window of a shop, a woman stares straight at her, standing like a pale statue- a monument to tragedy. She reminds Kara of the bundle of dry flowers hanging upside down on her wall back in Orvai, and for a moment, they just gaze at each other. 

    Her face is almost peaceful, her expression gentle, like that of a dreamer, or a person observing a sight at a vast distance.

    "Woah," Alex says as she follows Kara's line of sight. Heart heavy with the weight of it, Kara walks forward, driving her cleaver into the top of the woman's head, unable to look as the woman topples stiffly to the side, falling from view.

    The two exit the city, satisfied with their findings, and return with the wagons in tow, Alex leading the oxen forwards, and Kara, standing atop the wagon, her bow strung and at the ready.

    The city feels more like a mausoleum than a place meant to house the living, and as they pass still form after still form, they execute each, unwilling to risk leaving known enemies to their rear.

    Where are they all? Kara wonders, frowning. She spies only twelve in the road, and yet another handful down the streets they pass, but their numbers seem far too thin. 

    Perhaps they have all gathered at the far end of the city.

    Without delays or the traffic that once clogged the streets, they arrive at the inner ring within fifteen minutes, continuing up the main road to the castle. The hill steepens steadily, and the cattle huff with exertion as they climb on, dragging the heavy wagons up in a feat of sheer strength.

    They reach the caste within the hour, pulling up into the shadow of the tall walls with excitement and adrenaline. When they pass below the portcullis, Kara almost expects disaster in exchange for the ease of their passage, but the world remains still, as though rooting for their progress for the first time. 

    When they arrive at the castle's granary, Kara eyes the heavy iron padlock on the door, and with a grimace, realizes she has neither key nor axe with which to break in. 

    She can think of only one thing to do.

    Retrieving one of the shovels, she looks around guiltily, and Alex squints skeptically at Kara, flinching when she raises the tool, only to bring it down with a loud crash against the lock. 

    She looks around again, awaiting the appearance of the dead, and seeing nothing she raises the shovel again, slamming it down.  

    The sound of metal striking metal rings out once more, twice more, and yet a third time. Lips pulled back over her teeth in a soundless snarl, she brings the shovel down against the lock with one final huff, and the lock snaps open, the inner mechanism failing with a satisfying snap.

    Pleased, Kara yanks it from its fastening, lifting the heavy wooden beam that lies across the door, allowing it to swing open.

    And she almost tackles Alex in a hug, for joy and elation flood her at the sight of the large receptacles full of threshed grain.

    Here lies more than enough to atone for what has been lost. What bounty we have been graced with in this, our hour of need! 

    Quickly, they tie the cattle to a ring upon the granary wall, and from the wagons they retrieve first the barrels and crates, lining them up beside the large stone tubs of grain. 

    Together they shovel great heaping mounds of gold into the containers, and when they stand full and heavy, they heft the great wooden barrels and crates, walking them back towards the largest of the wagons. 

    Not for the first time, Kara is grateful Alex came with her, for the task is more than one man could easily accomplish in a day. They take one of the sacks of sacks, dumping the lot of bags out onto the floor, and once checked for holes, they fill each to the top with the loose grain that flows like liquid life.

    By midday, the sun is hot and high in the sky, melting the last stubborn inches of snow atop the hill, and the first wagon is piled ridiculously high with sack upon sack of grain. 

    From the second, smaller wagon, they pull one of the great canvas wagon covers, unfurling it over the top of the mound of packed grain. It strains slightly as they tie it securely over the huge mound of goods, only to shove even more sacks beneath its cover until it bulges out in an attempt to hold it all. 

    Though it looks woefully, tediously secured, the goods remain firmly in place, held together in some places by the sheer force of tension. 

    Three more long hours pass in a monotony of shoveling and hefting grain, and as Kara tosses a sack atop the second wagon, her peripheries catch on the whisper of a motion. 

    Her head whips around, jumping with surprise as her eyes catch on the face and form of a man, staring at her from around the corner. He moves towards her with a languid slowness, and at his presence, a thrill of fear rolls up Kara's spine.

    Our time is nearly up.

    "Alex," she hisses, pointing him out, and with a sidelong glance, Alex nods, picking up the pace with which she fills the sacks before her as Kara pulls the cleaver from her belt.

    The man falls with the same stiffness of winter in his bones, and when Kara peers around the corner, her stomach drops. Nearly twenty meander slowly toward them from the courtyard, and with so much at stake, she refuses to push their luck.

    In silent agreement, they quickly tie the second canvas overtop the back wagon, and just as the first damned souls round the corner, they untie the oxen, walking them quickly about.  

    From the height of the castle, the main road nearly resembles a slide, and as they walk before the cattle leading them onwards, they realize their mistake immediately. At the first sign of downward slope, the heavy wagon begins to roll forward, pressing the cattle to pick up their feet slightly, only to gain speed at an alarming rate.

    Oh. Oh no.

    Flinging an arm about the neck of the ox nearest to her in an attempt to slow the beast, she finds herself swept away as the oxen break out into a full run, snorting and grunting with terror as they plummet.

    Their hooves clatter against the uneven cobble, and had Kara been able to do much more than hold on, she would have cringed at the way the wagons smacked and jumped along behind them.

    A hoarse shout rings out, and her eyes briefly catch on Alex, whose arm is wrapped about the neck of the other ox in a similar fashion. Like little windsocks, Alex's feet fly out behind her as the cattle barrel down the road, and Kara screams at her own loss of control, giddy with terror as her body is flung hither and thither like a doll made of rags.

    Her feet seem barely to skim the ground as she kicks them out in a futile attempt to run beside the beasts, and with a flinch, they shoot past the inner wall.

    Though the angle of the hill lessens significantly, the wagon's momentum drives them on, and as the wind tears their screams from their throats, Kara and Alex hang on for dear life. 

    As best they can, they tuck their faces in towards the pumping shoulders of the great beasts, struggling to retain their grips as they slam fully into the still-standing bodies of the dead they had executed on their way up, smacking them aside like painful marble towers.

    And as quickly as it began, they start to lose momentum, their feet slapping painfully against the ground as they slow to a stop just outside of Argo's western gates.

    There they stand for a long, dazed moment, the four of them panting and staring straight out across the vast expanse of patchy snow, as if none can believe they are still alive.

    When she finally turns her head to look at Alex, a wild, shaky laugh bubbles up in her throat as shock gives way to the most simple release of panic she can muster. 

    The residual thrill of adrenaline courses wildly through her, and she lets out a great whoop even as Alex remains standing, her arms still locked about the cattle's neck. With a glazed expression, she says in a voice so distant it sounds as though her very soul is vacant,

    "We lived."

    -

    When a child yells that she has sighted two oxen pulling a great load from the north, Lena drops the pail of water in her hands, virtually sprinting to the field where Zil Eshs and Ehl Ahvrig play together, and with a fond greeting and a handful of oats, she begs passage. 

    Together they ride out, the thudding of their hooves jarring her teeth as they race towards the distant wagon.

    "Hail! Alex and Kara call, waving excitedly from where they walk beside the cattle, and as Lena approaches, the frown of reproach upon her face melts into sheer wonder.

    "Is that-?" She asks, barely able to hope.

    And Kara hands the lead to Alex, walking up to Zil Eshs with a wide smile that creases the corners of her eyes.

    "Aye, it is," she says simply, and she is so beautiful Lena forgets how to breathe.

    "Ride with me?" She asks, holding out a riding blanket to Kara who takes it gladly, turning to greet Ehl Ahvrig with a fond smile and a pat on the shoulder. The horse stoops slightly, offering her permission, and Kara vaults up onto her back.

    Together they fall back behind the slow oxen, riding side by side.

    "You," Lena says, her eyes fixed pointedly forward, "shall be the death of me." 

    She can practically feel the wide, innocent eyes Kara directs her way, but before she can speak, Lena continues flatly, "You left. You left without saying goodbye- a true goodbye." 

    Kara has the decency to deflate slightly at the accusation, and finally Lena looks at her.

    "Why did you not tell me of your venture? We could have found more people to go with you. What if you had gotten hurt? I could have helped you!" She says, the ineloquence of her speech compensated by the passion of her tone, rising with every word. 

    In the face of her distress, Kara fumbles slightly. "You work ceaselessly, every hour of the day, and I... Well. I Wanted to do something that would help.

    I wanted to limit the risk of involving others in this scheme should my hunch prove to be incorrect, but fortunately, Alex would not allow me to go alone."

    And as Kara speaks, Lena shakes her head furiously, her jaw clenching.

    She cannot possibly be this dense, she thinks, and a thousand vying concepts scramble her thoughts as she grapples desperately with her words.

    "Kara, I- You-" she starts, before sighing, deflating with defeat. 

    "I understand Kara, I truly do. And I appreciate what you have done for all of us- the both of you,"

    But do you not see the power you have to undo me? She feels like screaming. You could break me easier than a child with a toy.

    And that thought is enough to terrify her.

    "Do you even know how to fix a wagon wheel?" Is what flies from Lena's mouth as she frowns petulantly. With another wince, Kara slowly shakes her head. 

    "Does Alex?" 

    Again, Kara shakes her head, looking more than chastized.

    "And what if you had gotten stuck, in the middle of winter, all alone!"

    "In my defense, I did not go al-"

    Lena's sharp glare cuts the words from Kara's tongue, and she shuts her mouth with an audible click.

    And for the life of her, Lena cannot figure out why exactly she is so upset that Kara did not tell her. Did not take her. 

    She just….

    Kara- Kara's brave and foolish heart that she admires more than anything in this world seems to cause her endless grief. 

    But they have no time for true conversation, for as they enter into Orvai, the villagers in the streets cheer Kara and Alex on, following the heaping wagon to the granary, whose roof has been firmly patched. 

    Together they dismount, and before Kara can leave to help unload the wagons, Lena catches her wrist.

    "Why?"

    Is all she can ask, but she knows that Kara understands.

    Why risk your life for something so uncertain?

    She looks at Lena for a long, quiet moment, and there is a depth that shines in her eyes that Lena cannot fathom. It pulls her in, inviting her to try, and in a voice so soft it sends a shiver through Lena's chest, Kara says, "Krop osh, ripp osh." 

    Before she can ask exactly what that means, Kara steps closer, bowing her head slightly, and for a wild, aching moment Lena thinks Kara might kiss her. Really kiss her. 

    Her heart stops, and her eyes flutter closed as the warmth of Kara's breath washes over her, and when the soft press of Kara's lips lands upon her cheek, she cannot breathe.

    And Kara steps back, leaving the world to rush in on Lena once more as she turns to join the line of people gathering up sack after sack of grain, and all Lena can do is stare after her, her heart fluttering like a bird.

    Chapter End Notes

    Tbh I did so much research on medieval grain storage/harvesting (and by that I mean I skimmed like 3 links worth of information) and even more research on wagons/draft animals. And still somehow nothing in this chapter is accurate :')

    This chapter was so much fun to write though, and I am SO EXCITED FOR THE NEXT FEW.

    Enjoy~

    -Krop osh, ripp osh = for us, for you

    -Next: A thesis on leadership.

    The Divine Right to Rule

    Chapter Summary

    In which Winn does something deeply stressful.

    Chapter Notes

    Icy winds give way to a steady cold as the worst of winter passes, and the village of Orvai steps tentatively from its grave leaving but a single soul behind.

    Surrounded by family and neighbors, a man of many seasons fades like a whisper in the night. The breath within his chest quickens slightly, only to rattle thickly as he breathes out his last sigh, the depth of which severs the tether between flesh and life.  

    Like a gentle rain they weep, holding each other close as they sit with he who is no longer himself.

    And in their grief they find not a pure sorrow, nor a pure pain, but a mirror of beauty- of a love that remains long past the fading of fruit. In that awareness, they find such sweetness in having known love- in having cherished it; in having found that the bitterness of loss has always been a side to love hiding in plain sight.

    The shadow that offers man the relief of light.

    Yet, even as the tears run freshly down the faces of his grandchildren he stirs, sitting to regard them with a peculiar, dazed expression.

    Gasping with confusion and filled with wonder, they stare at him, knowing not why this feels so familiar. But then it dawns on everyone, and as they grab his arms and hold him to the bed, they know with absolute certainty.

    -

    "But how can this be?" Lena asks, shaking her head as she paces furiously round the table where James, Winn, Alex, Sam and Kara sit, roused from sleep when a young lad brought the news to their door.

    Rubbing the bleariness from their eyes, they turn doggedly to follow Lena's progress round the table as she continues ranting, "How could he have been damned without ever having been bitten- without having ever having seen battle against the damned?"

    The room falls silent, broken only by the fall of her foot upon the floor until Alex murmurs, "I have a thought…"

    And though Lena dreads what Alex is about to say, she is grateful. Grateful, for Alex seems never to shy away from the brutal truths of this world- truths she voices so her friends do not have to.

    "What if… What if the reaction is not constrained to contact, but to death? For this is the first of natural causes we have vied with, should death by tooth of the dead be counted as unnatural.

    Perhaps we all carry this infection within us, like a bear in the winter, only to emerge once the flesh is surrendered.

    We are certainly the only creature susceptible to the rising."

    And the same thought spirals through Lena's head over and over and over again, this time voiced by James in his stoic manner, his hands clenched into tense fists.

    "Then we are all damned."

    The statement falls like the lash of a whip, and for a moment Lena pauses in her pacing, eyes fixed on the ground before her.

    "Even so," she murmurs finally, "We still have our lives. Regardless of how they end, we must consider an existence beyond this nightmare.

    I propose," she says, turning to lay her palms flat upon the table, "That we chart several courses of action to meet the implications of this threat. We cannot oppose death at every turn, so we must determine set reactions so that all are prepared for such eventualities."

    Sam frowns, nodding in agreement as she asks, "What must be done? Say perchance a fellow perishes in his sleep. We cannot reasonably separate people from each other every night, we have not the means, nor the right."

    "Aye, we cannot. We must then provide a series of plans to meet the weaknesses that scenarios of that nature expose." Lena replies, frowning as she continues on her path.

    "We must ensure that the people understand the importance of containing the newly deceased to the room they turn in.

    Aside from the watch, we must also set a better system of warning so that all may know what dangers may lurk among us."

    "What think you of placing bells on posts at certain intervals throughout the streets?"
     Says James, ever practical.

    "Aye," nods Lena, "and perhaps we ought to place…"

    Winn bolts from the room, returning with a parchment and quill, scribbling furiously as they continue to throw idea after idea out.

    As they chew over failsafes, Lena wonders absently whether- if they all truly carry this disease, the bite of the living may begin the process of setting the flesh to fester.

    Like a devil called upon by man's best intentions, such a scenario occurs within three days when an infant bites his brother's finger hard enough to draw blood. The swelling and blackening of his finger begins that night, and with grim faces, J'onn holds the youth down as Alex amputates the digit.

    And it is a new terror, to suddenly fear the kiss of a lover, or the ignorance of a child, and for a time many fear the injury of their own tooth as it involuntarily closes upon the inner cheek.

    But despite fear, lovers take care with their kisses, and mothers still hold their babies to their breasts, and cheeks are bitten only to result in soreness.

    And the air fills with the thundering of hammers as bells are forged- many large bells that hang at the intersection to every street, with a reminder tacked below.

    Always close the door behind you.

    Above all property, save first your life.

    Warn your neighbor.

    -

    Spring comes with a sort of aching exhaustion- like the relaxation of a knotted muscle that has held onto tension for years.

    The world bursts with new life and the green shoots of growth in the fields breathes hope into all. By now, a symbiosis between guard and farmer has become muscle memory, and the excitement of the harvest of winter's late crops has every person in high spirits.

    The mistrust Lena was once accustomed to, clear in the eyes of many, has thawed into pleasant nods, and conversations in the street. One day, an older woman by the name of Eravhras catches her arm, pushing a pair of thick mittens into her hand, saying with a kind smile, "For next winter."

    Long after Eravhras walks away, Lena stares after her, the wool catching on the skin of her hands as she pulls them on, and they are more fine; more important than any garment she has ever worn.

    One peaceful night, Winn leans against her desk in the great hall, his arms crossed over his chest and his chin tucked in.

    The scratching of Lena's quill against the parchment is loud as she pours over the gestation reports of spring's first piglets.

    "I love Orvai," Winn says suddenly, startling her out of her thoughts. "I was born here; raised here; I have known these people since I entered this world. And for them, I would give anything.

    Anything." He whispers.

    "When I first accepted the role of steward, in the absence of Lady Catherine, I swore to myself if ever I was not the most suited to this position, I would offer it to one that was."

    Distantly aware of the shock coursing through her, Lena stares up at Winn, knowing exactly where he is heading, and completely incapable of fathoming it.

    And he stands, straight and regal- holds her in his iron gaze, and says clearly and without fear, "The time has come for me to humble myself to you.

    On behalf of the people of Orvai, I ask of you, Lena Luthor, to accept the position of Orvai's leader, and the responsibilities that entails."

    As his words echo within her head, Lena just stares at him, stunned.

    "Me?"

    "Aye," Winn nods, and his face shows no hint of a jest.

    Could I? She wonders. Should I? What right have I to accept leadership over these people?

    And she hears the sonorous voice of her father, ringing out, "The divine right to rule flows swiftly through your veins, Alexander. Our blood- the Luthor blood, affords us this sacred right and responsibility."

    "I cannot," she says, biting the words out, and she feels a pang of guilt as Winn's face drops.

    "For why?" He asks gently, his brow furrowed with confusion.

    "For… For I have come to understand that every comfort of my life has come about by the hands of these people- a people who never chose to give of that labor to me, and my family. And beyond the theft of their hands, there exists the price paid by those who have fallen," she says bitterly. "By the hand of my family, be that directly or indirectly.

    My family, who claimed the right to decide the fates of men they had never laid eyes upon. My family, who by that entitlement, sapped the riches of the land and left its people to perish in their wake.

    For playing party to this, however ignorantly, I am forever indebted to our people. I have been long before I met them, and it would be a vile continuation of my family's legacy for me to accept this position, and in doing so, to decide myself their leader."

    Winn looks at her for one long, quiet moment, as the silence fades into thoughtful contemplation.

    "Well said, Lena. You would have made an excellent leader."

    And at his words a deep and abiding gratitude wells up within her, for the work of her hands brings her pride for the first time in her life, and in the giving of herself, she has found a life worth living.

    -

    One Month Later

    It is rare that the guard is short, but it is a simple eventuality. Though Lena knows she ought to be at the meeting Orvai holds, she understands it to be a tedious recital of the logistics and numbers already firmly rooted within her head, so with a guilty excuse, she allows Winn to shoulder the speeches, following the farmers to their fields before dawn.

    As she observes the tilling of the soil, she thinks again of every meal that had been laid out upon the grand table of her youth- of the labor that went into every bite, and she feels once more ashamed.

    But such shame, she finds, comes with an unerring gratitude for her new understanding of the world, and for her new knowledge of these laborious, essential tasks.

    By the time they lay down their work and return to Orvai, the sun is high in the sky.

    Confusion settles over Lena, for it appears that every man, woman and child remain gathered in the streets, the crowd thickening towards the center of the village.

    "She returns!" Bellows out a voice, and she winces as all eyes turn towards her. Just above the heads of the crowd, she sees Winn, standing on a small wooden stage, his finger pointing directly at her.

    The crowd thins before her, and with a sense of unreality, a path parts to the center.

    What are you doing Winn?

    She steps forward, passing between the tall rows of people, feeling awkward amongst them for the first time in months. An odd formality hangs in the air, and when she steps onto the stage to stand before Winn, she feels more nervous than she has ever felt before.

    And she turns to face the gathered crowd which is a mistake, for the number of eyes suddenly trained upon her makes her knees weak.

    But then she sees Kara in the crowd, smiling brilliantly, and when she realizes Lena has seen her, her hand shoots up, waving wildly.

    "Friends, neighbors," Winn says loudly, and Lena startles slightly at the sound.

    "Earlier this day I put this question to you: Were I to relinquish my post as your leader to any other, to whom would you see it go?"

    And it is more surreal than anything that has ever happened in her young life when the sound of her name echoes from the throats of over two hundred people.

    Winn turns to her then- takes her hands in his and holds her eyes with his steady gaze.

    "Lena," he says, in an almost quiet voice. "If you would accept this post by the will of our people, I will gladly relinquish it to you.

    "But you are a fine leader, Winn!" She protests, her voice shaking as involuntary tremors run up through her chest. "Would they not still be perfectly happy if you remained their leader?"

    "Would you ask them to choose?" He asks, wincing slightly.

    "I… What if they would rather have you?"

    "They shall have both of us. Think of this not as an abandonment, but as a shifting of roles between the two of us, for I will gladly serve at your right hand if you would have me."

    And Lena turns to face the crowd.

    -

    The days pass by in a blur of work- of settling dispute after petty dispute; of organizing the guards shifts; of monitoring the ever shifting rations, and ensuring the tradesmen have the resources to ply their crafts.

    Every waking moment the weight of the needs of the people lies heavy on Lena's shoulders and mind, for she is by now, well aware of the consequences of miscalculation. It is with great relief that she acknowledges the severity of such consequences have lessened slightly without the bite of winter to aid them.

    But late one night the sound of an iron bell clangs loudly through the streets, followed by the sound of a shrill scream that fades to silence. Lena sits bolt upright at her desk, almost unsure if she had heard correctly, yet utterly, terribly certain.

    Her hand flies to her hip, but it is empty, and cursing, she remembers she had left her sword at Winn's house, having been prepared for a long quiet night of paperwork.

    Slowly, tentatively she peeks out of the giant doors of the town hall, scanning for danger in the stillness. Seeing none, she runs from the doors to the shadow of the nearest building, peering down the darkness of the streets in the direction of the sound.

    And from the darkness, Erish charges Lena, knocking her bodily to the ground. Her head smacks against the cobble below, and her vision goes white for just a moment.

    Her body is moving- scrabbling, at the cobble as she kicks at his hands. His sweet smile has twisted into a sorrowful grimace, and his once gentle hands grab at her legs, pulling himself nearer to her.

    She screams, slamming her heel into his nose as he dives to sink his teeth into her calf, and his head jerks back. Out of nowhere, a thin ribbon of shining steel joins him from forehead to back, and heart pounding, Lena watches him go limp, even as she frantically frees herself from his grasp.

    A hand enters her vision, and she looks up to see James, his arm outstretched towards her. Arms trembling, she flings her hand into his and he yanks her to her feet, raising his sword as he beckons her in the direction of the nearest house.

    She shakes her head, and he frowns at her, flicking his blade in a little circle that seems to spell out, Why?

    "The bell rang from the East," she says, pointing. "We must go towards it, for there lies the head of this serpent."

    Setting his jaw firmly, James nods, yanking a dagger from his belt. He holds it out towards Lena and together they race eastward until they arrive at a crossroads where nearly six armed guards stand in a defensive circle.  

    Flickering torch light illuminates their center, and when Lena and James approach, the guard parts to let them through.

    The sight that greets her takes her breath away.

    A tall man wearing the colors of the house of Luthor lies face down in the dirt, an all too familiar cleaver buried in his skull. Beside him rises the post upon which is mounted a bloodied iron bell, and at the foot of that post kneels Kara, holding the head of a youth in her lap.

    His stomach is a bloody mess, and his face is pale for lack of blood. The air seems to shake as his mother bends and moans with the grief known only to those who have buried their children, and the low keening sound sends chills down Lena's spine.

    The woman reaches for her son's small hand; presses it to her lips, to her cheek, as though she could warm it with her love.

    Kara looks at nothing but the boy's face, one hand cupped to his warm cheek, and the other wrapped around the cold steel of a dagger. The point rests delicately at his temple, and when he blinks his brown eyes open like a fawn fresh to this world, the blade consummates its purpose.

    Lena flinches as his mother screams out, almost as though her son has been slain again before her very eyes, and the sharpness of it wounds her heart.

    Gently, as though he might break, Kara picks the boy up within her arms, his blood soaking into her shirt as his mother takes his hand once more. Lena's heart feels like a stone within her chest as she yanks the cleaver from the man's skull, walking on as the ring of guards begins to move, the procession continuing down the street to the town hall.

    There, behind the safety of closed doors, they lay down his small body, leaving his mother to hold him one last time.

    At the town center, they ring the great bell, signaling the guard whilst warning all others to remain within their houses. The tolling continues on and on, and in the waiting, Lena holds the cleaver out towards Kara.

    She turns away, a look of mingled horror, disgust, and sadness crossing her face at the sight of it, and Lena catches her face in her hand, cooing "Hey, hey, maybe you ought to go inside, brave heart. We can do this without you."

    And Kara's gaze snaps to her with brutal force, and it feels like the wind has been knocked from her chest.

    "I am staying with you." She grits out, her voice trembling as she speaks, and with a shaking hand she curls her fist around the handle of her cleaver, the blade flashing as it trembles.

    A feeling of worry seeds low in Lena's gut, but she nods, turning to stand beside Kara. They have not the time to indulge in worry or terror, for the shuffling, stumbling footsteps of the dead gives birth to a river of terror.

    Lena shudders at the sight, and though she wishes more than anything that she had her sword, she squares her shoulders, facing the road.

    The first woman that nears her is wiry and insubstantial, and with a brush of Lena's arm, she sweeps the woman's grabbing hands aside, burying her dagger into the crown of her head.

    Beside her, Kara slashes almost wildly at a man, nearly severing his hand before swinging her cleaver into his head with so much force it splits his skull to the nose. Her eyes are alight with something manic, and as she fights with single-minded purpose, Lena dances around her, shielding her from the hands she does not see.

    Behind them, James strikes like a serpent, coiling to deliver a singular powerful blow before retreating again, crouched low to the ground. Lena fells her fourth as Kara slays her seventh, and from the approaching crowd she spies the familiar face of Nia,.

    No…

    At first, Lena is struck with disbelief and shock until Nia leaps between the oncoming people, her whip-like sword whistling through the air with a high-pitched ssswip.

    And more familiar faces replace those that are rotting, until all those who guard Orvai stand facing each other amidst the sea of dead. When they line the bodies up, they count a total of forty-three bodies to pile into a greasy, tragic pyre far in the distant fields.

    None amongst the dead but Erish are claimed, and when the day passes from dawn to noon to evening, the gruesome and mundane labors of the day come to a close, and the village gathers round two carefully stacked pyres that send their stars high up into the heavens.

    -

    The blur of sleeplessness lends an almost drunken cast to the world as Kara and Lena enter the house. Kara says not a word to her, falling into the nearest chair with slumped shoulders, leaning her elbows on her knees as she stares sightlessly at the floor.

    Unsure, Lena stands back, pulling anxiously at her fingers.

    "I… I am so very sorry, Kara."

    "Why?" Kara asks in a dull, flat tone.

    "For what you had to do. For… For the man- he.. He was very clearly a servant of my family, and I-"

    Slowly Kara lifts her head, fixing Lena with a burning stare, raw with grief.

    "I was on watch."

    "What?"

    "I was on watch, over the forest."

    And Lena's blood runs cold as sudden understanding washes through her.

    "He-" Kara says, choking on the word as she gestures helplessly in the air. "His mother said he was out to relieve himself, and… And he rang the bell. He rang the bell for all of us, to account for my mistake.

    His blood shall stain my hands forever."

    The torment of that statement wrings at Lena, and she shakes her head, kneeling before Kara.

    "Love," she whispers, brushing the tears from Kara's cheeks. "You are not a god. Mistakes are never intended, and ill fate considers no man's plans."

    Kara just shakes her head, crumbling in on herself, her shoulders shaking violently. And Lena stands, retrieves a basin of clear water and carries it to the table, setting it gently upon the wooden surface.

    She takes Kara's hands, still marred by the dried blood that had seeped into the cracks of her skin and nails. She takes Kara's hands between her own and lowers them into the water, gently rubbing at her hands and nails until they are bloodless once more.

    And she leads Kara into the bed she shares with Sam, with a request that Sam might share Alex's bed for the night, to which her friend responds with evident delight.

    Lena sits on the bed with Kara, pulling her blond head into her lap, running her fingers through the soft warm hairs as Kara's tears soak into the fabric of her tunic.

    -

    Pale dawn streams through the window, harsh and grey with grief and a numb quality that seems to revel in the memory of the past two days.

    Dark rings encircle Lena's eyes as she gazes at Kara's face, peaceful for once as she slumbers. And Lena feels such an aching grief for her- feels such fear for what is to come.

    With a deep sigh, she turns her head from side to side, stretching the stiffness from her neck as her restless mind turns the problem back and forth like a foreign object to be understood.

    What of the next time? She thinks. Orvai as it stands is terribly vulnerable. Yet any permanent method of protecting it and the surrounding lands with the resources we have would be shoddy and impractical.

    If numbers greater than these come upon us, then Orvai may yet be taken, or should similar instances occur within the fields, the farm guard will surely find itself overwhelmed.

    Then what?

    And she shudders as she recalls the number of dead that had flooded into the streets- past peoples doors and windows and into the shadowed corners by their houses. She recalls the face of the boy, brave and still, and knows she cannot allow things to continue on in this manner.

    We need the strength of a wall, a voice whispers in the back of her head for the thousandth time.

    The problem with the construction of a wall arises due to the odd sprawling layout of Orvai, and the makeshift houses that are beginning to dot the outskirts for the newer residents, foretelling of necessary expansions. Its farms and houses exist in a peculiar spread, and a protective wall whose integrity would truly last would require many seasons of resources and building.

    And then a thought springs into her head.

    A wild, improbable thought, and for a moment Lena just stares at Kara, wondering if somehow she had planted the thought.

    She knows of a place with great walls; a place with towering gates, and enough rooms and beds for all who live in Orvai.

    Kara blinks awake, her eyes bright as they meet Lena's, only for the spark within to dull, fading into blankness.

    "Goodmorning, brave heart," Lena murmurs, stroking Kara's hair from her temple.

    Kara dips her chin, turning her face into the blanket even as she takes Lena's hand in hers, her fingers rough and trembling.

    "I have been thinking all night," Lena says quietly, and Kara turns her face back up to look at her.

    "I have decided that I am going to gather the village of Orvai," she says, and the surety of her voice surprises her. "I am going to put to a vote that we move to Argo."

    Kara's jaw drops, and for a moment, a look of pure disbelief consumes her face. Lena has never seen this particular expression on her face before, and in the silence her thoughts tumblr from her mouth in a waterfall.

    "Orvai has grown too many for the houses to catch up. As it remains, she is not defendable should we face any greater host. A wall around her would be impractical, but Argo…

    Say we were to capture a portion of the lower ring- we would have a wall to two sides of us, and the rest would nearly be walled off by buildings, between which, barriers might easily be erected."

    As she considers the plot, Kara's face melts from shock to wonder to approval. "Many squandered resources lie within the city walls," she muses, her voice raw with crying. "It could surely house us, and house us well.

    When Alex and I passed through the western gate, there were seldom souls in sight. Perhaps they had gathered to the east, or perhaps they dispersed amongst the wilds to wreak their havoc.

    Regardless, to take a portion of the city might just be doable."

    And despite her lack of sleep, a surge of energy floods Lena. "Would you, and all those who once lived in Argo gather to draw a map of the city? Its rough dimensions, and the placement of her streets to the best of your memories?"

    Kara sits upright, pressing a fist to her head with a wince before nodding.

    "Aye."

    -

    Three hundred whispering voices fill the air as the citizens of Orvai mill about the village center. Lena stands on the stage, her entire stomach trembling as she prepares herself to speak.

    But then she looks into the eyes of her friends, her neighbors- the people she has labored alongside; fought alongside; sworn her life to, and a great courage stills her limbs.

    "Friends," she says, her voice ringing out with surprising clarity. "Our days have indeed been touched by tragedy- a fate I fear is simply a warning; a taste of what may yet come.

    Should another host greater than this attempt to breach Orvai our streets shall be taken, and our crops trampled."

    Many of the villagers stare down or away, refusing to look at her as she speaks. Many seem displeased, but most are despairing.

    "We need protection," Lena says. "Strong walls that we may rely upon to guard us long past the time our children's children are well seasoned."

    And she takes a deep breath, all eyes having returned to hers, and she feels as though she is jumping from a cliff.

    "I believe we must seek sanctuary in Argo."

    A ripple of surprise becomes a roar of protest, and she waits patiently for the shouting to die down.

    "I know I may never understand the bond you have with this land. For many of you, it encompasses your entire life- every joy and sorrow; every precious memory; Orvai has served you well.

    The strength of her lies not only within her houses and lands, but also within the hearts of her people- you are the blood of this land, for without you, what is your house but a house?

    Your home lies within your fellow man; within yourself, and it is that which I seek to protect at all costs, for your life is the most valuable thing you may possess in this world."

    She pulls then from her belt a square of folded parchment, unfolding it to hold between her widely outstretched arms. "I asked those who once hailed from Argo to assist me in the construction of a map- a map of Argo's streets and walls.

    If we were to take a portion from here-"

    Lena says, tapping a spot in the outer ring north of the western gates, "to here," she says, touching a spot towards the southern wall, "we would have enough houses for every man, woman and child of orvai.

    Good, strong houses, already outfitted with beds and many supplies.

    And walls," she says, tracing the inner and outer ring, "to either side of us.

    We could capture the city in increments, for the houses too provide the structure for moveable walls.

    In a month's time, when the fields have been harvested, I move we hold a vote. A vote to decide the fate of Orvai, be it to remain or to capture the city."

    The silence is deafening.

    Lena bites her lip nervously, glancing from face to turbulent face, when she spots Winn in the crowd. He holds her gaze, and nods his encouragement.

    "Thank you."

    Without even a mumbled word, all disperse, seeming for the world as though they are fleeing, and Lena's heart drops as she sits at the edge of the stage, staring sightlessly at the map between her hands.

    "I see why she likes you." Winn's voice cuts in as he steps before the stage to stand by her.

    "What?" Lena says, her head snapping up.

    "You have the same bold streak of madness within you."

    And she knows instantly who he speaks of.

    "I- Winn, I fear they may never forgive me for this. What right have I to ask them to leave? To leave everything they have ever known of life? And what better options lie before us? How ought we avoid the traps of life?"

    And Winn folds his arms, tucking his chin against his chest as he thinks for a moment.

    "Though many may be angry, or hurt, all know you, Lena. We chose you, for we see you have our best interests within your heart. And we believe in that- trust in that, even if the choice you have laid before us is more difficult than the choosing between which hand to sacrifice.

    But know this," he says, catching her eyes. "I will follow you to the end."

    And Lena has to put the parchment down because tears are falling from the corners of her eyes. She stands and wraps Winn in a hug, whispering by his neck, "Thank you,

    Thank you."

    -

    The month passes with the silent tension coiled within a predator's haunches as it prepares to spring. As the wheat matures, it coils ever tighter and though the villagers remain polite, there is a guardedness to them that gives Lena pause.

    She is filled with questions that only action may offer the answers to, and though she wants to trust in her own hands, she remains unsure.

    It is in this unsurity that Kara finds her, sitting on a bench outside of the town hall, staring out into the night sky. She sits beside Lena, pulling her knees to her chest.

    Without looking over, Lena whispers, "In my whole life, I never dreamed that anyone would ever follow me, willingly or not."

    Im her periphery, Kara faces her, but Lena keeps her gaze firmly fixed upon the constellations.

    "Leadership was only ever going to be something I fell subservient to," she says, "but now everyone relies upon me, and I am… Terrified."

    She laughs then, looking at the houses around her.

    "I am terrified that I shall fail these people. That when tragedy strikes as it inevitably will, that I won't know what to do, or…. Or I will do the wrong thing.

    That even now I am doing the wrong thing.

    But there is nowhere safe in this world for us, Kara," she says in a small broken voice, and then a choked laugh breaks free from her.

    "If my father could see me now, he would tell me I am here because the divine right to rule flows in my veins," she says bitterly, and Kara stiffens slightly beside her.

    "My family always spoke of the Luthor blood, and how it set us above people."

    As she speaks, she feels the untruth of the notion within her heart.

    "But I understand more than anything now, that blood without the willingness to sacrifice it, can never earn the trust of a people. There exists no such thing as a right to govern people's lives for them- only the privilege to vow that I will take care of them to the best of my abilities, or that I shall die trying."

    And as she says that, Kara regards her clearly, remaining silent for a long moment.

    "You know," she says with such kindness in her voice it could make Lena weep, and she wraps an arm around her shoulders, pulling her close. "That though you have been chosen to speak for us, you do not bear the burden of our collective fates.

    We all stand beside you upon level ground. We see what you do; we see your heart, and we defer to you because we trust your guidance, but these decisions are never ones you must make alone. Nor should they be. The burden of responsibility; of consequence weighs on every person's head.

    El Mayarah, Lena."

    And it's like a crushing weight has lifted from Lena's shoulders.

    "El Mayarah." She echoes quietly.

    And Kara looks as though she is about to tell Lena something, but instead she turns her face to the heavens, and together they watch as the stars wheel in the sky.


    Chapter End Notes

    -WE ARE ABOUT TO GET WILD, BUCKLE IN EVERYONE

    As always, thank you for reading, and let me know what you think of this story!

    Next: I am the light to your shadow, and the shadow of your light.

    Birthright

    Chapter Summary

    "One day we reading were for our delight
    Of Lancelot, how Love did him enthrall.
    Alone we were and without any fear.
    Full many a time our eyes together drew
    That reading, and drove the color from our faces;
    But one point only was it that o'ercame us.
    When as we read of the much-longed-for smile,
    Being by such a noble lover kissed,
    This one, who ne'er from me shall be divided..."

    -Inferno, Canto V.
    Dante

    Chapter Notes

    The sonorous blow of the horn vibrates the very fabric of the air, marking the beginning of the siege of Argo. A warm breeze carries the distant scent of rot and the sweat of people and horses alike, wafting like a cloud over the gathered villagers.

    They stand in nervous rows of threes before Argo's western gate, fidgeting, checking their weapons, and glancing at the tall stone walls with trepidation as the hour crawls by.

    In the center line, the cart-bearing horses snort and toss their heads, their ears turned back towards their drivers as the baritone pitch sounds from Argo's eastern wall, echoing far out beyond the western plains.

    The men assigned to blow the horns are distant, having climbed tall ladders to brave the wall walk together. As she listens to their calls, Kara's hand flexes around her cleaver, honed and gleaming.

    Every few minutes she glances over at her team, their nervous energy serving as a mirror to her own, and only once does she allow herself to glance over her shoulder to look at Lena.

    For the hundredth futile time she wishes they had been assigned to the same team, and as she tries to force the worry from her mind, she can almost feel the tension of the crowd rising like a panic within her chest.

    Finally, with an almost dreamlike quality, James thrusts his sword into the air cutting loose every fear to fall into the pool of adrenaline. The length of light he clutches in his hand flashes like a sign from the gods as he lowers his arm towards the gate.

    And the crowd steps forward as one, their footsteps muffled with rags even as the cart's wheels strike noisily against the cobble.

    A wave of anxiety washes over Kara as Lena disappears around a corner, but she has no time to think on it, for she, Querl, and a young woman named Urishesh are suddenly hurtling straight up the main road.

    Dark alleys and streets pass by in a blur, and panting with exertion, they soon stand just inside Argo's inner wall. Every street around is empty, save for one straggler who steps from his house like a man just risen from sleep.

    Kara raises her cleaver, the blade dazzling in the sun, and bids the man return to his eternal slumbers.

    Two towers cast long shadows past the inner walls, and when they run beneath them, Querl splits off towards the right, and Kara to the left. Bow drawn, Urishesh leaps into the back of the loaded cart, pivoting to stare down each street.  

    Kara tears open the door only to jump back with a quiet curse as a man stumbles towards her, casting her in the darkness of his shadow as they stagger into the sun.

    The colors of the house of Luthor adorn his breastplate, and his metal shoes scrape loudly on the ground making them scream and spark.

    A loud metallic clang echoes out from beside them, and as the man lifts his head towards it, a bouquet of feathers sprouts from his right eye.

    He drops with a loud crash, and Kara, eyes wide, turns to Urishesh with a nod of thanks, resuming her mad sprint to the door.

    Steeling herself, she tears open the door once more, holding her cleaver before her like a shield. The room is still and empty, and before courage can desert her, she launches herself up the stairs, stumbling only once to catch herself upon her hands.

    Without pause she scrambles up to the top and into a small cramped room, wherein stands a large wooden mechanism. Beside it, a man stares dolefully at her through thin pale eyes, reaching for her with a weak groan.

    A shining helm adorns his brow, cradling his sunken cheeks, and from his gut protrudes a length of rusted steel that pins him solidly to the wall.

    Frowning at the large wheels and levers, Kara skirts him warily, and just as she is about to reach for the wheel a great BOOM shudders the building.

    As she flinches, turning towards the open window behind her, the man takes three wobbling steps forward. The decay of his flesh allows him to push past the hilt of the sword with an oddly dry tearing sound, and with a yell Kara leaps back, slashing instinctually towards his head as he descends on her shoulder.

    The shock of the strike reverberates from his helm down her arm, rendering it numb with pain, and she curses, clutching her forearm as she stumbles back behind the great wheel.

    This is hardly fair, she thinks, pouting at the dent in his helm, and then down at the flattened edge of her blade. The thick length of rope wound about a large wooden spool catches her eye, and with a great yell she throws her arm out, slashing at it.

    The rope snaps, and the wheel of the contraption spins like a lathe, its spokes lashing out to slam into the man, knocking his head clean off.

    The same BOOM sounds from below as the second portcullis slams firmly into the ground, shaking the tower with the force of it, and with a great heaving breath, Kara wipes the sweat from her brow.

    She runs down the stairs towards the door that faces the outer ring, grunting as she lifts the heavy wooden beam.

    Nearly fifty dead swarm outside, and she slams the door closed with a yelp, fear lending her strength as she slams the wooden beam back into place.

    And then she hears a scream. A familiar scream that rings out to the left of the tower, and with a sinking feeling, Kara sprints up the stairs once more, stumbling forward to squint out the window.

    Below, the street holds no sign of Urishesh, the horse, or the cart, and in their place nearly fifty of the damned mill about. Several paw at the door she had so recently peeked out of, but she pays them no mind, instead scanning between the houses, her eyes flicking from alley to street until she spots Lena.

    She dances alone in a courtyard, panicked grace lending her a deadly edge as the damned pour ceaselessly from the entrance before her.

    And Kara knows there are too many- wants to scream at the damned that swarm the street below her, and in a fit of frustration she pushes away from the window, pacing wildly as she searches for anything that might prove useful.

    Finding nothing, she leans far out the window, and as she swings her head first left and then right, the sliver of a rooftop's corner peeks out from around the tower's edge.

    Brow furrowed and jaw set, Kara glares at the distance, refusing to look at the ground.

    I can make that, she thinks with far more confidence than she feels.

    "Querl!" She shouts, desperate with hope as her voice fades into the open sky.

    "Aye!" Comes the distant reply, and Kara breathes a sigh of relief.

    "Are you safe?"

    "Aye!"

    Good.

    "Querl the ground below is taken, and Urishesh has fled. Remain as long as you can, I will return for you!"

    "What of you?"

    And she sheathes her bloody cleaver with a silent apology to the tanner, stepping up onto the ledge of the window.

    "I go to assist Lena!"

    The wind seems louder from this height, and butterflies race through her from crown to heel. Her fingers are tight with nervous energy as she grabs onto the edge of the window, her every muscle trembling as she crouches, measuring the distance through narrowed eyes.

    I'm crazy. I'm crazy I'm crazy I'm-

    Before she can convince herself otherwise, she springs forward into empty space, anchored only by her hand upon the window's ledge.

    And when she lets go it's like flying- exhilarating, terrifying, and unbelievable. A giddy laugh has just enough time to bubble up from her throat before her chest is slamming down onto the rooftop, legs swinging wildly as she clutches at the roof.

    For a moment Kara cannot draw breath, and the feeling is enough to send panic racing through her. It is as if her lungs have forgotten how to expand, and she stills with nausea, tears leaking from the corners of her eyes as her stomach shakes with the pain.

    In a rush, blessed air floods her lungs, and though her head aches with the impact of her landing, she forces herself to pull her legs up, shaking her head as she staggers to her feet.

    Once the world begins to spin at a slower rotation, Kara takes off towards the courtyard, leaping from rooftop to rooftop, and above her, Querl watches on, letting out a low whistle.

    -

    The sun blazes fiercely above- a distant vouyer of the battle between tragedy and hope, and beneath its watchful gaze, Lena stands alone.

    Her team has fallen. Her team has fallen and she has failed them, and their valiant faces stare mockingly up at her, willing her to acknowledge their sound defeat.

    But she cannot look- cannot even weep, for even as their limbs and lungs sate the starving masses, the crowd pushes in, staunched only by the tourniquet of the courtyard entrance through which they flood.

    The street beyond is so thickly packed with jostling bodies that it appears a river of confused and apathetic faces. They pour through the gap only to fall to the merciless sweep of Lena's arm, and she dances back and forth until her heel is pressed against a wall.  

    Teeth bared in a snarl, she squares her shoulders, seeking to become the embodiment of ruthless efficiency as she stabs and slashes in a convoluted web of action and reaction.

    Though her aim holds true, her arm is unaccustomed to the prolonged demands of a siege, tiring quickly, and at the weakening of her flesh they begin to press in, crowding around her until they rise up in a terrifying wall of hunger.

    Panting, Lena tries to still her mind- to let go of all things: every worry and joy, in pursuit of deadly peace as James had once advised her to do over his own blade.

    But all she can think of is Kara.

    And though it is a distraction from her present, the thought offers her life- a tangible reality to hold, even as the strength of her limbs fail and the dead press in around her, their fingers pulling at the leather of her chestplate, greaves and bracers.

    Teeth close upon the tough material in several places, even as she hacks at the hands and faces closing in on her. And the terror within her stills to an almost peaceful acceptance as-

    A hoarse roar sounds overhead, and like a mighty eagle Kara drops from the sky, eyes bright with rage as she ruthlessly swings her cleaver, hacking at those gathered round Lena as though they were overgrown brambles.

    Old, black blood splatters the pavement, and without missing a beat, Kara ducks below the singing arc of Lena's sword as she raises her arm to clear the air before them.

    When Kara moves to press her back against hers, Lena feels safe for the first time since parting ways at the city gate.

    Together they face the crowd, Kara darting forward dealing quick decisive blows only to jump back as the long blade of the sword reaches past her. Once, Lena sees the wide flash of a cleaver embedded in the neck of a tall man, and she drives her blade up through his jaw without hesitation.

    Immediately leaping forward, Kara yanks the knife from his neck, even as Lena thrusts the point of her sword into the snarled face of a woman intent on falling upon Kara's exposed back.

    By the end of ten minutes, roughly forty fallen bodies provide all with treacherous footing, and even as the crowd thins, stumbling and crawling over their fallen companions, Lena steps on the soft neck of a young girl.

    Her skin, slick with black blood, slides beneath the sole of Lena's boot, and with a shout she falls, eyes wide as a man immediately topples onto her.

    He writhes against her, his teeth snapping at her exposed neck as he strains to crawl farther up her body, and as she cries out as the unique scent of his rot floods her nose.

    In a daze, she stares at his suddenly limp form, barely able to fathom the cleaver being pulled from his skull just inches from her face. As she lies there, Kara lashes out, her arm fanning in a blur across Lena's vision before she kicks out, her boot colliding with the man that pins Lena to the ground.

    He rolls from her, and without any time to adjust Kara grabs her beneath the elbow, yanking her solidly to her feet.

    Lena dives for her sword, pressing her back once more against Kara's, and patiently they wait for the remaining dead to approach, breathing heavily in the reprieve as their assailants stumble across the mass grave.

    When the last woman falls to her blade, Lena's arm drops to her side as though it is made of lead, but she does not relinquish her sword.

    It weighs upon her shoulder, and when she turns to Kara, she is reminded of the first time they truly met. They face each other, bloodied and haggard, and that same noble fire burns in the depths of Kara's eyes.

    Staggering forward, Lena can barely will her trembling limbs to obey her as she throws her free arm around Kara's shoulders, pulling her close in a firm hug.

    "Thank you," she whispers, willing Kara to understand every emotion, thought and memory that lies behind the word. And Kara tucks her bloodied face against Lena's neck, holding her tightly as though she is a boulder in a turbulent river.

    "Where is your patrol?" Lena asks in a voice small with fear for the answer.

    "Querl is likely still trapped in the tower of the inner wall. As for Urishesh, I know not. Both she, the horse, and the cart were nowhere to be seen when I fled the portcullis. My gamble lies with her having searched out the nearest team.

    What of yours?"

    And terribly Lena returns to the world, her lips trembling slightly as she points to three bloody piles beside the large cart.

    With a look of great sorrow and understanding, Kara pulls her forward, and as she had nearly a year ago, she tucks Lena into her arm, making a shield of herself. This time Lena allows it, tearing her eyes from those she once called her friends.

    They return to the main street, the deep bass of the horns accented by the scattered rhythm of hammers against nails. Many teams of two stand in the main road facing James, and when Kara and Lena walk up, he greets them with a wave.

    At their somber expressions, his face hardens, and he asks, "The portcullis?"

    "Secured," Kara breathes. "Querl is safe, but I know not where Urishesh fled to."

    With a nod, James points towards a row of houses to the south. "She believed you two to be lost to the towers for the remainder of the siege, and joined the efforts to clear the southwestern quadrant."

    "And the North?" He asks quietly, and Lena suspects he already knows.

    "It has yet to be secured. We need another horse."

    James nods, whistling to a team of three men, approaching from farther up the main road. Quickly he points towards a row of single-story buildings, handing Kara a small pouch of crushed charcoal, ordering, "Clear that row of houses. This team will take their horse to patch the breach."

    A bitter flavor spreads across Lena's tongue as she nods, and so it is that she and Kara pass by many streets and alleys, appraising the tall stacks of barrels, crates and wagons loaded with stones.

    Between several houses, tall wooden walls stand braced between the buildings as above them, people point past their boundary with drawn bows.

    When they approach the first house, Kara moves to grab the door's handle, only to freeze when Lena hisses through her teeth, shaking her head. At Kara's questioning look, Lena's eyes flick towards the dark windows, and raising a fist, she raps on the glass three times.

    A man and a woman stagger up, and when they are the only two to approach, Lena nods to Kara, who swings the door open.

    When the man stumbles out, Lena catches him through his crown, and the woman follows behind him shortly, entering into the light of day like a new babe. She stares at the sun with wonder, and as she lets out a soft gasp of amazement, it dies in her throat as the cleaver descends upon her head.

    When every door hangs open and every crevice lies exposed, they mark the door with a thick charcoal dash, continuing on down the row until their assigned lot stands clear.

    By the time the sun crests its highest point, every captured house and building bears the mark of emptiness, and the gruesome chores begin. Carts that once held heavy barrels and small wooden walls now tower high with the bodies of the fallen.

    Many gasps arise when they take in the numbers of the fallen that litter the courtyard, and when Lena points out the two that used to be their own, a somber quiet falls upon the group.

    Their weak and barely moving corpses meet a gentle mercy upon the tip of a dagger, and they are swaddled within pale linen sheets as gentle as the last time their mothers held them.

    Cruel fate seems to be laughing at Lena this dark day, for her team is the only team to suffer true casualty. And in the days to come when she faces one of the men's grieving families, little in her young life has prepared her for the way his mother screams as she crumples to her knees.

    "How was it that my Jharhen fell?" She sobs, and the silent question that curls beneath seems to ask, And how were you the only one to survive?

    As Lena speaks, the woman's face falls to shadow, and she mutters darkly, "You… Curse you for ever raising this hair brained scheme for glory. Curse you for another Luthor who prevails, even as the blood of our innocent pools at your feet.

    Our son would have died well upon our land, if not for you and your ravings of a place safer than Orvai. Get thee out, and torture my soul no longer by your presence."

    And Lena bites her lip as she nods, keeping her peace as she forces down every tear and objection.

    She is right. If we had never come here, those men would still be alive. And she knows only good grace has spared her, as the woman's speech echoes around her head long after she leaves the house.

    Another Luthor who prevails…

    As Kara marks the door of the clear house, Lena stops short at the sight of a small group gathered in the main road. Alex kneels on the cobble, surrounded by three men, two of whom hold down another fourth man.

    As Lena approaches, her heart drops as Winn yells into the sky, the whites of his eyes wide and frantic. With grim faces the men hold his shoulders as Alex ties a strip of cloth tightly above his elbow, cinching it down until he winces.

    His face goes pale and slack, and he lies back to stare at the sky once more, his entire body shaking with tremors as he squeezes his eyes shut. Above him, James stands, his eyes glued to Winn's face with a glazed expression, his mouth pressed into a flat line, and his eyes wide.

    Jaw clenched, he raises his sword high.

    Kara pulls Lena into her chest, shielding her face, and Lena squeezes her eyes shut even as the wet sounds of tearing flesh and Winn's screams cement themselves within her mind forever. And Winn groans slightly, nearer to unconsciousness than lucidity as everyone around him erupts into a flurry of action, pressing rags to the stump of his arm, tying yet another linen about the wound to prevent unnecessary loss of blood.

    They lay him out on a stretcher and carry him into a nearby house into which Alex follows him, her cheeks ashen and the skin about her eyes pinched with worry.

    James is the last to enter the house, a single tear tracking down his face as he steps into the shadow, and Lena's heart breaks for him.

    And still, she has not the time for sorrow, for a man runs towards her- one of the hornblowers, she recognizes.

    "Hail, Lena!" He greets, jogging up to her.

    "Hail Lehn," she replies, raising a hand. "Well met."

    "Aye, well met, though you may not think the same once you hear these tidings. You know the length to which we sounded our horns, yet despite the expected numbers, our summons brought but three hundred souls to the gate."

    "How can this be?" Lena asks, her eyes wide with disbelief.

    "I know not."

    By the time night falls, nearly a hundred-fifty damned souls are counted within the captured sector.

    Not even close to the numbers we had expected. Where then have they gone? Yet this strange twist of fate must be counted a small blessing.

    -

    A Night And A Day Later

    Darkness falls heavy over Argo, thick with rain and a sense of lucid unreality. Lena stumbles through the door of a small house, lingering upon the broad line of charcoal that bisects the wood. Drenched and exhausted, she flops onto the thin single bed with a groan, sniffing as the old dust upon the blankets flies into the air.

    A knock sounds at her door, and she lifts her head, tense with expectation only to relax as Kara's face pokes in, lit from below.

    "Care for some company?" She says with a tired smile, and Lena can't help the large grin that stretches her cheeks.

    "If it is yours? Always," she says, and Kara steps into the room. She holds a lantern aloft, and from behind her back she pulls a dark glass bottle.

    "Is that-?" Lena whispers, eyes tracking the bottle.

    "It is old, but that's what makes it good," Kara says with a wink. Lena looks on longingly, her eyes fixed on the bottle.

    "I could kiss you," she says, for she hasn't had a proper drink since this whole bloody ordeal began. And Kara looks at Lena in a way that reminds her of a night spent by a fire, and Lena can almost feel the heat of her, nearing, nearing…

    With her teeth, Kara pulls the cork from the bottle, spitting it carelessly to the ground to take a long draft. Her eyes drift closed, and the motion of her throat is nearly as hypnotic as the small trail that spills from the corner of her mouth.

    When Lena rises from the bed, Kara gulps, watching her as though she were a cat, padding unassumingly up to a mouse. She pulls the bottle from Kara's stiff grip, wiping her thumb across the wine-stained corner of Kara's mouth before bringing the ruby droplet between her lips.

    The bitterness of the wine spreads across her tongue, along with the hint of something sweet- something she wants to believe is Kara.

    She looks up into eyes she expects to be blue, but they are dark as the night, and beneath the weight of that gaze, Lena swallows thickly. She brings the bottle to her own lips, turning towards the bed with something akin to relief as the moment breaks over her back, and the thickness of the drink sweeps her mind away.

    She turns to sit on the bed, patting the dusty moth-eaten blanket beside her, and Kara moves to put the lantern on the small wooden table to sit beside her, quiet and contemplative.

    And like the wine they share, this moment feels bittersweet for a reason Lena doesn't understand. She finds herself gazing at Kara, and as she stares, a tear falls from the corner of Kara's eye, followed by another, and another, and this brave soul cries so often her tears should not surprise Lena by now, but they do.

    Her heart feels open and wild, and without thinking Lena brushes away the shining droplets, whispering into the space between them, "What is it darling?"

    And Kara looks deep into her eyes with something vulnerable- with a pain Lena has only ever known the scent of.

    "It's just… Being here, I suppose," Kara says softly, and with a sudden intensity she asks, "Do you know why the city is named Argo?"

    Eyebrows raised, Lena shakes her head.

    "Argo is a Kryptonian name, meaning 'Garden'. It was named this many centuries ago, just as Orvai and Kandor were, for then, the great cities of this land had been built and governed by a different family.

    Lena frowns with confusion. "I thought the Luthors had always ruled Argo."

    Kara shakes her head bitterly.

    "Long before your ancestors set foot on this land, the country of Krypton was ruled by the family of the house of El. Great scholars and thinkers; stewards of the land; a people of science and spirituality, whose greatest quest was knowledge.

    Under their rule, the land flourished, and the people were rich with culture and livelihood."

    "In all my years, I have never heard nor read of their chronicle," Lena says frowning.

    "You would not have," Kara says somberly. "A hundred years past, a warlord- your great grandfather Lucious Luthor, marched across the shallow sea from the shores of Daxam.

    Impressed by the richness of these lands and its peoples, he promised them his friendship, but in the end, he brought only a conquering force.

    The country of Krypton had no true military, preferring to invest their every resource in the growth of their land and their people, and so, Daxam quickly conquered them.
