    Where stands the castle was once a great library and place of learning, where all were welcome to study. The reason the road runs straight to the castle and does not detour to the maze of the city is for their belief that knowledge and understanding must be accessible to all.  

    In the hands of the Luthors, the great libraries of Krypton fell to ashes, and the history of the kingdom was violently, forcibly retold. Most Kryptonians found themselves enslaved, forced to build great walls and militant castles; a monument to their defeat, even as their fine homes and cities were seized by Daxamites.

    Understanding nothing of the land nor beauty, Lucious felled, burned, and stripped of wealth the bountiful forests he once coveted- slaughtered the roaming plains of cattle and sheep, and when his fist finally closed over Krypton, he executed every member of the house of El save a small group that managed to escape.

    In the madness, two mothers fled the city with their children, a boy and a girl, and for fourteen years they wandered the wilderness, raising their children together in secrecy. There, tucked away in the far reaches of the forest, they endured long past the height of Lucius' short reign, though he never stopped hunting them.

    Appalled, Lena sits back, her eyes wide. "Why are you telling me this, Kara?" She whispers, trying to make sense of what Kara's story points to. She had been distantly aware of the many gaps in the history even as she absorbed it, but the erasure of an entire people seemed almost preposterous.

    "I tell you because it is important that you know the true history of your relationship to these people and to this land… And because it is important to me that you know who I am." And Kara slides her hands beneath Lena's, holding them gently.

    "Because I want you to understand me."

    And it still doesn't make sense yet, but Lena recognizes the telling of this story as an act of trust, so she remains silent, waiting for Kara to continue.

    "The two children- Alura and Zor El, grew older together, and even as their mothers passed into the eternal night, they lived well, for the land was kind to them where they knew people would not be.

    But nature offered them too much bounty."

    The woman endured a terrible pregnancy- one that the wilderness could not aid in alleviating, and in a desperate bid to hold onto each other, she sought the doctors of Argo, praying to their God Rao that he might spare their lives."

    "And was there hope?"

    Kara's face is grim, and filled with a sorrow so aching, Lena feels tears prickling at the corners of her eyes.

    "For the woman, no. But her child- a little girl entered the world even as she faded from it. Her beloved took the child into the wilderness, and there he raised her for seven years, teaching her of the land and its workings. Of the now forgotten tales and beliefs of their people.

    But he too fell ill, and once more the house of El sought mercy from the doctors of Argo.

    Alas, it was as though their fortunes had turned against them in every way, for as he slept, he was recognized by the features he shared with his father by a doctor of many winters. Spurred by greed, the old man reported him to the house of Luthor, and there, Zor El perished within the city's inner walls.

    "And the child?" Lena asks anxiously.

    A small half-smile turns up the corner of Kara's mouth, even as a tear leaks from the corner of her eye. "The child was small and wily. When her father was captured she fled the city, and found sanctuary outside the walls, in the cottage of two kindhearted peasants, who already cared for a child of their own. They took her in, taught her the ways of this new world. Their language, their dress..."

    With startling clarity, Kara looks up into Lena's eyes, and as she speaks, every word falls from her tongue like a hammerblow. "My name," she says, "is Kara Zor-El. I am the last blood of the house of El, the last heir to Argo- to this land that has never been mine, yet has never been anything but."

    And Lena is speechless.

    Every notion of birthright, every strange twisting path of fate, they all seem to lay themselves out before her like a ridiculous sea of contention somehow perfectly aligned to the tragic concepts of ironic fate and destiny.

    Thought after thought slams into her like the strike from a club, and she cannot breathe. The sins of her family; the life she has led- the life Kara was always meant to lead; the life she herself would have led had she not been taken from Kandor by Lionel, and she is irrevocably aware of the ancient rules of birthright.

    With a sudden, terrible understanding, she thinks of everything her family has stripped from Kara; from so many; from the very land itself. She thinks of her own life and cannot view any memory- any passing statement the same, and she is viscerally aware of the ways in which the roles and lives of peasants and nobility are consigned to mere chance and chance alone.

    And she remembers every ignorant word that has fallen from her tongue- of the ways Kara and her own experiences have continually challenged her to view her position through different eyes, and she feels more shame than ever before for it all seems so obvious now.

    "I did not know," Lena whispers, her voice shaking with every word. "I swear to you, Kara, I did not know…" and she feels like sobbing, but she holds it within her, meeting Kara's eyes. "But I understand now if you can never truly trust me. If you… If you hate me for this.

    I am so sorry, Kara. I am so sorry for what my family has taken from you."

    And all she wants to do is to gather Kara up in her arms and to make a shield of herself, but she holds herself instead, filled to the brim with unsurity.

    "I don't hate you," Kara says, and with that kindly spoken phrase, Lena closes her eyes at the warmth of Kara's palm against her cheek.

    "I could never. I tell you these things because I- because I trust you with this knowledge; this knowledge which, in the hands of any other Luthor would have damned me.

    Because you are the first and only Luthor to have ever earned this trust from me and my people.

    Because the responsibilities you bear have been rightfully earned. You are tied to them, and may never be free of the effects of that childhood, but every day that you choose to listen- to conduct yourself with kindness and thoughtfulness for every man and beast's life, you distance yourself from that legacy ever farther.

    You are more than your blood; your name, for you… You have chosen to make of yourself a true leader."

    And at Kara's words, Lena feels almost dizzy.

    "I could never hate you," Kara repeats in that same impossibly soft voice, and for a moment, Lena feels like she means something more than that.

    So it is that the last daughter of the house of Luthor, and the last daughter of the house of El sit together in the bed of a house that belongs to neither of them, drinking far into the night as the rain washes the blood from the streets.

    -

    Two Months Later

    The days are long and hot once more, and Lena finds her days filled to the brim with the small decisions everybody relies upon, and when one night she is stuck at her desk pouring over a ledger that notes their stocks and distribution of wood resources.

    The demands of the walls advancement, as well as the construction of a wall to guard the farmlands from trampling feet requires an exorbitant amount of lumber and stone, but lumber must be chosen from far and wide so as not to destroy the merits of the current forest growth.

    The morning's activities had begun far before the light of dawn graced her window, and now the stars blaze fiercely overhead, bringing with them a comfortable warmth.

    Bleary with depletion, Lena rubs at her eyes, squinting at the columns of information that dance across the paper. They make little sense, seeming almost obstinate with defiance, and she sits back wearily only to jump with a start when she looks up to see Kara standing alone in the center of the large courtroom, awaiting mediation.

    "Sorry," Kara says, holding her hands up before her innocently, "I came only to request an audience."

    "Oh?" Lena says, her cheek dimpling as she sits straighter, her hand waving accommodatingly through the air.

    "And what is it that you would bring to my attention, brave heart?"

    With an almost bashful smile, Kara drops her gaze to the floor, turning to pace with an overly thoughtful frown.

    "Well you see, my dilemma is this: my friend… My best friend, as it happens, she and I used to spend many a night beneath the stars together, trading knowledge, and stories for food.

    Now I find that every moment of her day is occupied- mind you, by many a worthy cause." Kara stops pacing, turning to face Lena.

    "But I miss her."

    All exhaustion forgotten, Lena is on her feet, rounding her desk, and Kara strides up to meet her, smiling widely as Lena throws her arms tightly around her neck.

    "I miss you," Kara whispers into her hair, and Lena feels nothing but warmth. She leans back to look at Kara, only to realize how close she is- so close that the warmth of her breath washes over Lena's cheek with every word.

    "Some days, I cannot fathom the ways in which my best friend is the most difficult person in the land to gain an audience with."

    And Lena's heart stutters in her chest, and her tongue feels loose with sudden carelessness.

    "Tonight, she says, playing with the soft ends of Kara's hair, "fortune smiles upon you. There may be nothing left to do, that would not meet more fulfilling conclusions tomorrow."

    Then Lena remembers, and she pulls away, taking Kara's hand to lead her to her desk.

    "I have something for you, Kara."

    And Kara smiles brilliantly at her. "Oh? A favor of yours?"

    Lena just rolls her eyes, the tips of her ears heating ridiculously as she shuffles through one of the drawers.

    "Something like it."

    Onto Kara's outstretched palm, Lena places a small leather pouch, which Kara upends gingerly into her other hand.

    A silver ring tumbles out, glinting up at them in the lamplight. The band of it is wide and smooth, and upon its face rises the crest of the house of El.

    For several long seconds Kara stares wordlessly down at the ring, and suddenly nervous, Lena begins pulling at her fingers as she babbles.

    "The silversmith asked if there was any favor he could offer me. At first I could not think of a thing, but he insisted, and after some time, I gave him my silver coins. From them, he made this."

    Kara gazes at her with a completely unreadable expression, and in one fluid motion, she slides the ring onto the middle finger of her right hand, brings her hand up to Lena's cheek, and kisses her.

    The metal of the ring is cool against her cheek in clear contrast to the heat of Kara's mouth. She loses herself for a moment, hands tangling in Kara's hair, as concentrated energy floods her every atom, and she gasps as her rear runs up against her desk.

    Still Kara pushes closer, pulling at the frayed ends of her consciousness as pure desire washes through her, both soothed and excited by the soft stroke of Kara's tongue against hers.

    And she has never felt so… So-

    Like her mind has suddenly awoken from a dream, a terrible thought floods her with fear- a vision of Kara, damned and standing before Lena, awaiting the mercy of her blade.

    Tearing herself away Lena gasps for air, chest heaving, and Kara jumps back like she's been burned. She stares at Lena, wide-eyed and equally breathless, her face filled with worry, and for a moment all Lena feels like doing is curling up to cry.

    "Oh Lena, I'm so sorry, I didn't-" Kara is saying in quiet, anxious tones, and it's too much.

    "No!" Lena says loudly, and Kara shuts her jaw with an audible click.

    "No," Lena says softer, shaking her head. "It isn't that."

    "What is then?" Kara asks taking a small half-step closer, and Lena knows she is holding back a torrent of worry to allow her the space to speak, and she leans into that safety.

    "I…" She begins, heaving a sigh that bleeds into a quiet sob. "I am not sure I can allow myself a happiness like this offers. Not for reasons of deserving, but because I could not… bear it if I allowed myself to have this, only to have it taken from me, especially in the manner in which this world takes from us."

    Tears build within Kara's eyes but never fall, and drawing in a shaky breath she nods, saying, "There is no greater sorrow, than to be mindful of the happy time, in misery, and that thy teacher knows."

    And Lena steps forward once more, bringing her hand to Kara's. "Dante," she says softly, and Kara nods, and the ring on her hand is now warm. As she cries, Lena allows Kara to gather her up in a firm hug, sharing in her quiet miseries. She cries for this world, and for what it means to live in it.

    She cries, because the softest thing in this world is the one thing she cannot allow herself to fully have, lest it destroy her.

    Chapter End Notes

    Homecoming

    Chapter Summary

    To live a life of purpose and to find dignity in death.

    Chapter Notes

    The trees array themselves in dusk's glorious standard as autumn's harvest draws ever nearer. Those who tend the fields of Orvai circulate in guarded shifts that span three long days before making the journey back to Argo, relieved of their duties by the next shift.

    Argo itself is a battlefield of action as slowly, carefully, patrols clear the city's lower ring in segments, pushing the barricades farther out like a virus overtaking its host.

    Three short months have passed since the initial siege of the lower ring, and when the two barricades finally meet signaling the end of the occupation, all celebrate with boisterous abandon in the streets, indulging in a great feast of hot meat, fresh breads, and tart apples.

    Children run through the streets as though they are at a fair, laughing and skipping in a way they have not been allowed to for the past year.

    They weave through the tall buildings, still black with soot and char, and upon the wallwalk of the lower ring, Lena watches the festivities below.

    As the light dwindles, several lamps disperse like tiny embers into the night, following their winding paths only to wink out one by one.

    The wind tugs gently at her hair, and she turns to face it, gazing out over the farm lands beyond.

    Acres upon acres of fields and crops stretch out before her like a golden sea, and as she revels in the peace of it, a dark shadow presses down upon her.

    She turns, allowing herself to finally look upon the castle, pale and ghostly in the fading light. It looks more than ever like an ornate box- a simple square filled with other squares that had once meant so much.

    Yet somehow, devoid of life and purpose, the squares still retain their power- still taunt her with both mystery and answer, calling a terrible anxiety to rise within her.

    And stranger still, she knows it to be an inevitability she must face- the city must be cleared, and the castle offers a virtual bounty of resources, metal, weaponry and armor.

    And memories. a lifetime of memories…

    Her excuses to avoid the castle have dwindled one by one like crystals of frost before a candle as every captured building and street place its looming towers nearer. Just this morning a thick, strong length of rope was finally finished, fastened to the second portcullis in preparation of the siege of the inner wall.

    They had raised the first gate five days prior, and the following days, hornblowers had drawn as many of the dead as they could to the lattice of the second gate's wood, laboring to stab, or to shoot down any that stumbled up to the gate.

    Tomorrow, I shall walk the halls of my family. Of my girlhood. Of my entire world, what feels like a lifetime ago.

    As many times as Lena repeats that concept to herself, it still feels foreign, so distant it feels like holding on to a breath already exhaled. She drinks deeply of the crisp air, shivering as the dark windows of the castle stare down at her.

    And she cannot meet its gaze, her eyes falling instead to the outer ring- to the houses below, where stands the small home she shares with Kara, and warmth blossoms within her once more.

    After traveling and living together for so long- after having never lived alone, it had felt almost odd when faced with the option to pursue individual housing- a sentiment Lena would have rather died than express, though Kara had no qualms, voicing her request that they might still live together boldly and honestly.  

    With one backwards glance to the castle, Lena turns to descend the wall, bidding goodnight to James, who remains a tall, unmoving figure against the Western sky, watching over the land with an almost meditative focus.

    Her boots knock against the cobble as she follows the path, and slowly, slowly she begins to walk faster, faster until she is running- running headlong down the street, her panting loud and harsh as she sprints forward, only to slow to a stop, gazing breathlessly at the flickering lamplight in Kara's window.

    -

    The morning carries with it the scent of stale rot and cold water, old, fresh, and brittle in Lena's lungs. As the portcullis rises almost gingerly, the bodies piled against it fall forwards, rolling sadly in the dust before their feet.

    Several people spring forward, hurriedly loading body after body into a large cart, and after nearly thirty minutes, a path appears through them.

    Though there are many, Lena cannot bring herself to look at their faces for fear that she might…

    We can do this without you, Kara had said, holding Lena's hands gently between her own. You need not see to know. If you wish it, I will act as your eyes and your ears.

    The offer was born of a kindness so very true to Kara, but something deep within Lena knows that this act will shape her in ways she does not fully understand- in pivotal ways that will define a certain measure of her.

    There is a strength she would ask of herself to endure- the strength of facing the ruins of her old life, however gruesome they may be; to know, even though she suspects the pain of it to be unfathomable.

    To her right, Kara steps forward, gingerly picking her way between the sprawling limbs and dusty torsos, and once inside the wall, their group of fifty splits off into three patrols, twenty to clear the inner ring circling left, another twenty to the right.

    The third ascends the grassy hill, and it is with this group Lena hurries on, every step elevating the nervous energy within her until she feels as though she is vibrating out of her skin.     The Open Doors

    Chapter Summary

    In which every door leads somewhere.

    She fidgets nervously, her shoulders rigid as she faces the crowd, bright eyes darting from face to face.

    In this moment, Lena is reminded of the first time she had addressed the whole of Orvai, and quietly she moves to stand by Eve's side, laying a comforting hand upon the young woman's shoulder.

    Though it trembles, her voice, when it comes, rings out clearly and steadily over the heads of the gathered crowd like the cry of a gorcrow.

    "Three nights past I stood guard over the harvest of the western fields. The night before our departure the moon shone full- and beneath her white face I spied nearly fifty damned souls pacing the southern forest. 

    They seemed to follow a common purpose unbeknownst to me, and curious, I tracked their numbers until we stood just south of the plains of Jerat. 

    Like some accursed army, those twenty met with a great rank off their own, whose numbers I thought to be yet another group of fifty, until- until they swarmed thicker and thicker, and the forest crashed and bent loudly beneath the strength of their numbers. 

    And I climbed- the tallest tree I could find revealed to my eye nearly two-thousand dead and damned gathered over the plains, leaching endlessly from the southern forests."

    As Eve recounts her tale, great calm suffuses Lena even as horror blooms in the eyes of all those gathered before her. A hushed murmur ripples through the crowd, and soon a sharp lamentation rings out over the gathered peoples. 

    "What has Jerat to do with us? Have we not endured enough?" 

    Jaw squared, Lena gazes out, her eye catching on Winn's gaunt and hopeless face. His visage takes her aback- he looks small and weary, and his foreign countenance is almost frightening. 

    Steeling herself against the trembling of her own courage, she holds his eye, allowing every ferocity of hope to embolden her words. 

    "We have endured hell and more, and yet this storm, we must weather too. Our crops lie vulnerable to the west, and should this threat remain unmet, their Numbers could easily sweep into our lands come winter, and therein lies our doom. 

    Should we choose to wait- to gamble with fate, and to meet with their numbers only when they set foot upon our lands we risk befouling the land that would raise our crops and livestock.

    Should we face them together- should every able-bodied soul fell nearly fifteen damned, this doom shall fall from our shoulders like water.

    Before us lies a crossroads, wherein every person must decide their lot- be it to wait and to hope this threat passes us by like a cloud in the night, or to act- to meet with this threat before it has the chance to spring itself upon us.

    I would not ask a vote of you, nor do I delight in asking for your assistance in meeting this threat, but it is your right to know, and in knowing, to cast your own lots.

    They range far too near to our borders to simply allow them to stay. Unless there be any brave souls who desire to face this lot beside us, I shall depart with Kara in the morrow to face their numbers."

    She swallows thickly as the words leave her mouth, and it feels like the very foretelling of her doom- of Kara's, and like an arrow, her eyes meet with a blue so clear it would take her breath away. And she feels no fear then, even as the hushed whispers of the crowd grow to a near clamor, until a deep voice rings out, loud and clear.

    "I will go with you. You shall not face this threat alone. This is our enemy to face."

    All eyes turn to James, and she sees the weariness of this world burning within his eyes- his helpless desire to end this madness, even as he bears the weight of their stares with dignity, offering no more and no less. Beside him, Winn stares down- stares bitterly at the stump of his arm, his jaw clenched and his brow dark with storm.

    "I too, will stand by you," Nia shouts from the far edges of the crowd, her small hand raised high in the air, only to be joined by a second as Brainy stands beside her with a solemn nod. 

    As though it were the catalyst to a flood, several more hands shoot up, until a virtual sea of palms faces Lena- a sea that raises her up. 

    A sea that threatens to drown her.

    -For those of you who aren't familiar, when Lena says "You must know by now, where you go I shall follow. Your people are my people too…" it is a nod to book of Ruth- a widow of the land of Moab, who loves her mother-in-law so dearly, she follows her into the foreign land of Judah.

    This is her pledge: "Do not urge me to leave you or to turn back from you. Where you go I will go, and where you stay I will stay. Your people will be my people and your God my God. Where you die I will die, and there I will be buried. May the Lord deal with me, be it ever so severely, if even death separates you and me.”

    Next: The sleeping dragon wakes.

    The Plains of Jerat

    Chapter Summary

    To war against the sea.

    Chapter Notes

    All things must end.
    All things must end.

    The mantra echoes soundlessly through Lena's head, rising and falling with every beat of her heart as she gazes out across the vast expanse of grassy plains. It stretches out before her, thick and heavy with the cloying scent of rotting flesh- a scent that twines with another equally terrible odor: a torturous perfume concocted by some sadistic god.  

    Though she has never seen the plains themselves, she remembers one hazy eve of her girlhood wherein she had eavesdropped on the castle guard. The two had grumbled loudly to one another of the wretched smells wafting from the plains, and that night, when her old maid had bustled her into bed, she had mused aloud over its significance.

    The woman had listened quietly for a time, brushing out the small and sparse tangles in Lena's hair until it was smooth and glistened like a river.

    "This wicked stench you speak of is the breath of a great and terrible dragon," she had whispered, wrinkles stretching as her eyes became big and serious. "He sleeps beneath those grasslands, dreamin' on and on, and will do until the day he is called upon to wake."

    "How do you know?" Lena had asked, wide-eyed and quiet with awe, and to her boundless wonder the old woman simply tapped the side of her nose with a twinkle in her eye.

    "I know, for these stories belong not to me but to those who came long before me. It is only now that they begin to be forgot."

    "Baseless superstition," the King had scoffed when, the following day, Lena had spoken carelessly of her new knowledge. "There exist no such things as dragons. Had there, I would have one for myself: a guard for this castle, and a protector for you, my little princess. Now tell me, where was it you heard this tall tale?" 

    As he gazed down at her from beneath his thickly furrowed brow, she had wondered if her father might truly possess the strength and ability to bring low such a fearsome creature. She wonders now if he would have had the strength to defy this world too- to tame it and to make it his own.

    Perhaps, but at a cost equally damning.

    She had never seen her dear maid again, and within a fortnight the position was filled by a young girl from the distant city of Kandor, leaving the old woman to fade into the distant past: another blurry face to be fondly remembered and bitterly mourned.

    Now, with that girl at her side; with the strength of nearly a hundred capable souls at her side, she once more faces this world with bared teeth and a feral will to survive- to protect all that is worth living for.

    Many of those around her gag silently as the scentscape of Jerat wafts over them, seeping into their hair and the fabric of their clothes. At the forefront of their forces, those with spears shift their weight from foot to foot, the sun glinting off the tips of their weapons as they wave behind a short wall of shield-bearers. 

    Behind them stand seven rows of people- an arsenal of weaponry tied to waist, torso and shoulder alike as all those with bows fix heavy arrows to strings. The armory had outfitted several with various pieces of plate and leather, though few sport a cohesive set. Many had to forgo the larger pieces of metal plate, its sheer weight proving far too heavy for most, and the incongruence of it all lends a slightly disheveled look to their meager force. 

    Sitting tall upon a sweet mare the color of oats, James flashes like a shining beacon before the small army, the plate upon his breast alight with the blessing of the early sun. A pair of steel bracers encircle his forearms, and thin leather pads are strapped to the fronts of his woven trousers. Though he reminds Lena of a hardy beetle, he still somehow manages to appear intimidating. 

    As she sweats beneath her own armor, Lena thanks every god in the heavens that the height of summer has long past, though had it been at its hottest, she would still be glad for the protection- more than glad, for before them lies a roiling sea of damned souls greater than any had imagined. 

    When the last army of Argo had arrived at the borders of Jerat, the flatness of the plains had greatly distorted the sheer numbers of the gathered crowd, and as they neared, a terrible understanding fell upon all. 

    Sixteen summers past, as Lena had walked about the castle gardens, she had come upon a terrifying, fascinating sight: a small blood-red lump lying still upon the gravel path. There had been a constant shimmering sort of reflectivity to it that gave it the appearance of motion, even as it remained stubbornly in one place. 

    Curiosity, having always triumphed over any sense of wariness in her young heart (though never to the point of foolishness,) bade her near, and she soon discovered the red to be thousands upon thousands of little ants milling about the surface of some small object.

    The day was young, and Lena, having few responsibilities at such a tender age and an abundance of time, had crouched down before the phenomena, determined to observe its every stage. 

    As the sun crested the sky, a stream of ants poured in and out of the odd structure, melting it slowly before her eyes: a mold of red that fell into the shapes of bones and beak, as bite after tiny bite the ants chiseled away at the creature in an attempt to become it.

    Completely absorbed in the little creatures, Lena had not noticed the darkening of the evening, nor the silent approach of the Queen, who had exclaimed with outrage and disgust when her eyes landed upon the little bird. 

    Banished from the sight to partake in the customary silence of supper, all Lena could think about was the little creatures, and when she returned the following day, every trace of the ants and the bird had long disappeared.

    Now, nearly seven-thousand dead by Lena's count crawl and stumble across the plains, plastering its grasses to the ground. She imagines the great dragon of Jerat- imagines them dancing along its mountainous hyde, grinding the scorching flesh between their cold teeth until nothing but ash remains. 

    Horses toss their heads and snort nervously as the joyful cries of gore crows shatter the sky above their heads. Too far to Lena's left, Kara pats Ehl Ahvrig's neck reassuringly, cooing soft soothing words into the horse's ear. Blue eyes flick up to meet Lena's stare, and even at the foot of death's table, she cannot help but lose the sting of the world to Kara; cannot help how beautiful she finds her: an atonement for the sins of this world, wherein she finds a balance to the ugliness of life. 

    Even then, to the quick of her, Lena cannot stave off the dread roiling in her gut as she witnesses the crumbling of their every future. 

    But should Lena's gut hold dread, her heart holds boundless, fearless love, and she holds within it all those she would call her family; welds them to her very being. 

    Should Lena's heart hold love, her mind holds courage- the knowledge of what must be done to earn the chance to live; of what she would do- will always do for her people. 

    All three lurch as James raises his blade, calling a hush like the wind to blow through the grasses, stilling them as though time had left them behind. Every archer falls too to that timelessness, bows uplifted like a prayer to the heavens- a pledge to follow the guidance of his shining steel. 

    His arm drops, and a flock of graceful fowl reborn as hornets take to the air, arcing swiftly on their last flight towards the thick of the masses, their targets swallowed effortlessly by the press of bodies. 

    Again, the shining blade salutes the sun, and again the air hums with a choir of twangs and whistles. Over and over again they dive into the sea like kingfishers- deadly hunters, whose marks are felled with a whisper, and when every archer's quiver is nearly empty, Lena's gut grows heavy, for the act seems to have been as futile as the sinking of nearly four hundred arrows into the mud. 

    "And then what…" 

    Zil Eshs flicks his ears back as her voice breaks upon the whispered question. Though most of the dead are riddled with a year's worth of decay and weathering, she knows that to focus on the weakness of any individual would be as futile and dangerous as picking out the trickle of a brook from a waterfall.

    When Lena looks about her, she sees her own despair mirrored in the eyes of all- a despondency that thickens like tar with every passing second.

    When James raises his hand in a salute that beckons her forwards, she feels almost frozen, fingers barely flexing on the rope affixed to Zil Eshs' shoulders. She moves, for it is her duty; she moves, and tries not to stare at the swarming plains behind his gleaming shoulders. 

    As Lena nears, James mutters somberly, "We must try."

    "Aye," Lena answers hollowly. "Though I fear that those of us who would remain shall be fated to remain forever."

    "Have we any other choices before us?"

    "My mind betrays me. I cannot think of any other."

    Gritting her teeth, Lena looks down at the black and shining hairs of Zil Eshs' mane. Should fortune smile upon them, perhaps the herd might turn West and to the sea- but if not...
     
    "Perhaps we might lead them North- send them down Striped River and thence to the sea?" She asks, knowing the answer before it comes.

    "I hesitate to trap ourselves against its flow."

    "Then what?"

    "Then… We face them. As planned, we draw them South then North, then South again, and ever East, like a toy tied to a string. We shall execute their numbers until all arms tire and we can slay no more.

    We hope that it is enough. That our strength together would fell if not all, then enough to make the difference."

    Lena meets James' burning eyes then, tries to mirror the set of his shoulders even as she grows ever distant from this place, and all-too stuck within it. 

    The sharp click of James' tongue snaps her back into the horrors of this reality as he guides his horse forwards to address their sullen ranks.

    "We stand upon the threshold of darkness," his voice rings out over their heads, drawing the eyes of thousands as the swarming damned seem to still, turning in an eerie wave to listen.

    "From which there is no shame in turning from. My lot is cast. I shall ride to meet this evil of innocent face, and should any cast their lot with mine, do so with hope in your hearts- for a day where we may yet see our people safe, and whole."

    Lena's eyes flick over to Winn where he sits amongst the hornblowers, and her heart breaks with James' every word. Fervently, ardently she hopes for that day like a prayer- imagines Kara in the peaceful plains, playing freely with Ehl Ahvrig, not grim-faced and terrifyingly small before the rotting masses.

    "We have the strength to face even this, for we-" he cries, his arm sweeping out in a wide arc, "are bonded by our courage; by the sweat of our brows, and by our love for our people- for this land!

    I burn with it."

    A tremor runs through Lena's shoulders, and she burns, the haze of dread falling from her shoulders like cobwebs. 

    "And should I burn for it, I shall be glad kindling.

    So I ask, who will stand with me this day?"

    Steel flashing with the sky, Lena raises her blade in a salute, a raw cry leaping from her throat to join with the thundering roar as all who stand in the field raise their fists with her.

    Almost light with anticipation, James grins, a feral shine to his eye, and he growls, "Then guard yourselves well, and those beside you."

    The flutter of anxiety courses through her as she gazes out over her people. None have moved to return to Argo, and though the fact ought to make her feel brave, she feels only a gnawing regret.

    "Riders- to the North! Arms- to the South!  Ready!" He calls out, and the twelve horses bearing hornblowers gather to the left of James as a thicket of weaponry blooms amongst the ranks. Nervous anticipation of the cataclysmic event seems to infect all, and many shift about, gripping and regripping their weapons, some turning their heads about in an attempt to catch one last glimpse of a friend or a lover.

    Lena cannot help but do the same. 

    "Horns!" 

    James bellows, and Lena tears her eyes from Kara's, yanking her horn from her belt and lifting it to her lips. A sweet and pure hymn ignites the air as the breath of twenty others join in a chorus that rolls swiftly across the plains.

    "Let them break their teeth upon our blades," James taunts across the plains, and with a graceful lift of his arm, he thrusts his sword into the belly of the sky- a glowing brand that calls Lena to stand her ground, though her every desire is to take those beside her and run as far away as possible.

    "For Krypton!"

    The sword lowers like an accusing finger, sending the call rolling across the plain, and as James leads his mare forward in a trot, the solemn march begins.

    Lena's arms shake with every footfall upon the ground, and each moment passes with honed clarity. She watches the hornblowers dwindle North, sending a silent prayer to the heavens as Zil Eshs paces slowly behind the rows of shields and spears as they near the Southern reaches of the crowd.

    The last acre that lies between them thins at an alarming pace, until several of the damned break away from the bulk of the crowd, stumbling quickly forwards only to fall swiftly upon the line of spears that protrudes behind their wall of shields. 

    All hold their ground, intent on avoiding the hazard of tripping upon fallen bodies, and as the wave of dead presses almost gently into the ranks of shields, their numbers seem to fill the very sky like a plague of locusts. 

    A spear thrusts out, catching a man through the top of his skull, and as though he had never fallen, another two stand in his place as slowly their ranks slide back, baiting the pressing darkness onwards. 

    Little hills of bodies begin to rise, marked by many tripping, falling corpses catching on one another, and as the song of the Horsemen sounds to the North, a portion of the crowd begins to split off, leaving nearly three-thousand to continue on in a South-easterly direction.

    As the people of Argo pass off spears and shields in shifts felling foe after foe in an endless mill, the damned press against them with relentless persistence. At the passing of an hour, a gap- slightly too wide breaks between the shields, allowing the thick press of bodies to infiltrate their line and leaving all who stand behind it to scream and slash and cut with furious effort.

    A familiar yell splits the air to Lena's right, and she turns to glimpse Sam slashing out with a short dagger, her arm blurring with the motion. Lena almost thinks she has missed, but for the corpse that sinks to its knees before her.

    All anxiety turns to action as Lena's own blade sings, dipping into the sea of the damned to flick small black diamonds into the air. Two fall to the sweeping edge of her sword, passing easily through their heads when, in her periphery, a swift motion peeks in and out between the crowd in ghostly flashes.

    Though she spends several precious moments searching she does not see it again, and the odd terror it strikes within her heart lingers, leaping as it flashes again amidst the hungering crowd.

    Far too near…

    And then she sees it. She sees it briefly behind Sam, and with a cry of stubborn refusal, Lena loops one arm about Zil Eshs' neck, swinging herself down beside her friend even as a scrawny young girl lunges at Sam from the right, almost angelic in her flight compared to the disease and rot that surrounds them.

    Thud. 

    The girl's mouth opens, her teeth shining like the brilliant marble of Argo in the dawn. Lena raises her sword.

    Thud.

    The angel flies nearer still, all suffering and rapture indistinguishable in her twisted expression. Lena brings her arm down in benevolent christening. 

    Thud.

    The corpse is light upon her blade, her face bathed in a warmth foreign to her for many months- a heat that marks her in some accusatory fashion. 

    With a great wrench Lena seals the part of herself that feels the weight of her into a deep and buried chamber. She whirls about, thrusting her arm into the head of something barely human- a mercy, it feels, and she marvels at that juxtaposition as she drags herself back up onto Zil Eshs with a great heave. 

    It is a graceless maneuver, but soon she sits tall and strong atop him once more, fighting constantly the Orphean desire to search out Kara as she forces herself to watch those within the reach of her arm. 

    The wound in their defenses heals itself once more, as a row of jabbing spears re-establish themselves behind the long wooden planks of shields.To Lena's right, Querl Dox bounds forward with his long bladed staff, allowing the person before him to fall back panting. He thrusts the long blade past his new shield mate- a woman of Argo named Grace, his jaw flexing even as the remainder of his expression embodies a near perfect calm.

    The star of sweat shines bright upon his brow, and as Grace readjusts her grip, hand grabbing at the edge of her shield, teeth close with brutal force upon her gloved fingers. She screams out, and Querl yanks his spear back from the forehead of one man to drive it into the rotting nose of the once-person that holds her. Quickly Grace yanks her hand back, tucking her shoulder against the board even as she cradles her shaking hand to her chest.

    On and on they strive, and as the weary sun falls from the heavens towards the Western skies, the great forest rises up behind their army like a distant cliff. Still the dead advance, dancing forwards with their limping, swaying gaits, pressing ever farther south. 

    Taking quick, frantic measurements of the distance between her people and the trees, Lena's eyes catch on something- subtle at first; a movement she pays no mind to until she takes a third, longer glance.

    Ice floods her veins as unnatural shadows stretch forth from beneath the leafy cover, only to break away into the distant forms of people- people that leech from between the trees in ones and twos until the entire treeline seems to sway and dance with bodies.

    "James!" Lena screams out, swinging her sword towards the advancing line, and as he turns upon his horse to follow the arrow of steel, his mouth falls soundlessly open. Like a dark cloud, the newcomers drag themselves forward to answer the call of horn and scream, and as they begin the long two-acre march, James shakes himself, turning swiftly about. 

    "FALL BACK! BACK, TO THE EAST!" 

    He roars, pointing with his sword towards the Eastern horizon, and at his word the line of shields and spears detaches, retracting like the singer of a wasp.

    Thus begins a mad and maddening race, as with distance, a grave miscalculation becomes abundantly clear. In the drawing of the herd by the hornblowers, the dead had dispersed, darkening the Northern horizon far to the East and West, and like a set of massive jaws closing about a rabbit's neck, their numbers crawl ever nearer towards their Southern reinforcements.

    As all on foot hurry East, rivers of sweat roll down Lena's face and neck. She can almost feel the rush of blood through her fingertips as she watches Kara's back- watches the dwindling gap that narrows about Kara's shoulders until it disappears.

    The jaws slam shut around them, and the world blurs into a lurid painting of terror, drawn by the hand of dream-like slowness as Zil Eshs carries them forward. Lena has no time for horror- no room for guilt as steel blurs at her will, passing like a specter through the face of a broken, aged man barely hobbling forward. 

    Banshee screams erupt from all sides, and confusion reigns as the unshielded borders of their army defend themselves bitterly against the onslaught. 

    For several terrible moments Lena loses the East, only to be oriented by brief glimpses of the sun and quick flashes of the trees between the rotting faces. As she leans back to stab at a woman, a spear springs past Zil Eshes, and with a whinny of surprise the black horse rears up, casting Lena to the ground. 

    Ears ringing, she gasps as air refuses to enter her lungs, and almost dizzy with nausea she forces her fist to grasp the hilt of her sword, arms trembling with the effort of lifting it. The blade hovers over the ground before her strength fails her, the weight of her gauntlets pinning her arm to the ground.  

    Beloved by fate, the rotted woman looms above Lena in ironic judgment, damning her as Lena would have her damned. 

    As if to grant Lena one last word, foul wind floods her lungs only to fly swiftly once more in a hoarse scream as a man blunts his teeth upon her greaves.

    White blurs across her field of vision, and, with a muted wet crunch, a thin line of silver appears between his eyes as an arrow- the last of its quiver, lodges itself in the top of the woman's skull. 

    Death leans down to kiss Lena, and eyes wide, she whips her head to the side, grunting as the slight corpse lands fully upon her chest, pinning her to the ground. 

    Almost inconceivably the burden lifts from her heart, and a strong hand takes firm hold of hers, pulling her from the bed of corpses. Sweat plasters Kara's forehead, sticking her stray hairs to her cheeks.

    A strange sort of ferocity burns in her eyes, and Lena feels it like a brand as Kara squeezes her hand tight, just once before letting go to push her up and onto Zil Eshs.

    As soon as Lena is seated, Kara dives to retrieve her cleaver and the arrow, shoving the gorey shaft back into her quiver with a look of disgust as she vaults up onto Ehl Ahvrig's back. As they press on Kara bends low, plucking a bright bloom of feathers from the meadow of shoulders. 

    With a quick shake to dislodge the flesh from its sharp tip, she whips the smooth length of yew from her back, setting arrow to taut string. A breath and the opening of her hand sends it like a wayward dream into the mind of one forever asleep. 

    Despite the fervor of their efforts, their small island dwindles further and further, yielding to the thickening ring of bodies that herds them into a tight, immobile knot.

    Teeth bared and arm clumsy with fatigue,  Lena strikes at any that come within reach with ever increasing inaccuracy. Her entire body trembles with every rise and fall of the blade, and she knows her strength will soon fail her even as she refuses to allow it- refuses to give up her soul with her arms at her sides. 

    Chills run down her spine as a piercing scream shatters the air, and when she glances over, the horror of yellowed teeth buried within soft flesh meets her eye. Blood wells around the wound, dripping down Eve's forearm as, with a quick yank, the young woman is pulled forward. Without hesitation James leaps forward, jabbing his sword quickly into the corpse's face, lips peeled back over a furious growl as he pulls Eve back behind the safety of his blade. 

    Oddly, his mouth opens with a soundless howl, and he drives his sword down into the barely visible crown of a tattered head, staggering back as he pushes Eve fully behind him. 

    A looming figure stumbles up to Lena then, and with a yell she throws her arms up,  lodging the tip of her blade through the heavy jaw, willing tongue and tooth to still. 

    Still, the air trembles with the terrible screams of her world, until the sky thunders with the sound of it. The rumble shakes through her knees, and the crowd before her seems suddenly confused, their attention scattering like roaches before a lantern as thunder reconciles into the beat of hoof against soil.

    Twelve noble vessels voyage into the sea of bodies, sailing in the tendency of winter's migrating fowl. The waters part before their flight, falling to momentum as those who stand in the way find themselves trampled or knocked spinning to the ground. 

    At the point of their arrow, Winn leans over the neck of his black steed, holding on by the strength of his knees as he raises a short-handled maul in his left fist. As the hornblowers scythe down a swathe of bodies, the army fights its way to them, flooding through the opening before it has the chance to heal itself once more.

    As the last person stumbles from the reach of grasping hands and groaning throats, Lena turns about, searching furiously only to urge Zil Eshs to fall back. He slows to trod nervously beside James and Eve as they stumble further and further from the waters, and with a frown of concern Lena searches them over. 

    Eve's forearm is an angry red, and Lena shudders with horror as she thinks on what awaits the young girl, only for her stomach to drop when her eyes fall to James' calf. It bears the same crimson tattoo of hunger, and for a moment she cannot speak. 

    "What happened to your horse?" is what she must ask.

    Refusing to meet her eye, James limps stiffly on, his mouth a straight line.
    "Dead."

    "Take mine."

    Frowning, James continues limping onwards, shaking his head without another word.

    "Take Zil Eshs and fly to Argo- both of you, as quickly as possible." Lena can almost feel the twitch of his jaw's muscle, and gritting her teeth she slides from Zil Eshs' back to stride briskly beside the pair. 

    In slow, measured words, she attempts to drive her words into the warrior's skull.

    "You must take Eve. Take her safely to Argo. Even if you leave now, your lives may not be spared. Think of Winn."

    A forked vein pulses in his forehead, and he stops in his tracks, nostrils flaring as he stares at her with an inscrutable look in his eye. But he nods finally, turning to regard Zil Eshs. 

    The horse eyes him warily, though he allows the pair to mount, casting a long-suffering huff in Lena's direction. When James grabs two shaky fistfulls of the stallion's mane the horse launches the trio forward, carrying them swiftly into the far distance.

    "To me!" Lena shouts, brandishing her sword, and as she jogs briskly on, so too do her people follow, shedding the slow dead like a clinging nightmare.

    "We must abandon this quest to folly," Lena shouts, her voice cracking with grief as she surveys those who remain. "Retreat to Argo! Horses and hornblowers, relinquish your horses to the wounded- ride in swift pairs to seek the doctors!"

    An organized sort of chaos erupts, and as Nia darts by, Lena calls out to her. Stained black with blood and wild-eyed, Nia whips around, searching until her sights land upon Lena.

    "Help me! All those who can ride together must do so with haste. We must separate those who have been wounded beyond repair from those who may yet be saved." Biting her lip, Lena looks down as she continues, "Only those who might feasibly make it to Argo must be given priority."

    The bright flash of steel passes before Nia's eyes, and, with a nod of understanding, she turns to push through to the remaining seven horses. 

    Squaring her shoulders, Lena continues forward, surveying the action as riders dismount, and those bitten are passed up onto horses, grim faced and stiff with fear. 

    Twelve wounded depart leaving three behind to lay down their arms and hands in the fields, and as she watches their departure, Lena's eyes catch on a horse- a white horse that trots freely beside their ranks, unburdened by a rider. 

    Panic floods her in a torrent as she takes an involuntary step towards Ehl Ahvrig, eyes jumping from face to face, heart sinking with increasing rapidity as she wheels frantically about, only to stop short, coming face to face with Kara.

    The sun is low behind her head, illuminating her stray hairs in a golden halo. She seems impossibly large as she gazes down at Lena, a great sea of emotion crashing within her blue eyes. Strangely too, there flashes a gleam of determination- a look that does nothing to soothe Lena's worry.

    But Kara just steps near, cupping Lena's blood-stained cheek in one rough, equally blackened hand, and kisses her with a softness that exists in almost violent contrast to all that surrounds them. 

    There is a slight tugging at Lena's belt; a quiet "I love you," spoken softly into her ear, and the warmth of Kara's hand is suddenly gone. When she opens her eyes again, Kara is sprinting towards Ehl Ahvrig, leaving Lena to stare helplessly, confusedly after her.

    All worry and confusion is replaced by dread as her eyes land on the gleaming horn that dangles from Kara's fist, and when Lena looks down at her own belt she already knows what she will find. 

    With a defiant, desperate shake of her head she gazes pleadingly at Kara- Kara who sits straight and tall atop Ehl Ahvrig's back, gazing back at her with more love than she could have ever fathomed a look could contain.

    Brutal understanding settles within her, and Lena's heart blossoms and breaks at the same time with the firm belief that Kara is about to do something incredibly foolish. Her legs carry her forward all-too slowly, and Kara is whooping loudly; Ehl Ahvrig's hooves are tearing at the dirt, and the stone in her gut grows heavier and heavier as Kara's form shrinks against the Northern horizon, skirting the advancing crowd. 

    The sound of Lena's horn rises and falls on the breeze, and as she listens, tears of frustration streak down her cheeks even as the bulk of the horde begins to turn, drawn by the sweet music. 

    Though the people of Argo are her very heart, for the first time Lena knows that, could she selfishly have chosen to take a horse and to follow Kara, she would have. The knowledge brings only guilt as she casts one last longing gaze to the North. 

    At the passing of an hour, the small patch of ants fades beneath the darkened sky, and heart aching with the weight of loss and anxiety, Lena strains to listen for the sound of the horn upon the wind.

    -

    In the absence of the sun the world grows cold and desolate, and when Ehl Ahvrig huffs out a weary sigh, the heat of her breath sends small puffs of smoke into the air. She plods slowly, steadily, onwards, her ears flicking with displeasure at every blow of the horn. 

    Kara can feel the mare's heavy panting breaths through her knees- can feel the claustrophobic weight of her armor as it presses down upon her shoulders, and through it all, she cannot truly seem to accustom herself to the lingering stench of fear and rot and Jerat as they coil thickly in her nose.  

    Flies buzz loudly by her ears, their tiny bodies bumping against her face as she rides ahead of the thick press of the damned. Almost loyally they trail after her, growling with what Kara can only imagine is frustration as she dangles before them like a taunt.

    As she follows the first stars to the North, she raises the horn once more to her lips, and, savoring the last kiss Lena might ever give her, she fills her lungs with the choking air, transforming it into a clear sound that ripples like water through the plains. 

    Smoke rises from her own lips as she whispers gentle encouragement into the horse's ear, counting down the hours until she is sure her people have long passed from the Plains of Jerat. With a murmured word she urges Ehl Ahvrig onwards, stealing precious time as the distance between them and the dead grows ever wider.

    The heavens stand silent vigil as the foul stench fades slightly with the dry ends of the grassy plains, and as they cross out of Jerat, Kara slides from Ehl Ahvrig's silver back, the weight of her armor driving her to one knee with bruising force.

    Teeth bared, she fumbles with the straps that encase her limbs and chest, growing ever more frantic until she is clawing her way out of the sweaty, bloody prison. As the Leather falls from her chest and shoulders she sighs with relief, shrugging her shoulders. 

    Cold and bright with the light of the moon, the chainmail jingles quietly,heavy upon her shoulders, and with a sigh of relief she allows her head to sag back. Beside her, Ehl Ahvrig shines like a star amid the gray plains, and as she bends to nibble at the soft green shoots beneath her hooves, a great tenderness wells up within Kara. 

    A small sob breaks from her throat as she strokes a hand down Ehl Ahvrig's soft hyde, and for a moment, she rests her forehead against the horse's shoulder. Hot thick tears roll down her cheeks, and her voice trembles as she whispers, "It is truth that I could live happily in the dirt with you to the end of my days."

    Sniffing, Kara wipes the wetness from her cheeks with the back of her knuckles, her heart full of love and grief as she gazes fondly at the horse.

    "Take care of Lena for me. Tell her to feed you many apples and carrots when winter passes."

    Kara turns her face first to the South, the dark swarm barely distinguishable against the night, then to the distant horizon where once rose the moon. And turning from it all with one lingering glance, Kara does what she had sworn never to do.

    She strikes Ehl Ahvrig hard on the rear, yelling, "Fly, to Argo!" 

    As she watches her last friend flee to the Northeast with a surprised whinny, a strange sense of calm settles over her. She lifts the horn to her lips once more, beckoning the herd forward until the soft crunch and rustle of grass twines once more with the odd rustle and pop of flesh and bone. 

    Once more Kara crosses to the bare edges of the grasslands, breathing deeply of the nauseous sky, the words of her father firmly in her mind as she surveys the plains alone. 

    And as death approaches, she thinks on her life- on the strangeness of it all. She wonders if this is what it is to have a purpose- if every moment of her life was meant to lead here.

    Falling to her knees, she pulls the gloves from her hands, searching the ground for a patch of dry grass suited to her needs. Her cleaver flashes as she severs a fistfull, wrapping it about into a loose little nest that she cradles carefully in her palm.

    Quickly she scrambles back to her discarded armor then- to the belt that lays beneath her pauldrons, and dropping her cleaver, beside them, she pulls the little bark tube from its leather pouch, drawing from it her last match.

    As she holds the little stick before her eyes, she thinks of Alex- of the way her sister had begged to come; of Alex's helpless rage when it was decided that Argo's doctors must remain behind the safety of Argo's walls. She thinks of the young boy whose life she had failed in the midnight streets of Orvai so long ago, and she thinks of Lena.

    Eyes sliding shut, Kara remembers the softness of her touch, of her voice; remembers too the look of betrayal as Kara had turned away from her.

    For this, she knows, Lena will never forgive her. But the crowd roams a mere bowshot away and she cannot dwell on the thought.

    With an easy flick of her wrist, Kara strikes the match against the rough bark, lighting it with a soft hisss. Hands shaking, she touches the flame to the center of the little nest, and when the bundle begins to glow with its own life, she rises; stands at the edge of the vast grassy plains, small against the glittering sky.

    The flame flickers between her hands like a tiny heartbeat, and carefully, reverently she bears it forwards, bending to place it at the feet of Jerat. 

    Awaken. 

    A small patch of brush warms with life, glowing gently at first only to leap and dance with abandon as the joy of living takes hold, and though Kara cannot truly hope to outrun a dragon she turns, sprinting away.

    The flames seem to catch on the air itself, racing South with a wild fervor as though encouraged by a high wind. A great whooooosh sucks every sound from the world, and in that silence, Kara knows a single moment of true peace before everything goes white.
