    A Dragon of Fire

    Chapter Summary

    Even the mountains once flowed like water.

    Chapter Notes

    Read The Updated Tags

    One league to the West a great pillar of fire and debris erupts into the sky with a violent orange flare, lending an eerie glow to the surrounding lands. For all its ire it fades in the fashion of dusk, and the sound of its passing is like thunder chasing an elusive bolt of lightning. 

    In its wake, a column of smoke larger than Argo herself rises, thick and blue in the silver night, and at the sight of it, all know that the dragon of Jerat has awoken. 

    Several stunned and silent moments pass in anticipation of a roar and cry that never comes, and in the persisting silence, murmurs of alarm begin to ripple through their meager numbers.

    Eyes wide, Lena watches as the night sky reappears, pale and filled with confused stars that wander sleepily to their thrones until all are established once more in glittering abundance. 

    A star winks, and a quiet rumble trembles beneath her feet, growing in volume and power until the very foundations of the land seem to cower.

    And the force of it…

    The force of it.

    Yet what follows is a silence so complete the world seems muted for a time- a silence wherein Lena strains to listen for any sound in the night- any semblance of music on the breeze.

    "Kara… Oh Kara, what have you done?"

    In answering, the bright moon casts her gaze upon the drifting smoke, lending it a lightness as it evanesces upon the breath of the sky. As if a sweeping current bleeds her of all strength, Lena's knees begin to tremble beneath the weight of her soul, and as the tattered army parts before her to trudge steadily Eastward, all she can do is stare. 

    The ghostly apparition of Sam takes Lena by the shoulder, wrapping an arm securely across her chest, and blinking down at the tether of her friend, Lena realizes she had been chasing the sun with no hope of catching it. 

    At the extinguishment of such hope, an all-consuming dread begins to drag her slowly, inexorably closer to the portal of Sam's ghostly world, and suddenly she is a new babe, unable to reconcile with the panic of this new existence.  

    Like all those new to this world, a terrible scream flies from her lungs only to find itself trapped within her skull, growing louder and louder until her eyes bulge with the strain of it.

    "Lena," Sam says, as if speaking from beneath the surface of the royal baths. "Lena, did you hear me?"

    And she tries to tell Sam that she did not, but that too remains trapped within her. In soft soothing tones that Lena recognizes as a star in a distant stable, Sam begins to speak into her ear, arms loosening about her shoulders.  

    "Lena, please- please, do not go. Would that I could go with you to search her out, but I cannot advise it- we must return with our wounded; we must prepare Argo should the dead have survived even that, and most of all we must rest. I swear to you, Lena. after, we shall search." 

    It is a duty Sam asks of her, and as Lena stands on the edge of who she once was- who she still could be, she knows that this is another confounded measure to be taken of her. A measure she must oblige or lose herself to. 

    The knowledge brings naught but frustration.

    The soft snow of ash begins to fall, raining gently down upon their hair and clinging to their lashes, and when Lena holds a hand to catch it, it is downy as the grey hyde of a goose.

    I will return for you. 

    Eyes sliding shut, Lena inhales a burning breath, and with more strength than lies within her heart she jerks her head in a tiny nod, allowing Sam to pull her gently onwards.

    When finally they rejoin their people, Winn glances at her with a misery to rival her own, and were it not for the twisted nature of their fates, she might have found it a comfort to share in such an understanding.

    The last four leagues of their journey are an agony- a monotony of meaningless steps that somehow make the difference, and as they trudge on, Argo's walls rise up against the horizon, blushing and gleaming with the rosy light of dawn. 

    Lena would rather be anywhere but trapped behind the claustrophobic stone walls as she walks beneath the towering gates, behind which livestock and their youthful tenders mill chaotically through the streets. Like a ghost Lena weaves through them, the toes of her boots dragging and tripping along the cobble with every step. 

    The hushed whispers and heavy stares of the elderly and children alike follow her doggedly through the streets, and as she passes by a courtyard full of nervously waddling geese, her eye catches on Zil Eshs.

    He prances warily back as she approaches, and she freezes- waits for the whites of his eyes to disappear once more before slowly taking a step back. The ansty quality of his bearing fades somewhat, and when finally he steps nearer, Lena can see the old dried blood that crusts his pelt.

    Numbly she begins to comb every inch of him for injury or bite, and finding none, the sudden whim of uncaring races through her. In a selfish act she cannot bring herself to regret, she places a hand upon his shoulder in a desperate request to mount. 

    With an indignant short Zil Eshs shies away, his ears flicking back, but Lena cannot bring herself to care. In a desperate plea, she asks once more, only for Zil Eshs to retreat to the far side of the courtyard, leaving her to stand amid the curious geese. 

    Teeth grit with frustration, she wheels about, searching madly for any alternative, only to see Sam in the entrance of the courtyard, watching with one eyebrow raised. "Are you leaving?" She asks in a gentle tone, beneath which lies a core of steel.

    "I must find her." Lena snaps, burying the surge of guilt that arises beneath a mountain of helpless rage. Refusing to meet Sam's eyes she begins to scan the streets for any horses, marching furiously towards the stables.

    "Lena, you must stop.” Sam orders in a quiet and firm voice. "I know you wish to find her. But now is not the time. The city is lost to chaos and grief, and we know not whether a dragon- a dragon may fly upon us. Our dead have yet to be mourned, our sick have yet to be tended, and we, you included, have just spent days traveling and fighting. You must rest and recover lest we risk losing you too."

    Biting back a hasty remark, Lena forces every stubborn thought into its rightful place before allowing a word to escape the prison of her teeth.

    “If Kara lives, then she wanders the plains of Jerat alone, pursued by a host potentially thousands strong. What if… What if she is injured? Sam, what if she is hurt, and needs help?" 

    Or worse…

    Lena cannot help the increasingly frantic note that rises in her voice, and suddenly the wound of every loss she has ever experienced comes rushing through her in a flood she cannot withstand. She does not remember falling, but when next she remembers herself she is on her knees in the dirt, with Sam's arms wrapped tightly about her shoulders. 

    "Kara has the audacity of a survivor." Sam says quietly. "I say this not to inspire false hope, but to say, perhaps there is no harm waiting to relinquish it a few moments more.”

    "She is the most foolish survivor I have ever known," Lena bites out bitterly. “So foolish and brave. I have never met one more willing to throw their life away, and of course it is she that I am doomed to love." 

    Shoulders shaking, Lena can barely speak through the choked feeling in her throat, silently mouthing the last words of her sentence.

    "I know… I know."

    Sam's voice is so soft, so understanding, Lena finally allows herself to lean into the safety of her arms. She does not know how long they sit there in the dirt; knows not either how long her face remains pressed into Sam's blood-soaked chest. 

    And she thinks of Alex then- Alex whose face matched the hue of her hair when Lena forbade her from accompanying their numbers; Alex, who will never forgive her. She thinks of James and she thinks of Winn, and she thinks of all those who have known the ruthless bite of loss. 

    For them, she takes a deep and steadying breath, wiping uselessly at her nose as she sits up, and dons the facade of strength.

    -

    Argo is like a held breath- one ever-nearing its exhale, as three days of scrambling and panic plateau into an unsettling calm. Outside the walls, the great funeral pyres remain stacked with wood and thatch- stoic obelisks that seek only to remind the living of their failed duty to the lost. 

    As the bloody dawn lights the Eastern sky, Urishesh draws her woolen coat tight about her face as she watches the young shepherds dart between their disgruntled herds of livestock, quieting them as best they can.

    Every morning brings a colder wind than the last, but she remains numb to it, gazing dutifully out across the lightening fields, feeling for all the world like a rabbit at the mouth of its den. All within Argo had taken to cowering beneath the nearest cover, darting from house to house, wondering obsessively whether doom would deal the hand of fire or hunger. She cannot claim to feel differently, but the restless terror within her finds slight relief in the work of watching over her people.

    When the sun begins its ascent into the lonely heavens, her eye catches on a slight, unnatural movement to the west- a movement that solidifies into a tiny, distant figure. 

    Gripping her longbow, Urishesh watches the progression of the figure as it nears until it resolves into a dark horse with an equally dark rider swaying drunkenly upon its back. She swears to herself, taking in a deep breath of the sharp air before bolting to the nearest tower, her footsteps echoing loudly against the stone stairs as she flies down them three at a time, and, arms waving wildly, she bursts into the street with a yell, hailing the guard at the gate. 

    One long hour passes as several eyes watch the progression of the lone figure- a man who sits slumped over upon the back of an equally unsteady horse, who stumbles and sways with every drag of its hoofs.

    “Halt!”

    The horse comes to a stop just before the gates, and though the feather of an arrow still tickles Urishesh's cheek, she breathes out a sigh of relief.

    “Be you a friend?”

    In response, the man leans far over the horse's neck- too far, toppling over to fall beneath the shadow of Argo. 

    -

    "Hail, Lena!" 

    The deep voice of Ather Hleam booms through the street, his bounding footsteps accompanied by the jingle and clink of his light armor. Weary and numb, Lena gives the saddlebags tied to Zil Eshs one last tug before wiping furiously at her wet cheeks.

    Ather Hleam is taller than most, and when he nears, his shadow completely engulfs her. Though he is known to her as an incredibly awkward person, there is a weathered edge to him, hidden behind his lanky frame and quiet tone; there is a grief to him too, that scrawls its sorrow permanently across his brow.

    "Hail," Lena replies hoarsely, and with an overly pained expression Ather Hleam stammers out, "Forgive me for disturbing you- I know you must be impatient to depart, but I felt important that you know- not twenty minutes past, a strange man approached the gates. He collapsed before them, and lies now in the infirmary.”

    Impatience, being an emotion Lena rarely feels, does indeed rear its brash head as yet another obstacle places itself between her and Jerat. She longs to disregard it; longs to end the infernal ignorance that has plagued her every waking moment, and in one selfish moment she almost does. 

    "Odd,” Lena grits out noncommittally, eyes passing once more over the lattice of straps and gear that adorns Zil Eshs's flank.

    “Indeed.”

    Fighting the urge to fidget beneath his expectant gaze, Lena finally turns to regard him with a huff. 

    “Thank you for bringing news of this to me. Should he be a friend, we surely now have the room and food to spare.”

    The sentence hangs between them, heavy as a swinging corpse, and with one solemn nod, Ather Hleam looks down at his feet. 

    “Of course.”

    Wincing with regret, Lena scolds herself with the reminder that she is not the only one to have lost dear ones to the madness of this world, and in a softer tone she asks, “How fare you?"

    "Not well," he mutters darkly, “but ‘tis a cursed truth we all share in.” 

    Lena nods miserably, and a dark part of her wonders if he too is about to condemn her for her failure, but he just clears his throat before continuing, “My mother stirs a hearty stew this eve. Should you go to her before you depart, you will find hot food and warm drink awaiting you.”

    “Thank you, Ather Hleam,” Lena whispers, her voice growing tight in her throat. “Your family has been good to me, and I do not take your kindness for granted.” 

    When he glances up from the ground, lips twisting in something Lena cannot quite call a smile, he holds out his forearm to her. With a thick sniffle Lena clasps it, and it is steady and solid beneath her hand.

    "Be well,” he says, “be strong. 

    “Be well.”

    And without another word he lets go, leaving Lena alone with Zil Eshs once more. 

    Before she can move to mount him, a youth leads a brown horse into the stables by a rope, leading it to a trough before tying the end of the tether through an iron ring. She pats the beast, and with the quiet promise of food, she turns to leave.

    With a soft snort the beast begins chuffing great gulps of water, and starting with surprise, Lena whirls around as Zil Eshs lets out a great whinny, suddenly light on his hooves as he prances over to nudge at the horse. 

    He runs his velvety nose over every inch of its hyde, and as she watches, a small pit of dread opens within Lena's soul. The clap of thunder seems to ring through her when she steps nearer, only to realize that the horse is coated in a thick layer of cracked mud.

    She knows instantly what color lies beneath it.

    “Wait!” She shouts, bolting after the young girl, and her voice is too loud in her own ears as the girl spins quickly about.

    “Yes?”

    “That horse- who does it belong to?”

    “A stranger, though I know not who. I was bid take her to the stables, so she might be allowed to rest.”

    Without another word Lena turns, sprinting headlong down the street, doors and walls blurring in her peripherals. It is five long minutes before she slows before the infirmary's wooden door, her boots slapping loudly against the ground.

    And suddenly she is afraid; suddenly she cannot bring her trembling fist to lift the handle for fear of what might lie on the other side. For fear that it might not be…

    The room, lit by a single lantern, smells of death and desperation, and in the dim light, Alex bends low over a figure, scrubbing vigorously at their back.  

    At her feet, a pile of muddy chainmail and singed fabric lies on the ground, and when Lena squints through the gloom, she gasps in horror.

    The stranger's entire back is red and blistered, from their neck to the backs of their thighs, and in several patches, their skin bears the shining marks of several tiny circular indentations.

    At first, Lena mistakes them for a thousand perfect little teeth marks, until her eyes fall once more to the ground.

    Even as Lena forces the terrible phrases of this riddle towards sense, they fight to remain in dissonance, refusing all reason until her eyes finally catch upon the dirty silver ring that glints proudly upon their right hand.

    She wants to cry; wants to scream with the intimate terror of the sight before her, but she just stands there frozen, fists trembling as she watches in helpless agony, the way Alex's hands swipe at Kara's back, sweat dripping from her brow.

    Lena can almost feel the fear behind every action, and that more than anything brings nausea to her gut. Every instinct bids her to rush forward- to gather Kara up in her arms and to hide her away from the rest of the world; from the pain of living and fighting and healing.

    Instead, she stumbles back, nearly falling through the door, allowing it to squeal shut over the macabre scene. She pants, bent over with shock, and heaving a shuddering sob she clutches the fabric over her knees, gazing without focus as the street spins beneath her feet. 

    Gasping, she tilts her face to the skies as though she might glimpse the laughing gods, and a curse upon everything flies to her tongue, only to die out as gratitude forces another silent cry from her. 

    Is she not behind that very door? And have I not watched lovers and families torn apart without any offering of grace?

    She sits now- sits until she cannot feel her legs, and as she listens, the frantic activity within the infirmary begins to dwindle into hushed whispers. Beside her, the door beside swings open, and when she turns to look she finds Winn's kind eyes gazing sadly down at her. 

    "I was just sitting with James," he says, his voice wavering slightly. 

    And suddenly, terribly, the world stills around Lena. The need to know takes brutal hold of her, and unable to remain in the purgatory of ignorance for a moment more, she springs to her feet. 

    With a gentle hand upon her shoulder, Winn murmurs, “Kara is safe. No bites on that one, and should she have the strength to weather this storm, she will survive.”

    The iron handle is cool beneath Lena's hand even as the tears are hot upon her cheeks, and like the crumbling of a cliff, her shoulders and head sag back with sudden exhaustion.

    “Sit with me a minute more, my friend. Alex will take good care of her, and it would do you well to prepare yourself before entering.”

    It feels suddenly impossible, just being on the other side of the door, but she knows Winn offers his advice for her sake. With a shuddering breath she nods, turning to sit on the ground once more beside Winn.

    “I shall never forgive her, even if she survives this.”

    “You will,” he smiles, nudging her gently with his shoulder. “And knowing Kara, I hold very few doubts that she will best even this.”

    -

    The deep shadows of Alex's face are sharp and ghoulish as she watches over Kara's sleeping form. Kara, who slumbers dreamlessly upon a small cot, bound with strips of stained bandages.

    "So long as the wound does not fester, she will live," Alex says in the hollow voice of relief, and even then- even sleepless and devoid of words and unwavering hope, the spark of elation ignites within her heart. 

    Her vision blurs with tears as she brings a hand to Alex's cheek, whispering a trembled “Thank you,” into the darkness between them. 

    “No need.”

    “I know, but- thank you.”

    Alex holds her gaze for one sorrowful moment before dipping her head, and with an air of exhaustion she leans against the nearest wall, murmuring to Lena and the guard, “I sleep in the home of Querl Dox and Nia Nal tonight- the door to nearest our left. Should any require attention, wake me, and I will come.”

    With a nod, Lena offers her thanks once more, and finally, blessedly she allows herself to gaze at Kara without qualm or fear. Like a mouse creeping up to a cat she crouches, shuffling her way to the edge of the cot.

    Kara’s face looks troubled, even in sleep, her noble brow furrowed in that familiar expression of worry and consternation, as though she were debating the most stubborn of mules. With the tip of one finger, Lena touches the pinched line of Kara's forehead, cooing softly into her ear, "I am here, love. By your side. Rest easy, for I promise, I shall watch over you until you wake."

    As if to acknowledge her words, Kara's face smooths into a more peaceful cast, and as Lena settles down on the rough wooden planks of the floor, Winn passes by, dropping a thin wool blanket into her lap with a gentle pat to her shoulder.

    Gazing after him, Lena's eyes fall upon James who slumbers on, his distinctive profile cutting against the light, and she watches as Winn settles down beside him in a mirror of her own position.

    Like a shepherd leading his flock through the long night, Kagur- a youth of distant Redhaven stands guard, gripping a tall spear in his untested hand. Beneath his watchful gaze, Lena allows herself to study Kara's sleeping face, and her unevenly singed hair, now carefully cleaned of mud.

    She counts the rise and fall of Kara's back until she cannot remember what number she had reached, only to begin again, and by these rhythms she measures the passing of the quiet night.

    When the dawn’s first rays fall upon Kara's face, it is enough to make Lena's soul cry out with bliss and agony, and sniffling back fresh tears she whispers, "Can you truly not know the power you hold over me, even now?”

    Her only response is the soft breeze that flees Kara's nose, only to fill her lungs once more.

    "I will never let you go, Kara," Lena whispers, wiping at her wet nose.

    "Please, do not make me. I love you.”

    "I love you, I love you, I love you."

    -

    One fearful day passes wherein all eyes flick nervously to the skies at the passing of every bird and insect; wherein unhappy cattle are herded into emptied houses, and a cold bites at all. None dare to light their fires, suffocating beneath the air of impending doom. 

    To some the surety of an end is a persistent terror, and to others, it is a comfort. But for all foreboding; for all surety of near and inevitable doom, the night passes silently by, taking only the whisper of Eve's untethered soul.

    James, having finally regained some semblance of strength, had sat up in the low light of dawn to find Eve feverish and shaking beside him, and when he had pulled the bandage from the stump of her shoulder, the sight of her discolored flesh had silenced his worried fussing instantly.

    Lena has never seen James truly cry- has never seen his serious strength waver, nor heard a sob from his throat until this moment. When Eve finally stills, every blade in the room raises in fearful salute. 

    Eve lunges at James so quickly, Winn misses her head, his dagger plunging into the fabric of her cot, and with a speed born of desperation he reaches out a hand, grabbing uselessly at her necrotic shoulder. 

    She slips easily from his grasp, falling heavily upon James, and, arms outstretched, he catches her like a father to his babe, turning to lay her down upon the floor.

    He holds himself there; holds Eve's shoulders to the ground, eyes squeezed shut even as she gazes accusingly into his. Winn dives like a hawk, and when warmth begins to soak into the fabric at James’ knees, he falls back into Winn's arms, grabbing at them with a cry that raises the hairs on Lena's scalp.

    That night, Lena dreams of Kara, damned or dead; she dreams of a great fiery beast stalking the land, and she dreams of the red glow of Argo. When she jerks awake, a cold sweat lies thick upon her limbs even as relief floods her at the sight of Kara's peaceful face. 

    The light of the third morning, when it comes, is soft and sweet, reaching through the small window to bathe Kara in golden light. Lena blinks the grit from her eyes only to close them against the tears that leak from their corners. As she dwells on Eve- refuses to acknowledge the red stain still fresh upon the floor, her lip begins to tremble-

    "Why do you cry, love?" 

    Lena's eyes snap open at the sound of Kara's rough voice, and she almost gasps at the striking brilliance of blue that greets her. Every rhythm of her body seems to stutter at the sight, and with a yell of elation Lena jumps up, waving to Alex. 

    The weary doctor offers a wide-eyed nod before turning to continue her work of tending, and immediately Lena drops down upon her knees to cradle Kara's face between her hands.
     
    Kara, who tries to lift her head only to still with a pained whimper as the flesh at the back of her neck stretches beneath the bandages. Brows knit with worry, Lena coos softly, smoothing her fingers over Kara's forehead and cheeks. 

    "Be still darling, you have been badly injured."

    Face tight with effort, the tension slowly bleeds from Kara's frame until she goes limp against the cot once more, resting her cheek against the rough fabric with a groan of pain. 

    The thud of Alex's boots are loud in Lena's ears, and with a hurried kiss to Kara's temple, she whispers, "I must go, so that Alex may tend to you properly. I shall return for you in the evening, I swear it.”

    "I love you," Kara mumbles, so softly Lena almost misses it, and suddenly, turning away is an impossible task. She allows herself one last aching look, and when the door to the infirmary shuts behind her, she stumbles from its shadow to face the open skies.

    -

    Alex crouches over Kara with an appraising eye, tssking as she wipes clean the remnants of an old poultice- a terrible ordeal that Kara faces by simply fading from consciousness. When next she wakes the sky is dark with twilight, and her mind alight with pain. 

    Every inch of her is hot and raw and seems to throb beneath the cool poultice of herbs that cakes her back. She wrinkles her nose at the foul smell of it, focusing instead on the single beam of moonlight that drifts like smoke through the window. 

    It seeks out a ribbon of hair as dark as the night, lending it a secretive luster that Kara longs to reach for. Instead she lies still, contenting herself with the indulgent act of observation. 

    Lena a scant foot before her, curled up into a tiny ball and wrapped tightly in a thin blanket. She is so near Kara could touch her- and Kara nearly braves the pain, only to cower in the face of it. 

    Sbe watches the rise and fall of Lena's sides, and the steady cadence begins to lul Kara once more into the haven of dreamless slumbering. 

    Just as her eyes begin to close, a deep sigh whooshes from Lena's nose, and wakeful with hope Kara whispers, “Are you awake?”

    With a soft groan, Lena's head lifts stiffly from the awkward angle it was held at, her eyes glinting in the darkness as she rolls onto her side.

    “Kara?” 

    “Yes.”

    Lena inches nearer, her breath bathing Kara's cheek in warmth as she whispers, “Oh, my brave heart, you're awake.”

    Kara can feel the trembling of Lena's lips soft against her cheek as she murmurs, “My foolish, brave heart."

    "Hey now, foolish?”

    "Hmm. Very well then, just brave.” Kara's eyes slide shut once more as Lena's fingers play through the short ends of her hair. 

    “Why did you do it?"

    Eyebrows raised, Kara says in the voice of truths iron, "Because I would do anything for you."

    Lena watches her for a time, and when her reply comes it is more troubled and serious than Kara has ever heard her. 

    "I ask for nothing from you but that you stay with me… We shall discuss your actions at a later time, else I may forgive you too easily. 

    Nay, what I mean to ask is why wake the dragon? Is not a dragon a more fearsome adversary than the dead?”

    "By tenfold. And yet, not at all.”

    “I know not what you mean, darling.”

    “What do you know of the dragons?”

    “I know them to be great winged beasts of myth, whose only fondness is found in gold. Armored with scales and the breath of fire, they would wreak senseless havoc on crop and cattle alike.”

    “Ahh, I thought as much. When I was young, my father told me of the dragons of the storms and of the winds; of the mountains and the sea. Of the dragon that once lay beneath Jerat.

    When I fell into the care of Eliza and Jeremiah those tales faded from my mind, dethroned by new dragons- the dragons of greed who oft find themselves bound by purposeful spite.

    Only now do I understand what my father spoke of.”

    A wide yawn splits Kara's jaws, and though she tries to hold herself still, her flesh betrays her. Gritting her teeth, she draws in a slow, trembling breath, holding herself almost rigidly still until the burning pain subsides to a dull smolder. 

    “All around us there roam ancient dragons.” Kara continues in a rough and hollow whisper, and she can almost feel the thrill that runs through Lena at her words. 

    “All are fearsome in their own right, and when fully in their power, they are unyielding and undiscerning in their natural dominance. 

    When we assembled at the plains, I knew instantly that there were far too many of the dead to lead away… I feared losing even a third of them. 

    So I put my trust in the dragon of fire- humbled myself before him, and to him I offered a flame.

    I had bid Ehl Ahvrig flee to Argo, but as I ran to the North with no true hope of escape, like an angel she came to run beside me. She bore us swiftly forth, sparing me the brunt of his fury, though ever more we shall bear his mark.

    You need fear him no longer. To the sky he has been freed, and should you meet with him again, he will likely bear the guise of the tempest."

    “I think I understand…” Lena murmurs, softly, and her breath tickles Kara's ear like the passing of a sprite. “And what of the dead? Need we fear their numbers?”

    Frowning, Kara tries to remember the chaos of the fields- tries to make sense of the charred limbs that had fallen like brimstone, but all she can recall is a searing pain and a terrible numbness.

    She remembers most the banks of Striped River- the way Ehl Ahvrig had shied away from the mud she had smoothed across the horse's singed flank, until the horse had rolled gingerly through it. She remembers the relief of the cool waters that had flowed between the links of chain to soak into her tunic, and she remembers the way Ehl Ahvrig had knelt wearily before her, allowing her to sling a leg over the horse's muddy back before the pain of it took her. 

    She remembers an agony of motion, and the desperate need to be… Somewhere. To find someone. To ride into the sun and to allow it to end her agony.

    “Though I could not place their surviving numbers, most who stood before the dragon fell to his fire. Enough that Argo will remain a sanctuary.”

    Kara can almost feel the relief in the sigh Lena exhales, and for a moment, every pain and aching of her flesh finds its purpose. 

    “Thank you, Kara.”

    Lena falters then, and Kara knows she cannot help the curiosity of her nature when she asks, “What was it like? Meeting the dragon.” And with the quality of those who have seen the faces of angels, Kara whispers back, "It was like standing beneath a waterfall."

    Chapter End Notes

    We've almost made it... It's bittersweet, wrapping up this story, because it has seen me through an incredibly transformative period of my life.

    Thank you for joining me on this journey, and for sharing this little universe with me.

    Next: The Epilogue.

    Epilogue: The Garden

    Chapter Summary

    i carry your heart with me (i carry it in
    my heart) i am never without it (anywhere
    i go you go, my dear; and whatever is done
    by only me is your doing, my darling)
    i fear
    no fate (for you are my fate, my sweet) i want
    no world (for beautiful you are my world, my true)
    and it's you are whatever a moon has always meant
    and whatever a sun will always sing is you

    here is the deepest secret nobody knows
    (here is the root of the root and the bud of the bud
    and the sky of the sky of a tree called life; which grows
    higher than soul can hope or mind can hide)
    and this is the wonder that's keeping the stars apart

    i carry your heart (i carry it in my heart)

    -e.e. cummings

    Chapter Notes

    A warm breeze stirs the lush grasses, rippling through the series of odd hills and valleys that pockmark the land. At the dousing of the sun, thousands of nightly insects strike up a choir of longing beneath the moon's soft light- a song that beckons forth sweet memories of long summers spent beneath the stars. 

    Such peace as this, Kara has not truly felt for five long years, and as she gazes out across the silver fields, the heavy shroud of darkness that mars her soul shifts slightly, like the tendrils of a noxious smoke probing for cracks in a stone roof. 

    As the purging of all poisons tend to be, it is an uncomfortable, sickly sensation- one she bears willingly, for she knows that to allow such a darkness to linger would be to spend the rest of her life with one foot in the grave.

    Ehl Ahvrig snorts quietly as she grazes on the tender grasses of the Plains of Jerat, and as Kara runs her own hands through the green shoots, a dull and persistent ache grows steadily within her chest. 

    How strange this life, she thinks to herself, sliding her fingers beneath the collar of her tunic where her flesh plays a mirror to the land. 

    The end of the world seems so common, and yet…

    For the first time, she bears lonely witness to little white wildflowers, glowing like clusters of dancing pearls amid the fields. Those once cursed to walk beyond the limits of their flesh have now found an accord with the land- one that every creature knows in time; this land, who welcomes every sin and bone, bidding all to find rest and sameness.

    Turning her face to the heavens, Kara calls upon the phantoms of memory; they spill through the sky like golden dust as she recounts deed and story; name and face, and suddenly she feels like the tiniest drop, lost amid a vast ocean. 

    No more does she dread the wounds of loss, whose touch has known everything; changed everything, leaving behind only the precious rite of bearing stories long past. So many stories that her shoulders sag beneath the weight of their responsibility, and yet no higher honor could she claim as her own. 

    “I swear to you.”

    The land rings with her solemn words as every memory turns its face to her- the kind eyes of her father; the too-young face of Eve; the lost nations who walk beside her only in spirit.

    “I swear, I shall tell of you- tell of your stories; bear them well and true until comes my day to join you. 

    I will not forget you.”

    The stars offer no response, blinking silently down at her. When she draws in her next trembling breath, the air is sweet in her lungs- a redemption that flows through her like the last sigh of life, coaxing the shadow from her heart to dissipate until all that remains is the residue of bittersweetness.

    It settles in Kara's bones, and without fight she allows grief to shed its visage of pain, exalting in the refinement of its true form. Love fills voids she had once reserved within her heart like seats of honor, and relief floods her as she reaffirms once more that such voids do not disappear without a trace; that love may yet live on past the illusion of endings, and that those places left empty by the sea may yet know their fill in rain. 

    Deftly she reaches behind her, pulling a small rolled up paper from the little bark tube at her belt and loosing the thin bit of cordage that encircles it. The moonlight, bright as it is, barely illuminates the strip, and when her eyes finally focus she half expects to see her words, once so carefully chosen, warped into the musings of a stranger. 

    They remain etched in sweeping lines she would not have recognized five years past, and with a nod she confirms her hand has not betrayed her. She rolls the narrow scroll once more, securing it in the safety of its wooden shell, and with one last look behind her she walks into the endless plains of Jerat, Ehl Ahvrig following close behind.

    -

    The day of the lost comes and goes with the lighting of ceremonial pyres and the recitation of names- a day wherein all raise a tankard to honor the fallen, and throw as much of a feast as the season will allow.

    Lena remembers it in drunk and disjointed flashes: crying as she whispers the names “Lillian” and “Lex” to herself; Sam stumbling drunkenly up to her, glowing with mirth; laughing loud and long at the story Querl tells of the time a large calf had run away with Erish clinging to its back. 

    She remembers songs, too- the kind learned in early childhood, now ringing forth from the throats of her friends and family; remembers humming along even as the words are lost to her. More than anything she remembers wishing that Kara would, somehow, appear at her side- to dance and sing and to eat of her fill. 

    She understands, though, the need behind Kara's quest, so she grudgingly resigns herself to the loneliness of the days that await her. At the end of the night when the door swings shut behind her, the darkness and quiet of their house remains stark and unsettling, even after the passing of twenty days. 

    Echoes of the warmth and life that usually fills their space seem all but a tease, and when she stands before the little bed she shares with Kara, she feels like crying. 

    She had longed for a time when neither she nor Kara would wake screaming in the night only to hold and shush the other to calm, and more often than not she has found herself sitting tucked beneath Kara's chin as together they watch the fading of twilight into morning. 

    She wishes now for the dawn. Always, it brings with it the love and the strength to resist the call of eternal night. With it, the world of dreams sheds its mask of terror to reveal a calm and fruitful land, though tragedy remains a simple fixture of life- one that seems to have relinquished only the severity of its bite upon the world, as those who once invoked such nameless terror upon the land had finally, blessedly succumbed to the endless bite themselves, leaving only a sickness to safeguard against.

    All carry the knife upon their person in wariness of such an event, and when Lena unbuckles her own, placing it gently on the small wooden stool beside the bed, she drops onto the stiff blankets with a weary sigh. 

    The pommel of her dagger glints sharply in the moonlight, smooth and burnished by the ceaseless visitation of her anxious hand. 

    Though she no longer holds the responsibility of the city upon her meager shoulders, deferring instead to the guidance of Argo's appointed counsel, she remains terribly on edge, often wearing a small depression in the floor when faced with any stillness.

    Long had she stood guard over the city, restless and alert, and in the afternoons she has to herself she plays tutor to the children and youths of Argo. Those days are the best and the worst, for her mind never quiets, ceaselessly preparing for the day when disaster will inevitably intrude upon the bounds of their tentative peace.

    One evening when she had returned home, her mind a raging storm, Kara had asked her in a quiet voice if she was happy.

    For a moment, she could not say.

    She sleeps with her love in her arms, and with that she could not ask for more; the work she does holds meaning- so much meaning it threatens to crush her.

    In the end she had nodded, but at her hesitation Kara remained quiet and thoughtful for several days, disappearing on occasion until one evening she had taken Lena by the hand and walked her up through the inner ring, up the hill, and still breathless, into the darkened halls of her wretched past.

    Her heart had raced as she followed Kara up, up, and up into the highest tower of the castle, wherein the low sun had bathed the tiny room in rosy light.

    By the window stood a simple padded bench, upon which lay stacked a small tower of books. Gesturing grandly towards the little scene, Kara had led her to sit by the window, and when she had carefully, almost nervously chosen a book, Kara had pulled one from the stack herself, moving to sit on the stone floor, her head resting against Lena's knee.

    So Lena had read, and read until the sun doused itself in the Western sea and Kara's soft snores were loud in the quiet. Since that day, the little pocket of perfection had become her refuge, and little by little she had found the thunder of her evenings washed clean with the clarity of long-forgotten reflection. 

    Eventually she had found herself climbing the hill alone with increased frequency, searching for a place to lay down her weary soul for a time- a ritual that softens the days ‘til Kara's return.

    Still, they drag on, each longer than the last, and as Lena closes her eyes to hasten the day's end, she awaits the dawn.

    -

    Twenty hot days and twenty soothing nights come to pass when finally, blessedly the tang of salt lies heavy on the breeze. Kara's shoulders sag with relief as she nears the far borders of the plains, feet weary and face red beneath the wrath of the sun.

    At first, crossing the plains had proved a treacherous task, for the ground beneath her feet was terribly uneven, unstable and littered with the half-buried remnants of broken and blackened bone. Thrice Kara had bid Ehl Ahvrig return for fear of a broken leg, and thrice the mare had studiously ignored her, picking a careful path across the plains until at last the land had smoothed like the calming of a lake.

    Never before has she crossed the entirety of Jerat and the Western grasslands, having spent most of her girlhood tucked safely within the bordering forests and only rarely passing through the shortest swathes of the fields to witness the mad race of spawning fish as they traveled up Striped River. Still, she remembers standing amid them- the way nothing but her father had stood taller than she. 

    Nothing for leagues. 

    She remembers the vast sky swallowing her whole into a blue no gem nor painted scene could rival, broken only by the clouds that had raced and towered and flown in streaming ribbons across the sky- the way they had burned with the light of lightning, and the setting sun. She remembers every hue laid out in the slow shifting gradient that yields only to the stars, and at the abrupt end of the waving grasses, she remembers another shade of blue.

    That of the Western sea.

    It glitters before her now, patient and calm, and though it hushes her sweetly, she remembers the gentle crash and tumble of it; the soft hand upon her face that would simultaneously batter great stone walls into handfuls of pebbles, and whisk trees away with a force she had never been able to fathom. 

    Until now. 

    Eagerly Kara kicks off her boots, tucking her socks neatly within before trudging into the sun warmed sand. Her toes drag little trenches behind her as she stumbles forth, and even against the gentle turf they ache terribly. 

    The cry of the sea birds are sweet in her ears as she reaches the borders of the creeping waves, and there she falls to her knees, trusting the land to catch her. Head bowed, she remains as the ocean washes up to meet her, sun-warmed water soaking through the fabric at her knees as a thick foam pushes past her, clinging to her shins and calves as the ocean returns to itself. She cracks one eye open to see Ehl Ahvrig approaching the waves, eyeing them curiously as she tentatively follows them back to their source only to buck and leap into the air, landing with a great splash as the waves rise up beneath her.

    Offering the water an overly offended side-eye Ehl Ahvrig trots back to Kara, gently nudging her shoulder, and with a smile Kara runs a hand over the velvety soft hairs of the horse's nose, delighting in all that is hers to witness. 

    “We made it,” she sighs, and like the sea breeze, the warm breath of Ehl Ahvrig ruffles her hair. 

    “We made it.”

    -

    The journey back to Argo is kinder, for though the summer sun beats down upon Kara's face and neck without regard, they follow the banks of Striped River, its waters offering a sweet and cool reprieve. 

    It feels like only months since she laid herself down in its shallows, unable to free herself of the prison of her armor even as the water soothed her burned flesh, and though three years have done little to fade the scars that dig their crescent nails into her back, she has become almost fond of the marks. 

    Some late nights as she lays in bed, Lena will trace the grooves and ridges with gentle fingers, laying soft kisses over every inch of healed skin until finally she drifts off.

    Argo lies a mere two leagues to the East, and after so many days and nights spent roaming the open plains she feels almost reluctant to entrap herself once more within Argo's stone walls, surrounded by the noise within. Still, her heart aches with longing as she thinks of Lena, and when her mind turns to Alex and the many friends she has made, she finds herself eager to return.

    It is late in the afternoon by the time Argo comes into sight, and well outside the walls she pats Ehl Ahvrig's weary shoulder, watching the horse's silver mane wave like the banner of the heavens. A high pitched whinny sounds from behind them, and breaking out into a grin of delight, Kara whoops as a youthful mare gallops up, her midnight pelt broken only by a small white star upon her forehead. 

    Ehl Ahvrig tosses her head, snorting with delight, and with the playfulness known to foals, the two horses take off across the field, leaving Kara to stand before the glowing city.

    Shaking her head with a fond smile she continues down the dusty road alone, passing by the fields and low barns that hug Argo's Western walls. In one such barn she spies Nia and Querl perched atop an empty cart, Nia gesturing animatedly as Querl gazes at her with rapt attention. 

    Two doleful looking oxen low with impatience as his fingers dance along the rigging of their heavy yoke, and when it falls from their necks, they remain in place for several long moments before meandering slowly into the low grasses.

    “Hail!” Kara calls, and she winces as the oxen stumble quickly away at the sound of her voice even as Nia's hand shoots up, beckoning her close.

    “Kara! How went your journey?”

    “Ahh, ‘twas long, but well.”

    “You have been sorely missed by all,” Nia says, wrapping Kara in a tight hug. “You look…. Different,” she says when she pulls back, eyes narrowing slightly.

    With a quick dip of his head, Querl offers his arm to Kara. “Well met. Aye, different, but not lacking. Taller, perhaps.”

    “I feel different,” Kara says with a rueful smile. “Perhaps it is simply the heat of the sun that has warped my image.”

    “Aye, red as a tomato you are, though no less handsome,” Nia says, nudging Querl with her elbow. He nods in quick agreement, and with an awkward laugh, Kara feels her face grow impossibly warmer. Before she can respond, Querl vaults into the back of the cart exclaiming, “Ah! I have something for you.”

    He returns clutching a folded piece of cloth, placing it gently into Kara's outstretched hands. Within lie several long, striped feathers, each capped with a brilliant band of orange.

    “Two days past we crossed the trail of several wild turkeys. We returned with two.”

    “Thank you! I had meant to search some out for quite some time. You have saved me days of hunting!” Kara exclaims, marveling at their coloration as she runs her fingers carefully along their soft edges. Tucking them carefully within her tunic, she smiles as Nia begins to speak with the same bright enthusiasm, informing Kara of any and all tidings of the past forty days, and together, the three of them enter into the city's gates.

    The first thing that greets them is a virtual swarm of children, chirping and yelling with delight as they surround Kara, tugging at her hands and asking where she has gone, and whether she will join them in their games.

    Grinning foolishly she ruffles their hair, hugging each in turn, and heart suddenly light she shakes her head with promises of “Soon”. 

    “They were almost insufferable in your absence,” Nia says with mock weariness, watching after them as they run off, content to resume their game. “Even Lena found herself outmatched.”

    “A greater humbling I have never known, than those at the hands of a child,” Kara says, even as she grins at the familiar image of Lena attempting to wrangle the unruly crowd.

    “Kara!”

    Head whipping to the side, Kara jumps as her name booms loudly throughout the streets, only to break out in a beaming smile as Alex runs towards her, arms outstretched.

    “Gods above I missed you!”

    “OOF” is all Kara can reply, the wind knocked clean from her lungs even as she throws her arms about Alex feeling light as air. Out of the corner of her eye Nia and Querl wave to her, turning down a street, and with an all-too-late wave, she turns her gaze back to Alex. 

    “I missed you so,” she wheezes out before drawing in a great breath, straightening with a lopsided grin. “How fare you and yours?”

    “Ahh, we are well. The days have not passed without their hardships, but for the most part, things have been peaceful. Ruby grows ever taller, and by the winter I wager she will stand as tall as Sam.”

    Happiness blooms within the garden of Kara's heart at the mention of her new niece. She remembers when Ruby, a girl of seven winters had arrived at Argo just three winters past, having been found in the ruins of Kandor, malnourished and half out of her mind. She remembers too the way Alex and Sam had fret ceaselessly over the girl, tending to her fragile body until she was strong and full of life once more.

    “I am almost certain Sam will throw a fit, when comes the day.”

    “Aye,” Alex sighs fondly, “and Ruby shall celebrate endlessly.”

    Running a hand through her auburn hair, Alex's cheeks puff out with a weary sigh. “You know some days… Most days, I wonder if I am truly doing right by her. She has been through far more than I could ever have fathomed at her age, and I- I know not what to say. I fear I have not the necessary knowledge, nor the patience needed to rear a child, let alone in a world such as this. 

    Honestly, she reminds me of you in a lot of ways…”

    Kara looks down at the scuffed toes of her leather boots before glancing back up to meet her sister's eye. 

    “We are beings of finite grace. Patience must know its limits, for all are possessed of a certain threshold. What determines the quality of your character is how you then conduct yourself during and after such states of gracelessness. To my eyes you yet retain your honor, in this regard.”

    “Aye, you are right. But… But what if it is still not enough?”

    “Ruby has been through hell, and through it somehow, she has found herself in the care of kind people who will love her- who will guide her through the turmoil of living. That she is not alone; that she is not bereft of love is one of the most important things you could have offered her.”

    Squinting up at the sky, Alex puffs out her cheeks with a deep exhale. “Thank you. I try to remind myself of that, but sometimes it just gets… lost.”

    “I know what you mean,” Kara says, nudging Alex with her shoulder. “If ever you feel your patience wearing thin, know you are not alone. We shall help you take care of her. 

    And you.”

    Alex’s lips quirk up into a little half-smile, and by unspoken consent, the two of them turn down the main road.

    “I am glad to be here with you,” she murmurs.

    “And I, with you.”

    Together they walk up to the door of Kara's home, only to find the small room quiet and still. A pang of disappointment shoots through her when she can find no recent signs of Lena save for a neatly folded pile of clothes on the corner of their bed, and with a grunt of exhaustion she pulls out one of the small benches tucked beneath the table, falling wearily onto it.

    “What of Urishesh? How fares her apprenticeship?” She asks as Alex plops down onto the bench opposite her.

    “Still sharp as a knife, that one. She takes quickly to the work, and without complaint. Quite a somber and determined young woman. I think she will make a fine doctor.”

    “Well done then!” Kara exclaims, a bright smile stretching her cheeks. “I am proud of the both of you. You do much for all of us, and I doubt not that the knowledge you pass on shall change the fates of many.”

    Looking down bashfully, Alex waves Kara's words away, muttering “It's nothing,” before rapping her knuckles against the table.“Ahh! Tomorrow, Sam makes macrows. Would you and Lena join us for supper?” 

    Kara's mouth waters at the thought of food, and she finds herself nodding before Alex is even done speaking. She is surprised to find just how much she missed sharing a meal with her family, and with a twinkle in her eye she replies, “Not even a dragon could keep me from your table.”

    “Now that, I believe. 

    Would that I could stay longer, but I must attend Ather Hleam's boy. He recovers from a high fever.” 

    Kara's heart drops at the mention of the young boy, so inquisitive and curious, and though worriedly she searches Alex's face, her sister remains calm, gaze clear and steady. 

    Kara attempts to calm her anxious thoughts, focusing instead on the meal to come. “May his recovery be swift,” she murmurs, her stomach rumbling loudly, and with a lopsided grin Alex nods, reaching across the table to ruffle her hair. 

    “Aye, he is a strong young lad, I have no doubt in him. Right, tomorrow then,” Alex says, rising from the bench with a great heave.

    “Tomorrow!”

    “Oh,” Alex throws over her shoulder. “Lena is likely within the castle.”

    The iron latch of the door clicks as it shuts behind her, and running her hands down her face, Kara groans.

    I walked to the sea and back, surely I can make it up one last hill.

    She flexes her feet in their worn boots, and though they practically beg for rest, she shakes her head, tossing the small parcel of feathers onto the table before rising with a grimace of determination to follow Alex through the door.

    As she passes beneath the wall of the inner ring, those few she walks by offer her a nod- a nearly formal dip of the head, and though she has had three years to accustom herself to this oddity, she can never shake the awkward feeling that comes along with it.

    She knows what it is they say about her- whispers that the dragon's spirit leapt into her, like a storm taking refuge in autumn's last leaf. Few had truly listened to her protests, and she had soon resigned herself to their little ritual. 

    As Kara passes by the armory, her ear is caught by the sounds of conversation, and continuing onwards she soon comes upon a low shaded terrace, whereunder she finds Winn and James sitting on either side of a small table. Between them lies a game of checkers over which Winn hunches, studying carefully the layout of the scattered pieces. 

    As he waits, James takes a bite of boiled sweet fig only to splutter as Winn jumps three of his pieces in quick succession with a look of barely contained delight. 

    James just stares down at the board with disbelief, gnashing his teeth before claiming indignantly, “Bahh, you distracted me, you did!”

    Winn's booming laughter echoes between the buildings, and he leans across the table, murmuring something to James with a particularly cheeky expression. The old knight blushes, waving him off with a fond scoff, and as Winn sits back with a tiny smile, he catches Kara's eye. 

    He dips his head in greeting, and following his line of sight, James raises a hand, raising his gentle voice.

    “Welcome back.” 

    “Many thanks!” Kara replies with a grin and a wave, as the two return to their game, James folding his thick forearms across his chest as he glares down at the little wooden pieces. She trots on at a brisk pace, only to slow as the edge of the houses yield to the base of the grassy hill.

    Tiny wildflowers dot the green slope, waving sweetly about her calves and shins as they burn with the slow ascent. Mind fixed on thoughts of Lena, she trudges onwards, wondering how the days might have changed her love, short as they have been, and eager to find out she quickens her pace.

    -

    The evening sun reaches through the solitary window of the castle's highest tower, bathing the little chamber in the light of golden memory. A book hangs idly from Lena's fingers as she tracks the dust that floats from sunlight into shadow like tiny stars winking out. 

    Though she has no true hopes of catching sight of Kara from so far away, the thought brings her comfort, and so, she falls to daydreams of a white horse standing tall against the horizon. She almost misses the gentle tapping that tugs at the edges of her consciousness, only for the air to sharpen with immediate, bone chilling fear as the sound resolves into the muted scuff of boots upon stone. Her hand flies to the dagger at her hip before she can stop herself, and her heart leaps in alarm as a very red, very familiar face peeks around the corner.

    “Kara!”

    Without hesitation Lena bolts upright, running forth to throw her arms about Kara, showering feather light kisses upon her scarlet cheeks, her nose, and her chapped lips. She is so real, so solid in Lena's arms, and just as tall and as noble as memory paints her. 

    Lena's cheeks hurt for the way she smiles, and when she leans back to look at Kara, she feels like crying. The sun catches in the ends of Kara's hair, and breathlessly Lena runs her fingers through the harmless flames, marveling at such a wonder.

    When she meets the blue of Kara's eyes they are strikingly clear, shrouded by neither mist nor shadow, and the familiar light held once more within them is enough to lift any darkness from her own.

    “Oh, darling, darling, I missed you so,” she murmurs, burying her face into Kara's neck. 

    “And I you, my love.”

    Kara's arms tighten around her like a warm bath, and basking in it Lena whispers, “How fare you?”

    “My soul has found its peace once more,” Kara replies, and her tone carries with it the striking quality of a lit candle, piercing the long-held dark. “The journey was difficult, but beautiful. Nature has reclaimed even scars such as those left upon Jerat.”

    “I am glad you went,” Lena says, smiling up at Kara, “though I have withered in your absence.” Her shoulders rise and fall with a dramatic sigh. “Still, all is not lost, for I find the remedy held between my own two hands.”

    With that, Lena cups Kara's warm cheeks as gently as she can, and with a shaking exhale Kara presses a kiss to her forehead, murmuring, “Wither never more my love, for at any distance, you shall always have me.”

    “I know,” Lena whispers, and as she gazes up and down Kara's frame she feels the weight of the world lift from her shoulders. Kara stands with a long-lost ease and a lightness to her bearing, and for the first time since Lena has known her, she is like a cloudless sky. 

    With an odd little smile, Kara takes her hands tugging her forth to sit once more on the bench, and there she kneels before Lena, laying a gentle kiss to the backs of her knuckles before pulling her hand away. 

    In Lena's palm there rests a tiny scroll of paper tied with a thin length of cordage, and when she looks up she finds Kara gazing at her with a gravity and an absolute surety that causes her heart to race. 

    Swallowing thickly, Lena's eyes flick back down to the scroll, and pinching the small tie between her fingers, she pulls until it falls away leaving the paper to unfurl slightly from its tightly wound state.

    It rustles quietly as she rolls it open with careful fingers, her gaze passing fondly over the shapes of Kara's bold and slightly uneven script, and she smiles for a just moment before her mouth falls open.

    The sun rose in the East as it has every morning. It wavers not in that regard and oft I wonder if it has any choice in the matter. Still it remains stalwart and true when very little may claim the same. Not you nor I nor even grey death have been left undone by the forces of change and though I have envied the sun it is to these forces I find myself forever owed.

    Should the dawn light a fire in the West I will recognize it as my own soul touched by the hand of you. I have known no loss not tempered by loves gain nor any gruesome sight not tempered by beauty. Everything before you I have loved and everything after have I loved differently for it sings of you always.

    vo rraop zrhueiao im zhgehv, khup gev nahni

    -Kara

    Silently Lena traces those final words, the tips of her fingers dwelling on the lines of Kara's name. Her mind, for the first time today, is devoid of distraction and anxiety, and she blinks in surprise as Kara's gentle hand brushes the tears from her cheeks. 

    "Khuhp gev nahni, Lena.” Kara says, her voice soft and stark in the quiet.

    “Khuhp nahni, Kara.”

    A smile, slow and sweet, lights Kara's face like the joyful sun, and able to bear being apart no longer, Lena places the note carefully beside her before taking Kara's face between her trembling hands. She leans down, the curtain of her hair falling about their faces, sun drenched and honey brown, and there in the harbor of that privacy she kisses Kara as tenderly as ever she has kissed her.

    As Kara holds her close, she finds the world has shuffled itself into some beautiful semblance of order once more, owed only to the merciful whims of chaos- brutal and yet so sweet; forever changed and yet still changing. In this moment of quiet she finds a surety within her heart- the truth of all truths: that so long as there remains a love to fight for, there exists a life to live for.

    The end.

     

    Chapter End Notes

    Here we are at the end of our tale, as bittersweet as any ending might be. Thank you to all who have come with me on this adventure, inspiring me to continue far beyond my own motivation.

    Life has changed in every regard since I began writing it half a year ago, and this story has served as an important reflection on many lessons learned.

    Until next time, I wish you all freedom, peace, and growth.

    Notes:

    - This work was meant to be 6 chapters :')

    -vo rraop zrhueiao im zhgehv, khuhp gev nahni = to you, the loveliness of this world, let me be yours/I am yours. (Said like a wishful prayer)

    -Khup nahni = I am yours.
